New src/services/import/: types.ts [IMPORT_SOURCES (the 8), IMPORT_PAYLOAD_KINDS (the 5 that read a JSON payload), ImportEnvelope {app:"divergence-ai",version,kind,payload}, ImportedPayload discriminated union, never-throw ImportOutcome union mirroring translate()/detectState()/runDebate()], chatHistory.ts [STRUCTURAL parsing of externally-exported logs, deliberately NOT per-vendor — CANON names no vendor and a named-format zoo would be fabricated spec, same "do not invent behavior for it" posture as LEFT NAVIGATION's Integrations; accepts a bare array / {messages} / {conversation}, role aliases (human/ai/model/bot), string OR content-block-array content, number-or-ISO timestamps with synthetic order-preserving fallback; a "system" turn is SKIPPED not relabelled, unreadable entries are skipped+counted not fatal], envelope.ts [per-kind validation of untrusted input: ids always REGENERATED (a file's id could collide with stored data and silently overwrite it), context bytes always RECOMPUTED from actual content (a file claiming bytes:0 on a huge string would otherwise walk straight past CANON's 10MB/50MB caps), imported technique stacks CAPPED at MAX_TECHNIQUE_STACK (the same class of bug Step 5.2 already caught once, arriving through a different door), unknown model→"auto"/bad directness→2, every guard REUSED not rewritten (isValidVariableName 7.4, isTechniqueId 4.1, isModelId 1.10, MAX_FILE_BYTES 7.1)]. STORE ADD: sessionStore.loadSessionRecord(record) — the action Step 9.1's PARKED note predicted verbatim ("a new sessionStore action ... calling hydrate() with the record's fields"); sets the six fields a SessionRecord carries, CLEARS draftInput+statePills (a record stores neither; carrying the current session's forward would strand a half-typed thought and stale pills above a conversation they don't belong to), leaves currentScreen untouched (navigation is orthogonal). New src/components/session/ImportModal.tsx — TWO-LEVEL, not a flat list of eight, because CANON's ADHD hard rule is explicit ("Never more than 5 to 7 simultaneous choices per view") and eight buttons breaks it outright; the split is semantic (outside content: file/URL/chat history = 3; saved app data: previous conversation/variables/context snapshot/saved prompts/template settings = 5), both inside the limit, group heading stays visible so nothing must be remembered mid-flow. REUSES rather than rebuilds: file→Step 7.1/7.2's uploadFiles() with the same accept list and shared OCR client, URL→Step 7.3's fetchUrlContext(), both of which already enforce the byte caps, so Import cannot become a side door around limits the rest of the app obeys; modal shell is Step 8.5's DownloadModal pattern unchanged; every store write goes through an EXISTING action (Import adds no new way to mutate state). Wired into QuickActionsRow's "More ▾" popover — Step 9.1 deliberately left Import unplaced ("not a Quick Actions button at all ... Step 9.3's own separate step"), and the overflow is the only slot available without adding a button the V3 screenshot doesn't have. Also consolidated the OCR client accessor: the tesseract WORKER was already shared app-wide inside ocr.ts, but AttachContextControls kept its own module-level lazy-init of the client; added getSharedOcrClient() to ocr.ts and routed BOTH consumers through it, removing the duplicate rather than adding a second convention alongside it. New src/styles/import.css (imported in main.tsx), mirroring export.css's modal shell. Each "technique-weight" proposal nudges that technique's weight by ±1 (increase/decrease), clamped to [MIN_TECHNIQUE_WEIGHT, MAX_TECHNIQUE_WEIGHT] = [-5, 5] so repeated learning-loop runs over an account's lifetime can't drift a technique's standing unboundedly — Step 10.1 already dampens WITHIN one analysis run (MIN_SAMPLE_SIZE/CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD); this bounds drift ACROSS many runs, a different failure mode. Last completed: STEP 5.3 — Error, retry, timeout (done. New src/services/pipeline/resilience.ts: TimeoutError, isTransientError (network TypeError/timeout/429/5xx retryable; other 4xx and unrecognized errors not), withTimeout (per-attempt abort via a merged AbortSignal), withResilience (exponential-backoff retry, real cancellation via deps.signal never retried), makeResilientClient (wraps a PipelineModelClient's complete/stream with both — stream retries ONLY before the first chunk is yielded, never after, so a mid-generation failure can't duplicate output the user already saw). orchestrator.ts: runPipeline now wraps deps.client internally with makeResilientClient (callers pass the raw client; resilience is guaranteed by construction, not left to the caller to remember) and wraps EVERY stage's call in its own try/catch yielding a terminal {kind:"error"} event on any exception — runPipeline itself now never throws, extending translate()'s Step 2.2 guarantee to the whole pipeline. New PipelineDeps.resilience?: ResilienceOptions for tuning/tests (production uses the defaults: 3 attempts, 300ms backoff doubling, 30s per-attempt timeout). No UI changes needed — the existing stage indicator (visible from time zero of any wait) already satisfies "indicator for any wait over 1 second," and the existing neutral fixed-copy error rendering (TranslationCard/StreamingAnswer, Steps 2.3/5.1) already satisfies "neutral non-blaming copy on failure." both sides run in PARALLEL via Promise.all and fail INDEPENDENTLY per ROUTING.md's "a partner API being down must not break the whole debate turn — fail that side gracefully with a visible retry, not a crash"; typed outcome union complete|partial|failed|empty-question, where a DebateTranscript exists ONLY on complete]. New src/components/multiAi/: DebateView [THE STEP'S NAMED OUTPUT — the two-column dual-stream view, each column independently showing its argument or its own error+retry, collapsing to one column on narrow viewports since two ~40-char columns would break CANON's "no dense info walls" harder than stacking], PartnerPicker [the four roster options with their role descriptions, not four bare vendor names — Claude deliberately absent so the user cannot construct the Claude-vs-Claude debate Feature 9 forbids], MultiAiActions [the screenshot's composer-footer control, slotted into the EXACT .composer__footer-row wrapper Step 8.2 created for it — no restructuring needed]. Last completed: STEP 8.3 — Debate mode (2-to-4-way debates; resolved doc conflict, extended architecture) (done. DECISION RESOLVED: debate mode supports 2-to-4-way debates (Claude + 1 to 3 partners), not fixed 2-way as originally spec'd. CANON.md Feature 9 and ROUTING.md DEBATE PARTNERS updated to reflect: user chooses 1-3 partners manually OR auto-select button picks best-fit partners based on question domain/complexity. Two selection modes: (a) Manual — multi-select checkboxes 1-3 partners from roster; (b) Auto-select — scoring heuristic matches roster strengths (agentic→GPT, abstract→Gemini, current-events→Grok, complex→DeepSeek) against question signals, defaults to 1 best-fit partner, expands to 2-3 on high complexity/scope signals. New src/services/debate/autoSelect.ts: deterministic scoring heuristic (not routing.js complexity scorer, simpler for this use case, documented as such per ROUTING.md guidance). Extended runDebate.ts to accept partnerIds array (1-3) instead of single partnerId, runs all participants in parallel via Promise.all, independent failure per ROUTING.md. DebateSide gained optional partnerId field, DebateOutcome union now uses sides array instead of claude/partner fields, builds transcript from Claude + first partner for Step 8.4 backward-compat. Updated PartnerPicker component: multi-select checkboxes (1-3 max) with disabled state when limit reached, selection count display. New AutoSelectButton component triggers auto-select scoring on current question. Updated DebateView: renders N columns (2-4) dynamically from sides array instead of fixed 2-column layout. Updated MultiAiActions: new selection UI with auto-select button + manual toggle, PartnerPicker shown only in manual mode, auto-select called fresh on every question, retry logic now works with side indices. Updated all runDebate tests to new partnerIds array signature and sides array output. Last completed: STEP 11.2 — Performance pass (done. SPOT CHECK confirmed CANON's two timing rules (line 105): sub-300ms for every visual interaction, an indicator for any wait over 1 second, no countdowns/forced timeouts. Audited the whole interaction surface against both (static audit — the app makes no live LLM/network calls in this no-network sandbox, so the classification/pipeline waits themselves can't be browser-measured here; parked for the deploy-time live pass, same standing limitation as every prior step). FINDINGS: the app is almost entirely plain synchronous React re-renders (dropdowns, popovers, accordions, visibility toggles, session/template/quick-tool actions, pill dismiss/correct, ratings, single-message export render, synchronous autoSelectPartners) — all trivially sub-300ms, no CSS transitions to wait on anywhere (grep: the only @keyframes is the decorative logo pulse, a sensory-rule item not a timing one). Every async wait already had a visible indicator EXCEPT one: pipeline run → StageIndicator + streaming ✓, OCR upload → "Reading files…" ✓, URL fetch → "Fetching…" ✓, import (files/JSON) → "Reading…" ✓, debate/consensus/synthesis → in-button "Debating…/Finding consensus…/Synthesizing…" ✓, 5s autosave → correctly silent background (not a user-initiated wait). THE ONE MISS: State Detection (Step 6.3) fires on every submit as a network classification that routinely exceeds 1s, but CenterColumn cleared the pill panel to null on submit and the panel is gated `{detection && …}`, so it blanked and silently reappeared with no indicator during the wait — a literal 1-second-rule miss. FIX (applied): CenterColumn now tracks a `detecting` flag (set true when detectState fires, cleared in its .then — which always runs since detectState never throws; guarded so a superseded/aborted run doesn't clear the newer run's flag), passed to Composer, which renders a quiet inline `role="status"` "Reading your message…" line in the panel's own slot while `detecting && !detection`. New `.state-detection-panel__detecting` style reuses the exact attach/URL/import status idiom — static metadata text, NO animation, so it satisfies the 1-second rule without violating the sensory "no competing stimuli / no excessive auto-animation" rule. No component render test added (repo convention: zero .test.tsx, no testing-library in STACK.md — components are typecheck+build verified only; not introducing RTL in an audit step). Clean tsc -b, clean vite build, clean oxlint (only the pre-existing unrelated QuickActionsRow exhaustive-deps warning), full suite 532 passing — no regression.)

Last completed: STEP 11.3 — Keyboard audit (done. SPOT CHECK confirmed CANON's Accessibility rule (line 115): 100% usable by Tab, Enter, Escape; plus the step's own typing-isolation and destructive-safety requirements. Static code audit (same no-live-browser posture as 11.2 — Playwright is explicitly Step 12.2's own tooling decision, STACK.md doesn't mention it, and introducing it here would preempt that step; the repo-wide "typecheck+build verified only, not render-tested" convention stands). KEYBOARD COMPLIANCE CHECKLIST: (1) Tab-reachability — grepped all 86 onClick call sites across every component; every single one is a real `<button>`/GlassButton/BlueMarbleButton/Pill(onClick) or native `<input>`, zero div/span click-traps found — PASS. (2) Focus rings — one global `:focus-visible` rule (keyboard.css) covers every native focusable, zero `outline:none` anywhere, one legitimate `tabIndex={-1}` (a hidden file input triggered by its own reachable button) — PASS. (3) Modals (DownloadModal, ImportModal) — both use useFocusTrap(true) + useDismissableLayer(true, onClose); focusTrap.ts correctly wraps Tab, returns focus to the trigger on release, covered by 14 happy-dom unit tests — PASS. (4) The 8 popovers/dropdowns (Attach, StateDetectionPanel, QuickToolsGrid tiles, SavedPromptsMenu, LoadTemplateMenu, QuickActionsRow's More, VisibilityMenu, TechniqueDropdown) all use useDismissableLayer (Escape + outside-click); only TechniqueDropdown additionally focus-traps (its own established Step 4.5 precedent) — the other 7 rely on natural DOM tab order, which still satisfies "operable by Tab/Enter/Escape" (focus-trapping every menu would be a retrofit of a deliberate, already-logged convention, not a rule violation) — PASS. (5) Accordion (AccordionStack) — collapsed panel content is conditionally unmounted (`{isOpen && <div>...}`), not just CSS-hidden, so it's correctly excluded from tab order when closed — PASS. (6) Pills (StatePill + PillCorrector) — dismiss/correct are separate real buttons (never nested), PillCorrector's options are real `role="menuitemradio"` buttons in natural DOM order (its own DECISIONS-logged no-trap choice, unchanged) — PASS. (7) Checkbox/radio groups (DownloadModal content+format, PartnerPicker, VisibilityMenu, TechniqueDropdown's manual list) all use native `<input type="checkbox"|"radio">` with proper `<fieldset>/<legend>`/`name` grouping where relevant — PASS. (8) Typing-isolation — `isTypingTarget` correctly classifies textarea/select/contentEditable/text-type-inputs as typing targets and checkbox/radio/button/range/color/file as not; zero `useGlobalShortcut` call sites exist anywhere in the app, so "typing never triggers a global shortcut" holds with nothing yet registered to test against — PASS (vacuous, logged as a real finding, not assumed). (9) Destructive-action safety — grepped every `useDismissableLayer` call site (10 of them): every `onDismiss` is a pure UI-close (`setOpen(false)`/`closePopover`/`closeMore`/`onClose`), none wire to a store-mutating action, so Escape can never destroy anything; the one genuinely destructive action in the whole app (Session Discard) is gated by `useConfirmable`'s two-step arm/confirm (a single stray Enter/Space/click cannot fire it); zero `onKeyDown` handlers exist anywhere in components, so no custom key interception could bypass any of this — PASS. (10) Hover-only reveals — grepped for `display:none`/`opacity:0` gated only by `:hover` with no keyboard equivalent; found none (the one `display:none` is the same legitimate hidden-file-input pattern as (2)) — PASS. THE ONE GAP FOUND AND FIXED: ImportModal's URL-import view (`view === "url"`) used a plain `<div>` instead of a `<form>`, so pressing Enter in the URL text field did nothing — inconsistent with the byte-identical URL-entry pattern in AttachContextControls (Step 7.3), which submits on Enter via `<form onSubmit>`. Not a hard-rule violation on its own (Tab to the Fetch button and pressing Enter/Space there still worked, so the flow was never unreachable), but a real, fixable inconsistency directly in this audit's scope. FIX: wrapped the URL row in `<form onSubmit>` (preventDefault + handleUrlFetch), changed the Fetch button to `type="submit"` — the sibling "Back" button already had an explicit `type="button"`, confirmed before the change so it wouldn't accidentally start submitting the new form. Clean tsc -b, clean vite build, clean oxlint (only the pre-existing unrelated QuickActionsRow exhaustive-deps warning), full suite 532 passing — no regression, no new render test (same repo-wide no-RTL convention as every prior UI step).)

Last completed: STEP 12.2 — End to end flows, run ahead of 12.1 per the operator's explicit instruction for this run (focus on Feature 9 — Debate/Consensus/Synthesis — plus the core flow) (done. SPOT CHECK confirmed the core flow named by the step's own text — type, TRANSLATE & ASK, pills, streamed answer, rate, autosave restore — matches CANON.md (Feature 5 pills, Feature 7 rating, "STORES AND PERSISTENCE" 5s autosave). @playwright/test added as a REAL devDependency at last (^1.61.1, npm registry access confirmed working) — playwright.config.ts points executablePath at this environment's pre-installed Chromium (/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194) rather than running `playwright install`, per this environment's own instruction. New tsconfig.e2e.json (mirrors tsconfig.app.json's bundler-mode settings so e2e/*.spec.ts can import src/ modules the same way components do) referenced from the root tsconfig.json — `tsc -b` now typechecks e2e/ too. METHOD: every model call the app would make (translate, detect, execute, debate, consensus, synthesis, and the debate partner's own proxy-openai call) is intercepted at the network boundary via Playwright's page.route() (e2e/mocks.ts) — nothing talks to a real model, since none is reachable in this sandbox (proxy live-deploy parked for 12.3, unchanged). Requests are routed to the right canned reply by SYSTEM PROMPT, imported directly from the real source files (TRANSLATION_SYSTEM_PROMPT, DETECTION_SYSTEM_PROMPT, debateSystemPrompt(), CONSENSUS_SYSTEM_PROMPT, SYNTHESIS_SYSTEM_PROMPT) rather than re-typed strings, so a prompt change elsewhere can't silently desync the mock; the one call with no fixed system prompt (the final streamed answer, since it can run on whichever model routing.js picked) is resolved by the `stream: true` flag, which only that call ever sets. Each mocked call has an artificial 250ms delay — a zero-latency mock resolves the entire five-stage pipeline within one tick, which hides exactly the transient UI (stage indicator, TranslationCard, partial streaming text) this suite exists to check; real latency is what makes those states observable. TWO FILES: e2e/core-flow.spec.ts (2 tests: the full core flow incl. reload-restore of conversation+rating; a second test confirming an unsubmitted draftInput alone survives reload) and e2e/multi-ai.spec.ts (2 tests: manual-selection debate through Consensus through Synthesis with real DebateView/ConsensusView/SynthesisView assertions; Consensus/Synthesis correctly disabled before a transcript exists). Manual partner selection (GPT-5.5 only) was used instead of Auto-select — deterministic, since autoSelectPartners' scoring heuristic is a variable not worth pinning a test to. REAL BUG FOUND: `npm run dev` does not load the app at all — src/services/routing.js is a UMD module Vite's dev server can't interop the same way `vite build` does (see DECISIONS/PARKED and CONVENTIONS.md's now-updated Dev commands caveat) — the first work in this entire build to actually drive `vite dev` in a real browser; every prior browser session used `vite preview`. Worked around by pointing playwright.config.ts's webServer at `vite build && vite preview` instead — not fixed, since fixing routing.js/routingService.ts is a src/ change outside this step's "build tests, don't refactor" boundary; escalated, not silently patched. Last completed: Operator-directed follow-up — fix the two CRITICAL VISUAL-AUDIT items (V8, V14) blocking V3 parity, per the operator's explicit priority call after the Step 11.5 severity report (done. REQUIRED FILES read: MATERIALS.md, MARBLE-CONTRACT.md, Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png — all already in the repo. Located the real files (the operator's suggested paths — App.tsx, ComposerPanel.tsx — don't exist in this repo's actual structure): src/styles/marble.css (both `.marble-slab` and `.surface-blue-marble`), src/styles/tokens.css, src/components/composer/TranslateAskButton.tsx -> BlueMarbleButton.tsx -> `.surface-blue-marble`. V14 ROOT CAUSE: `.marble-slab` painted the raw texture with zero darkening — MATERIALS.md's own hex range (#0B0C0E-#151618) is already correct in tokens.css, so the deviation was entirely in how the texture asset renders, not the base color. V8 ROOT CAUSE, confirmed with a real capture before touching anything: `.surface-blue-marble` blended the sapphire gradient OVER the texture with `background-blend-mode: color` — hue/saturation from the gradient, LUMINANCE entirely from the texture at whatever viewport-fixed point the button happened to sample, so brightness was fully unpredictable (a real screenshot showed near-black at one position, sharp bright cyan patches at another, in the same session). FIXES: V14 — a flat black scrim layered over the texture in `.marble-slab` (new `--slab-darken-opacity` token). V8 — reordered `.surface-blue-marble`'s layers so the gradient is the BASE (consistent luminance always shows) and the texture blends on top with `soft-light` instead of `color` (texture nudges the base, never overrides it — matches MATERIALS.md's own "texture visible at close inspection" on a reliable base, not a texture-dominated one), plus a calibrated darkening scrim (new `--button-darken-opacity` token, since the un-crushed gradient read more vivid than V3) and a cyan hairline border (new `--accent-cyan-tint-25` token — MATERIALS.md's spacing section specifies hairlines are "darker charcoal or cyan," not white, caught and corrected mid-session after an initial white-border attempt). CALIBRATION METHOD, since neither exact opacity value exists in MATERIALS.md or as a literal in the V3 PNG: built and served the app (`vite build` + `vite preview`, this sandbox's own pre-installed Chromium via the same Playwright setup Step 12.1/12.2 established), captured real screenshots at 1525px, 900px, and mid-scroll, cropped matching regions from Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png itself (loaded via a file:// URL in the same browser) for direct side-by-side comparison, and iterated the opacity tokens (--slab-darken-opacity: 0.55 -> 0.75; --button-darken-opacity: 0.35) until the renders matched — exactly the "use the V3 screenshot as your visual target" instruction, not a guess. Did NOT stop to ask for a hex/value, since the operator's own instructions explicitly authorized deriving it this way. MATERIALS.md NOT updated — its existing prose ("near-black," "barely noticeable," "not neon," "texture visible at close inspection") was already correct; the CODE didn't match the unchanged SPEC, not the other way around. VISUAL-AUDIT.md updated in place: V8 and V14 flipped from [ESCALATED] to [FIXED] with the full root-cause/fix/verification account. ## DECISIONS
- Locked decisions verified: three-marble system (not seven), claude-sonnet-5 for Balanced tier, state detection on button press (not live-as-you-type), routing engine already built (not rebuilt).
- Routing engine string change identified: routing.js needs claude-sonnet-5 update in MODELS table (Step 2.2 applies this).
- Texture files location clarified as needed before Step 3.1 (three files: one black marble, two greys).
- Translation test corpus (31_0_translation_test_cases.md) marked as optional for Step 2.4; not blocking current progress.
- STACK LOCKED (Step 1.1): React 19 + TypeScript + Vite 8 SPA; Zustand for the two CANON stores; IndexedDB via `idb` (CANON mandates IndexedDB by name); vanilla CSS custom properties for the marble/glass system (Tailwind and CSS-in-JS rejected); Tauri for Windows wrap, Capacitor for Android. Svelte 5 and SolidJS seriously considered, rejected because 58 remaining blind sessions make ecosystem predictability worth more than marginal reactivity gains. Full reasoning in STACK.md.
- Frame implemented as CSS grid (`grid-template-areas`), not nested flex: one declaration encodes all four locked dimensions (60/200/flex/300), harder for a later step to break one region in isolation. Regions carry data-testids: topbar, col-left, col-center, col-right.
- Layout dimensions live as CSS custom properties in src/styles/layout.css (--topbar-height, --left-col-width, --right-col-width); Step 1.2 tokens should extend this pattern in src/styles/tokens.css (created empty as a landing point).
- Vite 8 template ships oxlint (not eslint) — kept as-is rather than swapping.
- FONT (Step 1.2): Anthropic Sans (named in MATERIALS.md) is not publicly licensed and cannot ship. Substituted Inter (SIL OFL, freely shippable) as --font-family-base — closest clean humanist-geometric sans at the same register, and the de facto standard at the 12-14px sizes this whole type scale runs at. Only the stack is fixed here; loading/self-hosting the actual font file is Step 3.4's job, not this one.
- COLOR VALUES NOT GIVEN AS EXACT HEX (Step 1.2): MATERIALS.md/CANON.md give exact hex only for Black Marble, Blue Marble, cyan (#00D9FF), and purple (#8B5CF6). RSD, Interest, and Cognitive Mode pill colors are named only by hue ("red", "green", "blue"). Picked --state-rsd/--action-destructive #EF4444, --state-interest #22C55E from the screenshot by eye. --state-cognitive #3B9EFF deliberately kept distinct from Blue Marble's #2B4E9C-#4478E5 range so a state pill never visually reads as a button. --state-emotion aliases directly to --accent-purple (#8B5CF6) per MATERIALS.md's explicit statement they're the same purple. A later session with real pixel-sampling tools should verify these three against the screenshot and adjust the token values only (no component touches these directly).
- RADIUS (Step 1.2): MATERIALS.md gives cards a 12-16px range, buttons a flat 8px. --radius-button is 8px (unambiguous). --radius-card is fixed at 14px, the range's midpoint, as a single token a later step can retune without touching any component.
- TOKEN FILE ARCHITECTURE (Step 1.2): flat single-tier custom properties in tokens.css, no primitive/alias indirection layer. Simpler than a two-tier system and CONVENTIONS.md only asks for domain-prefixed semantic names, not a primitive layer — revisit only if token reuse patterns actually demand it later.
- SWATCH PAGE (Step 1.2): public/token-swatch.html, a static HTML file referencing /src/styles/tokens.css directly. Dev-only, not linked from AppShell or main.tsx, not part of the shipped bundle — exists purely so a session with a real browser can open it and eyeball every token at once against the screenshot.
- FILE-MANIFEST.md CORRECTION: an earlier version of this line claimed Step 0's manifest "was never written or saved anywhere in the project tree." That was wrong. The real Step 0 manifest genuinely existed — it just wasn't saved into this project folder at the time, so Step 1.3's REQUIRED FILES check couldn't find it and wrote its own stand-in under the mistaken belief that none had ever existed. The real Step 0 manifest has since surfaced and now lives at FILE-MANIFEST.md in the repo root — the authoritative Step 0 deliverable. Step 1.3's stand-in was renamed to FILE-MANIFEST-STEP1.3-ADDENDUM.md rather than deleted, because its texture-file findings (source filenames, the .jpg-extension-but-actually-PNG correction, the near-identical-textures flag, the seam-tiling measurement) were discovered during Step 1.3 itself, postdate the real Step 0 manifest, and are still accurate and still in use — see PARKED and MARBLE-CONTRACT.md.
- TEXTURE FILES DO NOT TILE SEAMLESSLY AS PROVIDED (Step 1.3): the three raw texture files are 1254x1254px. A prior investigation into these same three source files (MAX Effort - Marble Design Output / MARBLE-SLAB-ENFORCEMENT-CONTRACT.md — confirmed same files by matching 1254x1254 dimensions and matching descriptive names) measured that raw textures at this resolution do not tile seamlessly (wrap discontinuity 1.6x-2.9x baseline), which reads as a visible seam on background-repeat. That prior work already built the fix: seamless 2508x2508 mirrored supertiles (measured wrap discontinuity 0.00). Per explicit instruction, those pre-built supertiles were copied into this repo as `public/textures/black-marble-slab.jpg`, `grey-marble-slab-1.jpg`, `grey-marble-slab-2.jpg` and are what marble.css actually references as background-image. The three raw originals (`black-marble.png`, `grey-marble-1.png`, `grey-marble-2.png`, saved at Step 1.3) are kept only as canonical source of record per FILE-MANIFEST-STEP1.3-ADDENDUM.md — no component loads them. Full detail in MARBLE-CONTRACT.md and FILE-MANIFEST-STEP1.3-ADDENDUM.md (not the root FILE-MANIFEST.md, which is the real Step 0 deliverable and predates these textures being supplied).
- PRIOR-ART FOLDERS ARE UNVERIFIED UNTIL CONFIRMED (Step 1.3, explicit instruction for all future steps): the "Fable - MAX Effort - Marble Design Output" folder turned out to contain a directly relevant, verified prior investigation (used at Step 1.3, above), but that was confirmed with the person running this build before being used, not assumed. Any future step that finds other pre-existing output in that directory (or elsewhere outside the repo) must not use it on its own judgment — flag it and ask first, the same way Step 1.3 did.
- SLAB ARCHITECTURE (Step 1.3): one `.marble-slab` element (`position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 0`), mounted once in main.tsx as a sibling to `<AppShell />` (never nested inside it, so no ancestor can pick up a transform/filter/backdrop-filter that would silently break `position: fixed`). All app content wrapped in `.app-layer` (`position: relative; z-index: 1`) — currently just AppShell's root div. Technique ported directly from MARBLE-SLAB-ENFORCEMENT-CONTRACT.md's proven architecture (that prior work explicitly found `background-attachment: fixed` broken on iOS Safari and repaint-costly, using `position: fixed` on the slab instead — adopted here for the same reason).
- BLUE MARBLE BUTTON DEVIATES FROM PRIOR CONTRACT, DELIBERATELY (Step 1.3): the prior MARBLE-SLAB-ENFORCEMENT-CONTRACT.md banned marble texture on every component, no exceptions, and specifically forbade `background-attachment: fixed` anywhere. Current MATERIALS.md (CANON-authoritative, postdates that prior work) requires Blue Marble buttons to sample marble "from the same slab coordinates as the background" as a deliberate sole exception. `.surface-blue-marble` (marble.css) satisfies this using `background-attachment: fixed` on its marble layer ONLY (not on the slab itself, which still uses `position: fixed` per the prior contract's proven technique) — a narrower, bounded use case the prior contract never had to rule on, since its source design had no textured buttons at all. Chosen over per-button JavaScript position tuning, which the prior contract correctly identifies as fragile (drifts on any layout change). Full reasoning in MARBLE-CONTRACT.md "DEVIATION FROM PRIOR ART."
- GREY SUPERTILES SAVED BUT UNUSED (Step 1.3): `grey-marble-slab-1.jpg` and `grey-marble-slab-2.jpg` copied in alongside the black one for completeness, but no component references them — CANON's three-surface system (Black Marble, Smoked Glass, Blue Marble) has no defined use for a fourth "grey marble" surface. Reserved, not wired to anything, matching the prior contract's own treatment of the same asset ("ships unused so no future agent rebuilds it differently").
- BOTTOM SLAB BAND (Step 1.3): `.app-shell` height changed from `100vh` to `calc(100vh - var(--space-section-margin))` (24px) so a full-width strip of slab is visible below the three-column frame, per MATERIALS.md's slab-rule enforceable check. Grid rows/columns/areas and all locked dimensions (60/200/300) are unchanged — only the container's total height.
- TOKENS.CSS WAS NEVER ACTUALLY IMPORTED INTO THE APP (found and fixed at Step 1.3): Step 1.2 built tokens.css and linked it from the standalone swatch page, but main.tsx never imported it — harmless while AppShell was empty (Step 1.1), but marble.css's var() references would have silently resolved to nothing once real CSS started consuming them. Added `import "./styles/tokens.css"` to main.tsx, before layout.css and marble.css.
- LAYOUT.CSS HARDCODED VALUES CLEANED UP (Step 1.3): body's placeholder `background: #111; color: #eee; font-family: system-ui` and the three region borders' hardcoded `#333` replaced with token references (`--text-primary`, `--font-family-base`, `--surface-smoked-glass-border`) now that tokens.css is actually wired in. body's own background removed entirely — `.marble-slab` is a fixed full-viewport layer that fully covers it.
- SELF-CHECK METHOD (Step 1.3): per the step's own instruction, verification against MATERIALS.md's slab rule was done by a fresh-context Explore subagent given MATERIALS.md, MARBLE-CONTRACT.md, and the actual source files (not my reasoning about them) with instructions to statically audit for rule violations. All checks passed — see PARKED for what this does and does not cover (no real browser, so no literal resize/pixel test).
- PRIMITIVES REUSE STEP 1.3 MATERIAL, DO NOT RE-DECIDE IT (Step 1.4): GlassCard/GlassPanel use `.surface-smoked-glass` and BlueMarbleButton uses `.surface-blue-marble` exactly as built in Step 1.3 (marble.css) — unchanged. New CSS (primitives.css) only adds layout/sizing classes (`.primitive-glass-card`, `.primitive-glass-panel`, `.primitive-glass-button`) composed alongside the existing surface classes via multiple classNames, not by duplicating the material declarations.
- NEW `src/components/primitives/` FOLDER (Step 1.4): CONVENTIONS.md's repo-layout tree only shows `components/layout/` and `components/<feature>/` explicitly, no primitives category. Added anyway: primitives are cross-cutting (used by every future feature), analogous to `layout/` as a structural category rather than a single feature, and CONVENTIONS.md's own tree is illustrative ("one folder per CANON feature as it's built, e.g. ...") not exhaustive. Logged here per the same transparency principle as an unlisted dependency addition (CONVENTIONS.md rule 3) — flag it plainly rather than assume it's uncontroversial.
- GLASSBUTTON AND DROPDOWN GET A 44PX MINIMUM CLICK TARGET (Step 1.4): MATERIALS.md states 44px minimum only for Blue Marble buttons explicitly. Extended the same floor to GlassButton and Dropdown's `<select>` — CANON's ADHD hard rules ("Accessibility") apply generally, not just to the one surface MATERIALS.md happened to spell it out for.
- DROPDOWN IS A STYLED NATIVE `<select>` (Step 1.4): not a custom ARIA combobox/listbox. Gets keyboard operation (arrows, type-ahead, Enter, Escape) for free, satisfying "keyboard-focusable with a visible focus ring" without building custom ARIA at the primitive-library stage. A feature step needing a richer listbox (icons, multi-column, live-filtering) builds on top of this rather than this primitive being replaced.
- PILL IS GENERIC, NOT STATE-SPECIFIC (Step 1.4): `Pill` takes a caller-supplied `color` prop (a CSS color value, e.g. `var(--state-emotion)`), applied via a `--pill-color` custom property — it has no built-in knowledge of Emotion/RSD/Interest/Cognitive Mode. Step 6.3 (State pills UI) is what gives pills that meaning; this primitive only provides the shared visual shape. Renders as `<button>` (real tab stop) only when `onClick` is supplied, otherwise `<span>`, so decorative pills don't consume keyboard focus.
- FOCUS RING REUSES `--accent-cyan`, NO NEW TOKEN (Step 1.4): every primitive gets `outline: 2px solid var(--accent-cyan); outline-offset: 2px` on `:focus-visible` (primitives.css), satisfying the step's "visible focus ring" requirement and CANON's ADHD hard rule on the same. Reused the existing cyan accent token rather than adding a dedicated `--focus-ring-color` token — one fewer token for the same value, revisit only if a future step needs the focus color to diverge from cyan.
- AppShell.tsx's DEMO CONTENT REPLACED, NOT DUPLICATED (Step 1.4): Step 1.3's raw `surface-smoked-glass`/`surface-blue-marble` demo divs in AppShell.tsx are now built from the six real primitives instead. This doubles as the step's required "page rendering every primitive over the slab" deliverable — no separate showcase page was built, since AppShell already renders inside `.app-layer` over `.marble-slab`. Still explicitly demo content, not real feature UI; Step 1.5+ replaces it.
- TOPBAR AND LEFTNAV ARE REAL, NOT DEMO (Step 1.5): new `TopBar.tsx` and `LeftNav.tsx` in `components/layout/` replace all Step 1.3/1.4 demo content in AppShell.tsx entirely (no Pill/Dropdown/BlueMarbleButton demo usage left). Both reuse the Step 1.4 primitives (`GlassButton`) rather than inventing one-off nav-item styles.
- NO ICON LIBRARY YET; TEXT-LABEL PLACEHOLDERS (Step 1.5): the logo slot, Settings/Notifications/Help buttons, and all ten left-nav items render as plain text, not icons — STACK.md doesn't include an icon library and adding one is a stack decision outside this step's scope (CONVENTIONS.md rule 3: "Adding a dependency is a DECISIONS line"). Deferred to whichever step actually wires real icons (likely Step 1.6 for the logo specifically, and a polish/component step for the rest).
- CENTER COLUMN IS A BARE TEXT PLACEHOLDER, NOT A SCREENSHOT REPLICA (Step 1.5): the V3 screenshot shows a fully populated conversation (messages, input box, state pills, dropdowns, TRANSLATE & ASK). Step 1.5's own numbered scope explicitly says "Center column flex: empty conversation placeholder" — treated that as more specific than the general "match the screenshot exactly" instruction, since the populated conversation UI is explicitly built incrementally across Steps 5.0+ and 6.x. Center column here is one placeholder `<p>`, nothing else.
- RIGHT COLUMN IS TWO MINIMAL PLACEHOLDERS, NOT THE REAL GRID/ACCORDION (Step 1.5): built two `GlassPanel`s with placeholder text (Quick Tools; the six accordion section names in one sentence) rather than the actual 2x3 Quick Tools grid or six real accordion headers. Reasons: (1) Step 1.5's own scope says "placeholders," (2) CANON's locked decision "Quick Tools panel defaults to hidden (OFF)" has no meaningful toggle to be OFF from until Step 9.4 (Visibility toggle) exists — rendering a fully-built, always-visible grid now would contradict that decision. Steps 9.4/9.5/9.6 own the real versions.
- LOGOUT CONTROL ADDED DESPITE NOT BEING VISUALLY DISTINCT IN THE SCREENSHOT (Step 1.5): CANON.md's LOCKED DECISIONS explicitly states "logout button bottom-left under System Status," and Step 1.5's own numbered list names it, even though the V3 screenshot doesn't show separate visible logout text at that position. Followed the explicit written spec over an ambiguous screenshot read, per CANON.md's own precedence rule only applying when text and image actually disagree (here the image is just ambiguous/silent, not contradicting).
- NEW `--status-online` TOKEN (Step 1.5): `#22c55e`, same value as `--state-interest` — MATERIALS.md's palette only defines one green — but a separate named token, since System Status (health/connectivity) and Interest (a detected emotional-state dimension) are unrelated concepts that could diverge later even though they start identical.
- NEW `src/styles/shell.css` FILE (Step 1.5): structural layout for content *within* the topbar/left-nav regions (flex arrangement, gaps, the system-status row). Kept separate from `layout.css`, which CONVENTIONS.md scopes to "the structural frame ONLY (grid, dimensions)" — shell.css is the next layer in, parallel to how Step 1.3 got marble.css and Step 1.4 got primitives.css.
- LEFTNAV ITEMS ARE INERT (Step 1.5): the ten nav buttons and Trash/Logout have no click handlers, routing, or active-state styling (the screenshot shows "Home" highlighted as active, not replicated here). Real navigation/active-state behavior is explicitly Step 9.7's job ("Left nav content"); Step 1.5 only places structure and spacing correctly.
- LOGO AURORA COLORS AS LOGO-SCOPED TOKENS (Step 1.6): CANON names the aurora as "neon green, purple, pink blended" but gives exact hex for none of the three. Added `--logo-aurora-green` (#39ff14), `--logo-aurora-pink` (#ff4fd8), and `--logo-aurora-purple` (aliased to the existing `--accent-purple` #8b5cf6 so the "AI" text stays the app's one purple), plus a `--logo-aurora-gradient` composing them. Kept logo-scoped, not promoted to general accents — this three-color mix is the brand mark's identity, not a reusable UI accent. Green/pink hex are a confident pick from CANON's hue names; a session with the real brand source can retune the two token values without touching Logo.tsx.
- LOGO ANIMATION IS OPT-OUT, NOT ALWAYS-ON (Step 1.6): CANON's LOGO section calls for a "subtle synapse animation" while its sensory hard rule says "No excessive auto-animation." Reconciled by making the synapse pulse (slow 3.6s ease-in-out, opacity 0.4↔1, staggered per-node delays) run ONLY under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` — reduced-motion users get a fully static logo. This is the first `prefers-reduced-motion` guard in the codebase; later animated steps should follow the same pattern (Step 11.2/11.3 audits will expect it).
- LOGO IS HAND-BUILT SVG, NOT AN ASSET FILE OR ICON LIBRARY (Step 1.6): the brain is an inline SVG path in Logo.tsx with a gradient stroke and pulsing `<circle>` synapse nodes, not a raster/exported asset and not from an icon set. Consistent with the Step 1.5 note that no icon library exists in this build; the logo specifically needs custom animation so it's bespoke regardless. The Vite template's placeholder public/favicon.svg and icons.svg are still unreplaced (see PARKED) — this step built the top-bar mark, not the favicon.
- LOGO.CSS IS CO-LOCATED, NOT GLOBAL (Step 1.6): unlike shell.css/primitives.css/marble.css (global stylesheets imported from main.tsx), Logo.css sits next to Logo.tsx and is imported by it. CONVENTIONS.md explicitly permits `ComponentName.css` next to a component; used here because the logo's keyframes and SVG-gradient wiring are genuinely component-specific, not shared vocabulary. First component-scoped stylesheet in the repo.
- ZUSTAND INSTALLED (Step 1.7): executes STACK.md's Step 1.1 pre-decision ("Zustand for the two CANON stores"). zustand ^5.0.14, compatible with React 19. `idb` deliberately NOT installed yet — IndexedDB persistence is Step 1.8's job, and Step 1.7 builds only the in-memory stores + schemas.
- STORE FIELD TYPES: TOP-LEVEL AUTHORITATIVE, NESTED PROVISIONAL (Step 1.7): the two store shapes (SessionState, AccountState) and their top-level fields are fixed here. Nested domain types a later step owns in full are minimal/provisional and marked so in types.ts and STORE-CONTRACT.md: ContextItem (Steps 7.x), ConversationMessage (5.x/8.x), StatePills (6.x), ArchivedPair (9.x), LearnedPreferences (10.x/6.4). Those steps may ADD fields but must not rename/remove the top-level store fields without updating STORE-CONTRACT.md. Enum-ish unions (ModelId, DirectnessLevel, TechniqueId, PlanFlag, state-pill values) mirror names already fixed in CANON/ROUTING.
- FACTORY FUNCTIONS, NOT SHARED CONST DEFAULTS (Step 1.7): createInitialSessionState()/createInitialAccountState() return fresh objects each call, used for both store init and reset. Chosen over a shared `const initialState` so arrays/objects can't be aliased across sessions (a reset can't leak a prior session's mutated array). Verified by a specific smoke-test assertion.
- *_PERSISTED_KEYS AS THE AUTOSAVE SEAM (Step 1.7): each store exports SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS / ACCOUNT_PERSISTED_KEYS (the data field names, excluding actions) and a hydrate(partial) action. This is the seam Step 1.8's autosave layer uses to serialize/rehydrate without hardcoding field names or needing to distinguish action functions from data. Keeps 1.7 free of persistence code while giving 1.8 a clean contract.
- resetSession CLEARS SESSION STORE ONLY (Step 1.7): CANON says the session store is "cleared when a session closes"; the account store "persists across browser closes" and is never wiped by session close. So sessionStore has resetSession(); accountStore deliberately has NO reset-all action — only per-item mutations (removeSavedPrompt, removeVariable) remove individual entries.
- PLAN FLAG: FLAG NOT BILLING, DEFAULT FREE (Step 1.7): `plan: "free"|"paid"` lives in the account store, defaults "free" so routing.js's gated (free) path is exercised by default. Per ROUTING.md there is no payment/auth/account system — did NOT build billing, auth, or an upgrade flow. routing.js already accepts `plan` as input but isn't wired in yet (a later Phase-3 step); Step 1.7 only supplies the storage location.
- CONVENTIONS.md's "(Step 6.3)" STORE ANNOTATION IS STALE (Step 1.7): CONVENTIONS.md's repo-layout tree annotates the stores as built at "Step 6.3" — that's from the OLD plan numbering. The real plan (DIVERGENCE-BUILD-PROMPTS.md, the established authority for step ordering) builds them at Step 1.7. Followed the real plan; kept CONVENTIONS.md's file names (sessionStore.ts, accountStore.ts) and location (src/stores/), which are still correct. Also added src/stores/index.ts barrel and src/stores/types.ts (not individually enumerated in CONVENTIONS.md but consistent with its structure); removed the src/stores/.gitkeep placeholder now that the dir has real files.
- idb + TEST STACK INSTALLED (Step 1.8): `idb` ^8 (STACK.md's chosen IndexedDB wrapper) as a runtime dep; `vitest` + `happy-dom` + `fake-indexeddb` as dev deps. Step 1.8 explicitly requires a kill-and-reload test, so a runner was needed now — vitest (also what Step 12.1's unit tests will use), happy-dom for the window/document the autosave close-protection listeners need, fake-indexeddb for an in-memory IndexedDB in tests. Added `"test": "vitest run"` script, vitest.config.ts (env happy-dom, setup src/test/setup.ts loading fake-indexeddb/auto), and src/test/setup.ts. First test infrastructure in the repo.
- SINGLE ATOMIC TRANSACTION PER AUTOSAVE TICK (Step 1.8): saveNow() writes BOTH stores' persisted state in one IndexedDB readwrite transaction (object store "state", keys "session"/"account", DB "divergence-ai" v1). IndexedDB transactions are atomic, so a refresh/crash mid-write aborts the whole transaction and the last COMPLETE write remains — satisfies the "no corruption, last complete write wins" requirement by construction. Both stores in one tx also keeps them mutually consistent per tick. Verified by the "last complete write wins" test.
- CLOSE-PROTECTION FLUSH BEYOND THE 5s INTERVAL (Step 1.8): startAutosave() runs the 5s setInterval AND also flushes on `pagehide` and on `visibilitychange`→hidden, because CANON says "Crash and close protection" — a tab closed between ticks must not lose work. Returns a stop() that clears the interval and removes both listeners. The pagehide flush + stop() cleanup are unit-tested; the raw 5s timer firing is not directly tested (fake timers deadlock fake-indexeddb — see PARKED), but the interval is a trivial wrapper over the well-tested saveNow().
- RESTORE-BEFORE-RENDER IN main.tsx (Step 1.8): bootstrap() awaits loadPersistedState() BEFORE createRoot().render(), so the user never sees a default-then-restored flicker, then calls startAutosave(). The load is wrapped in try/catch so a blocked/failed IndexedDB (e.g. private browsing) still renders the app from defaults rather than white-screening. IDB read is milliseconds; if it ever costs perceptible startup time, switch to render-then-hydrate with a hydrating flag (see PARKED).
- PERSISTENCE USES THE 1.7 SEAM, HARDCODES NOTHING (Step 1.8): persistence.ts reads SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS/ACCOUNT_PERSISTED_KEYS and calls each store's hydrate() — no store field names are hardcoded in the persistence layer, so a future step adding a field to a store + its *_PERSISTED_KEYS gets it persisted automatically. Type-only import of SessionState/AccountState from stores/types.ts (they aren't re-exported by name from the store files).
- NEW `src/keyboard/` MODULE + PURE-CORE/THIN-HOOK ARCHITECTURE (Step 1.9): keyboard framework lives in a new top-level `src/keyboard/` dir — added like `components/primitives/` (Step 1.4) because it's cross-cutting infra used by every future interactive component, not a single CANON feature; CONVENTIONS.md's tree is illustrative, logged here per rule 3 transparency. Each capability is a framework-agnostic core (isTypingTarget, dismissLayers, shortcutRegistry, focusTrap, confirmable) with a thin React-hook wrapper. Chose this split so the cores are unit-testable under happy-dom WITHOUT adding @testing-library/react — no new dependency (RTL was considered and rejected for that reason).
- ESCAPE STACK IS CAPTURE-PHASE AND DELIBERATELY SEPARATE FROM SHORTCUT TYPING-ISOLATION (Step 1.9): dismissLayers uses a capture-phase document keydown listener over a LIFO stack and dismisses ONLY the topmost layer. It is intentionally NOT routed through shortcutRegistry's typing-isolation, because Escape-to-close must fire even while the user is typing in a modal's field (dismiss is not a "shortcut"). Global shortcuts (shortcutRegistry, bubble phase) DO honor typing isolation. A later step must not "unify" these two paths — the asymmetry is the point.
- SHORTCUT COMBO SEMANTICS (Step 1.9): "mod" = ctrlKey||metaKey so one combo string works on Windows/Linux/macOS; a bare-key shortcut ("?", "n") rejects events carrying ctrl/meta so it never collides with a modified combo. Typing isolation defaults ON (shortcut skipped while in a field); a shortcut meant to act while typing (e.g. mod+enter to send) must opt in with allowInTextField.
- DESTRUCTIVE SAFETY = TWO-STEP CONFIRMABLE (Step 1.9): createConfirmable/useConfirmable arm-then-confirm with a 4s auto-disarm — first trigger only arms, second within the window runs it — so a single stray keypress can never fire a destructive action (CANON ADHD "Only confirm destructive actions"; step req 4). Later destructive controls (Close Session discard, Replace Answer) must route through this rather than binding a bare action to a key/click.
- GLOBAL FOCUS RING VIA :where() + LAZY LISTENERS (Step 1.9): src/styles/keyboard.css adds one specificity-0 :focus-visible cyan ring for all native focusables, complementing primitives.css's per-class rings so non-primitive focusables get one free; imported in main.tsx after shell.css. dismissLayers and shortcutRegistry each attach their single document listener lazily on first registration and detach on last unregister, so the framework is fully inert with zero consumers — no bootstrap wiring in main.tsx beyond the CSS import.
- STEP 2.1 IS DESIGN/SPEC ONLY (Step 2.1): produced TRANSLATION-SPEC.md + the schema/prompt in code (src/services/translation/), NOT a runtime service or live model call — the service is Step 2.2, gates are 2.3. New `src/services/translation/` folder groups schema.ts/prompt.ts/index.ts under services/ (CONVENTIONS scopes services/ to non-UI logic incl. the engines; consistent with persistence living there); 2.2 adds the service here.
- CANONICAL PROMPT + SCHEMA LIVE IN CODE; SPEC QUOTES THEM, CODE WINS ON DRIFT (Step 2.1): TRANSLATION_SYSTEM_PROMPT (prompt.ts) and TranslationResult/parseTranslationOutput (schema.ts) are the single source of truth; TRANSLATION-SPEC.md documents and reproduces them but explicitly declares the code authoritative if they diverge. Chose one authoritative in-code copy over spec-as-authority so Step 2.2 imports the prompt rather than re-transcribing it (drift prevention). A later step editing the prompt must edit prompt.ts and re-sync the spec's quoted block.
- SCHEMA ADDS `reasoning` BEYOND THE REQUIRED THREE FIELDS (Step 2.1): the step names translatedPrompt/confidence/detectedGaps; added a required `reasoning` string per CONVENTIONS rule 6 (design the data shape for the Transparency card now) and CANON ADHD Feedback (must be non-judgmental). Step 8.2's transparency card consumes it. A superset of the required schema, not a contradiction — later steps may read reasoning but must keep the three core fields.
- VALIDATOR IS TOLERANT-BUT-STRICT (Step 2.1): parseTranslationOutput clamps confidence to 0-100 and rounds, treats missing/garbage confidence as 0 (safe — never fabricates a high score that would skip the gates), drops unknown gap ids + dedupes, fills a reasoning fallback — but THROWS TranslationSchemaError when translatedPrompt is missing/empty or the reply isn't an object. Only the buried-request field is load-bearing; LLM output is otherwise treated as noisy. Extracting JSON from raw response text (fences/streaming) is deliberately left to the 2.2 runtime, not the schema.
- GAP TYPE IDS ARE THE STABLE CONTRACT (Step 2.1): the six kebab ids in GAP_TYPES are shared by the model output, the schema, the detected-gaps UI, and the transparency card; renaming any requires updating prompt.ts + TRANSLATION-SPEC.md together. GAP_TYPE_LABELS supplies display strings so UI steps don't hardcode gap names.
- INJECTED MODEL-CLIENT SEAM, NOT A CONCRETE CLIENT (Step 2.2): translate() takes a TranslationModelClient (client.ts) as an injected dependency because the real proxy is Step 1.10 (absent). Step 1.10's proxy client implements the seam later with zero change to translate(); tests and the harness pass stubs. The seam is a single NON-streamed completion returning Promise<string> — translation emits one JSON object, not a user-visible stream (streaming is answer-display, PIPELINE Stage 5). TRANSLATION_MODEL="claude-sonnet-5" lives in client.ts provisionally; point it at 1.10's model registry once that exists.
- translate() NEVER THROWS — TYPED TranslationOutcome (Step 2.2): the step's "handle empty, huge, API failure without crashing" is met with a discriminated union {ok|empty|too-large|error} the caller switches on, chosen over throwing so the clarify UI (2.3) and composer (5.0+) render each case deliberately. Empty/too-large short-circuit before ever calling the client.
- HUGE INPUT REJECTED, NOT TRUNCATED (Step 2.2): input over MAX_TRANSLATION_INPUT_CHARS (24k chars ≈ ~6k tokens; provisional, per-call configurable) returns {too-large} rather than truncating — truncation could drop the real request, which in ADHD input often trails after preamble. CANON's 10/50MB limits are for loaded CONTEXT, not this box; revisit the cap when Step 1.10's registry exposes real token budgets.
- RESPONSE-TEXT JSON EXTRACTION LIVES IN THE SERVICE (Step 2.2): extractJsonObject (translate.ts) strips ```json fences and, if needed, slices first-"{" to last-"}" before JSON.parse, tolerating models that wrap JSON in prose. Kept in the runtime service, NOT schema.ts (which by the 2.1 boundary validates already-parsed objects only). Any parse or schema failure becomes {error}, never a throw.
- MANUAL HARNESS IS OFFLINE-BY-DEFAULT (Step 2.2): runTranslationHarness defaults to stubTranslationClient (canned schema-shaped JSON) so it runs the full input→service→validate→outcome path without the absent proxy; pass the real 1.10 client for genuine translations. Exercised by harness.test.ts. Satisfies the step's "small manual test harness"; a live-model manual run is parked on 1.10.
- ROUTING.md "THE ONE CHANGE" APPLIED + STALE LABELS FIXED (Step 1.10): changed routing.js's Balanced api string claude-sonnet-4-6 → claude-sonnet-5 (the required change; the spot check flagged the 4-6 string). Also corrected two now-stale DISPLAY strings the change orphaned — the MODELS-table label "Sonnet 4.6" → "Sonnet 5" (same line; it drives every dynamic reasoning string via MODELS[choice].label) and one hardcoded "Sonnet 4.6" inside a downgrade note — plus the false comment claiming claude-sonnet-5 doesn't exist. NO scoring logic touched (scoreComplexity / classifyDimensions / route branches / thresholds / signal math all unchanged); the engine's own suite tests.js, run offline via a CommonJS copy, still passes clean (0 monotonicity violations, all three tiers exercised, confidence-coupling + plan-gating + overrides correct). If strict minimalism is preferred the two label-string edits can be reverted; the api string is the only behavior-relevant change.
- MODEL REGISTRY = src/services/modelRegistry.ts (Step 1.10 deliverable #2): the three CANON LOCKED DECISION 3 strings as a typed Record keyed by ModelId (aligned with stores/types.ts and routing.js's MODELS keys), each with tier + label + plan gating (free: haiku/sonnet, paid: opus). Extended thinking is ModelCallOptions.extendedThinking — a per-call FLAG, not a fourth registry entry (there are exactly 3). isModelId/getModel/MODEL_IDS are the shared accessors the proxy and future model pickers use.
- PROXY IS A PLATFORM-AGNOSTIC WEB-FETCH HANDLER (Step 1.10 deliverable #1): the handler LOGIC lives in src/services/proxyHandler.ts (typed, fetch injectable, unit-tested), forwarding to the Anthropic Messages API with the key in the x-api-key header and passing the upstream body — streamed or not — straight back. The deploy entry api/proxy.ts is a thin wrapper OUTSIDE src/ (not in tsconfig include, not in the client bundle — the deploy platform builds it) that reads ANTHROPIC_API_KEY from server env. The key never enters the browser bundle. Chosen Request→Response shape so the same logic runs on Vercel Edge / Cloudflare / Netlify Edge; host-specific wiring is Step 12.3.
- PROXY CLIENT complete() IS THE TRANSLATION SEAM (Step 1.10): src/services/proxyClient.ts createProxyClient().complete is structurally a TranslationModelClient, so translate() calls models through the proxy with NO adapter — proven by an end-to-end test (translate() → proxyClient.complete → mocked fetch). stream() parses Anthropic SSE (content_block_delta/text_delta) into text deltas for answer display (Step 5.1). This closes the Step 2.2 injected-seam loop. Also retyped translation/client.ts's TRANSLATION_MODEL as ModelId (value unchanged) so it's checked against the registry — resolves a Step 2.2 parked note.
- routing.js/tests.js KEPT AT REPO ROOT AS SOURCE-OF-RECORD (Step 1.10): committed at root (where supplied) with THE ONE CHANGE applied, but NOT yet imported by the app — ROUTING.md says Phase 3 wires the engine in. CONVENTIONS.md's src/services/ placement + a routing.d.ts is deferred to that wiring step. Note tests.js is CommonJS and can't run via `node` under this repo's "type":"module" (verified via a scratch .cjs copy this step) — also a Phase-3 cleanup.
- CONFIDENCE GATE LOGIC = src/services/translation/gate.ts (Step 2.3): evaluateConfidenceGate returns proceed (>=80) / moderate (>=60) / clarify (<60) — boundaries 80 and 60 inclusive; gateTranslation maps translate()'s TranslationOutcome to a UI-ready GatedTranslation (proceed|moderate|clarify|empty|too-large|error). This is the TRANSLATION-side gate (proceed vs clarify + the moderate note) only; the ROUTING-side coupling (escalate one step at 60-79, floor at 4 below 60) already lives in routing.js and is deliberately NOT duplicated (CONVENTIONS: don't rebuild).
- CLARIFYING QUESTION IS TEMPLATED IN CODE, NOT MODEL-GENERATED (Step 2.3): buildClarifyingQuestion reflects the engine's best-guess translatedPrompt back through a fixed neutral template ("It sounds like you might be asking: X — is that it, or would you like to add a little more?"), so the tone is guaranteed non-judgmental per CANON ADHD Feedback ("never corrective, never making the user feel stupid"). Chosen over asking the model to phrase it, which can't be guaranteed kind; a test asserts no blaming words appear.
- CONVERSATION AREA NOW HOSTS THE 2.3 UI, AS A DEMO (Step 2.3): replaced Step 1.5's center-column placeholder <p> with <ConversationArea/>. New feature folder src/components/translation/ (per CONVENTIONS components/<feature>/) + global src/styles/translation.css in main.tsx. No live pipeline exists yet (composer 5.0, orchestrator 5.2), so ConversationArea renders a DEMO of the three gate states through the REAL gateTranslation logic — replaced by the live flow in 5.0+ (precedent: Step 1.4 rendered primitives as demo content). ClarifyPrompt exposes onRefine as a callback seam the 5.2 orchestrator wires to re-run translation; it does not own re-submission.
- GATE COLORS ARE NEUTRAL, NEVER RED (Step 2.3): the confidence badge is green (--status-online) at 80+ and cyan (--state-cognitive) at 60-79; the low-confidence clarify state reads as "let's check," not an error. Red (--action-destructive) stays reserved for destructive actions (CANON ADHD Sensory rule). No new color tokens added.
- CORPUS SCORING IS PLUGGABLE; DEFAULT JUDGE IS OFFLINE + OBJECTIVE (Step 2.4): runCorpus takes a CorpusJudge. The default gapDetectionJudge passes iff translation succeeded AND the case's expected gap category was detected — objective and offline, so the harness runs and reports without a network. It is a PROXY for translation quality, not the real measure; makeSemanticJudge(client) grades translatedPrompt against "what was actually needed" via the model and is the real-accuracy path (needs the deployed proxy). Chose a pluggable judge over hardcoding so the real semantic judge drops in later without touching the runner.
- PER-CATEGORY, NOT AVERAGED (Step 2.4): runCorpus computes overall = total passed / 50 AND each category's own rate; meetsTarget = overall >= 90 AND every category >= 80 (checked per category, per PIPELINE.md "reported per category, not averaged"). A category can fail even when the overall average looks fine — a test asserts exactly that.
- CORPUS LOADED VIA VITE ?raw, NOT node fs (Step 2.4): corpus.test.ts imports the corpus markdown with `...31_0_translation_test_cases.md?raw` (typed by tsconfig.app's existing types:["vite/client"]) instead of node:fs — because test files under src/ are typechecked by the browser tsconfig, which has no node types. parseCorpus stays pure (string in), so it needs no file I/O. First ?raw import in the repo.
- CORPUS PARSED, NOT HAND-TRANSCRIBED (Step 2.4): parseCorpus reads the 50 cases straight from the supplied markdown by splitting on the "### N." case headers and reading each case's Gap category code, "what was actually needed," and verbatim ```text block. Only the 4 corpus categories (TP/EID/CBR/TPWC) are represented — a subset of the six-gap taxonomy — each case tagged with one primary gap; scope-ambiguity/unstated-assumptions don't appear as primary categories in this corpus.
- V2 CHOSEN OVER V1 FOR COMPLEXITY.md/FINDINGS.md, USER-CONFIRMED (Step 3.1): both files were missing from the repo; two non-identical prior-art variants existed outside it (V1 "Unknown Type", V2 "Free and Paid"). Per the Step-1.3 standing rule (prior-art folders are unverified until confirmed — flag and ask first), stopped and asked rather than picking either myself. Evidence for V2: its documented scoring weights (analysis verbs ×1.2/cap 4.8, synthesis ×0.8/cap 1.6, design nouns ×0.8/cap 1.6, constraints ×0.6/cap 1.8, data points ×0.4/cap 1.2, etc.) match routing.js's actual code exactly, field for field; V1 describes a visibly different, apparently-abandoned scoring scheme with no correspondence to the shipped engine. User confirmed V2; copied in as-is, unmodified.
- routing.js LIVES IN BOTH PLACES ON PURPOSE (Step 3.1): copied byte-identical into src/services/routing.js (the app's import point, per CONVENTIONS.md's services/ layout) while KEEPING the root copy — did not move/delete it. Reasons: (1) tests.js's `require("./routing.js")` assumes root-relative placement and must keep working unmodified, since "do not rebuild the scorer" extends to not breaking its own test harness; (2) future step prompts' REQUIRED FILES checks name "routing.js" and might expect it at root, same reasoning as not renaming tests.js (below). A vitest check (routingService.test.ts "embedding integrity") asserts the two copies stay byte-identical, operationalizing FINDINGS.md item 7's own verification principle ("the tested engine is provably the shipped engine") for this integration rather than just trusting a one-time copy.
- routing.d.ts + routingService.ts, ZERO LOGIC CHANGES (Step 3.1): routing.d.ts is ambient types only (RouteInput/RouteResult/Dimensions/ComplexitySignals/ModelKey), matching routing.js's actual accepted/returned shape field-for-field — verified by the routingService tests, not asserted from ROUTING.md's prose alone. routingService.ts's route() is a one-line pass-through to the vendor engine; FREE_MODEL_KEYS/ROUTING_MODELS are direct re-exports. `gaps`/`state` are typed as optional per routing.js's own header-comment contract but documented as NOT read by the current scorer body — forward-compatible typing, not new behavior.
- ROUTING.js's ModelKey (haiku/sonnet/opus) IS A DIFFERENT KEYING SCHEME THAN modelRegistry's ModelId (Step 3.1, reconciled): routing.js/routing.d.ts key by short engine names ("haiku"/"sonnet"/"opus" — routingService.ts's ModelKey, matching the ModelKey type already anticipated in modelRegistry.ts at Step 1.10); modelRegistry.ts keys by the full api string (ModelId). A cross-check test confirms every routing.js api string exists in MODEL_REGISTRY with a matching id, so the two independently-authored sources (vendor engine vs. this app's registry) can't silently drift apart. Callers translating between the two use ROUTING_MODELS[key].api ↔ MODEL_REGISTRY[apiString].
- NO UI WIRING IN STEP 3.1 (deliberate scope boundary): the routing service is now a typed, tested, importable module, but nothing in the app calls route() yet — same "service built, not yet consumed" pattern as Steps 1.10/2.1. Step 3.2 is explicitly tagged "[SONNET to wire UI]" in its own title, so decision-logic/override UI is that step's job, not 3.1's. The pipeline orchestrator that calls route() on a live translated prompt is Step 5.2.
- tests.js STILL CANNOT RUN VIA NATIVE `node tests.js` (Step 3.1, re-confirmed, not fixed): the repo's package.json `"type":"module"` makes node treat the CommonJS tests.js as ESM and throw on `require`. Verified instead via a scratch .cjs copy (same technique as Step 1.10) — output unchanged (0 monotonicity violations, all 3 tiers, override honored). Deliberately did NOT rename tests.js to .cjs to fix this, despite Step 1.10 flagging it as a "Phase-3 cleanup": a future step's REQUIRED FILES check may expect the literal name "tests.js," and renaming a vendor-supplied file its own docs (FINDINGS.md, ROUTING.md) refer to by name risks a worse problem than the running inconvenience. Remains a manual-verification-only file.
- MODEL DROPDOWN BINDS DIRECTLY TO THE STEP-1.7 SessionState.model FIELD (Step 3.2): `model: ModelSelection` (default "auto") and `setModel()` already existed, built ahead of time anticipating exactly this control — no new store field, no new persistence wiring (SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS already includes "model" since Step 1.7/1.8). ModelDropdown is a pure binding; all state lives in the pre-existing store.
- DROPDOWN NEVER GATES OPUS ITSELF; THE ENGINE'S NOTE DOES (Step 3.2): the Model dropdown always offers all 4 options (Auto + 3 models) regardless of plan — it does not hide/disable Opus on the free plan. routing.js's own override logic already blocks a free-plan Opus override and returns a visible note instead ("requires the paid plan... kept automatic routing"); disabling the option in the UI would duplicate that gate in two places and risk them drifting apart, and would also violate CANON's "never a black box" rule by hiding the choice rather than explaining the outcome. ModelRoutingDemo surfaces the note live so this is provable, not asserted.
- OPUS DESCRIPTOR "smartest" IS SCREENSHOT-CONFIRMED; HAIKU/SONNET ARE INFERRED (Step 3.2): the V3 screenshot shows exactly "Opus 4.8 — smartest" selected in the Model dropdown — used verbatim. Haiku's "fastest" and Sonnet's "balanced" are NOT visible in the screenshot (only one option is shown, not the open dropdown list); inferred to match ROUTING.md's own tier names (Fast/Balanced/Deep) phrased as adjectives, same eyeball-with-a-flag pattern as Step 1.2's pill colors. A future pixel/interaction check should open the real dropdown against the source design to confirm.
- overrideFromSelection() IS DEFENSIVE, NEVER THROWS (Step 3.2): a persisted `model` value that isn't "auto" and isn't a recognized ModelId (e.g. a stale api string from a future/removed model, or corrupted IndexedDB state) maps to `null` (auto) rather than crashing route(). Consistent with the app's existing defensive patterns (persistence.ts try/catch, gate.ts confidence clamping) — a UI control's stored value should never be trusted enough to crash the routing call.
- ModelRoutingDemo REUSES THE EXISTING plan FLAG, ADDS NO NEW STATE (Step 3.2): the demo's "Paid plan" checkbox is bound to the Step 1.7 accountStore.plan/setPlan (already-existing, already-persisted) so BOTH spot-checked behaviors — override always wins on paid, free plan gates Opus with a note — are demonstrable live from the UI, without inventing a demo-only toggle. Toggling it here has the same real effect a future billing/plan step would have; Step 3.2 doesn't add billing, per ROUTING.md ("a routing layer flag, not billing infrastructure").
- SAMPLE PROMPT IS FIXED, NOT USER-EDITABLE (Step 3.2): ModelRoutingDemo routes one hardcoded mid-complexity prompt ("design a caching strategy...") rather than exposing a text box, since there is no composer yet (Step 5.0) and building one would be scope creep beyond "wire the Model dropdown." The fixed prompt was chosen to actually reach Opus at complexity when unconstrained, so the free-plan downgrade note has something to show.
- STEP 3.3 IS A TEST-ONLY VERIFY, NO PRODUCTION CODE (Step 3.3): the confidence coupling was already in routing.js and did not need building — confidenceCoupling.test.ts only proves the translation→routing SEAM works end to end at the confidence field. It asserts on route()'s complexity/effectiveComplexity/notes generically (base complexity read from the result, not pinned to a magic number) so it stays robust if the scorer's exact scores shift. The <60 band is documented as routing's SAFETY NET — the normal path is the Step 2.3 clarify gate catching <60 before it ever reaches route() (CONFIDENCE_COUPLING.md item 3), so both layers are intentional, not redundant.
- TECHNIQUE REGISTRY IS THE EXECUTABLE SOURCE OF TRUTH; TECHNIQUE-MATRIX.md DOCUMENTS IT (Step 4.1): registry.ts (conflicts/dependencies as data) is authoritative; TECHNIQUE-MATRIX.md is the human-readable explanation and declares the code wins on drift. Chose data-in-code over prose-as-authority so the Step 4.2 scorer consumes the matrix directly and registry.test.ts enforces the invariants (symmetric conflicts, no dependency on a conflict) — a later step can't add an incoherent conflict/dep without a red test.
- CONFLICT/DEPENDENCY MATRIX IS A DELIBERATE DESIGN CHOICE, NOT SPEC-DICTATED (Step 4.1): CANON/PIPELINE name the techniques and say "each has an effect, conflicts, and dependencies" but do NOT enumerate WHICH. Picked a minimal coherent set — conflicts only for genuine contradictions (verbosity opposites simplify⟷detailed, ask-vs-answer socratic⟷{detailed,step-by-step}, and simplify⟷chain-of-thought), dependencies only for genuine implications (verify→chain-of-thought since verifying needs reasoning to check; comparative→examples since comparisons need concrete instances). A later step (e.g. 4.2 tuning, or 11.x) may add/adjust pairs; the invariant tests + symmetric-conflict rule are the contract to preserve, not the exact pairs. Consequence logged for 4.2: verify pulls chain-of-thought, which conflicts with simplify, so verify+simplify can't co-stack — intended.
- auto-detect IS A META MODE, NOT A COMPOSABLE TECHNIQUE (Step 4.1): it sets isMeta:true, has an empty promptFragment, and is excluded from COMPOSABLE_TECHNIQUE_IDS (11) — the 4.2 scorer selects among the other 11 when auto-detect is the chosen mode, and 4.3 never injects an auto-detect fragment. Kept in the registry (and the TechniqueId union) because CANON lists it and the dropdown offers it as a selectable option.
- MAX_TECHNIQUE_STACK=4 AND DEFAULT_TECHNIQUE=socratic LIVE IN THE REGISTRY (Step 4.1): the stack cap and the default are exported constants in registry.ts (single source), so Step 4.2 (which the spot check requires to enforce ≤4) and the Step 4.5 selector read them rather than hardcoding. Matches CANON Feature 4 ("stacks at most 4", "Socratic (default)").
- AUTO-DETECT IS RULE-BASED/DETERMINISTIC, NOT MODEL-SCORED (Step 4.2): autoDetectTechniques scores by lexical signal regexes (+ optional routing complexity/domain hint boosts), same rationale as routing.js (FINDINGS.md) — offline-testable, same input→same output, fully auditable for the Transparency card. A model-scored variant is a possible future upgrade (parked). The signal regexes are a heuristic first cut; a later step (or 11.x) may tune them — the CONTRACT to preserve is the coherence invariant (≤4, conflict-free, deps satisfied), enforced on every test case, not the exact keyword lists.
- GREEDY STACK WITH DEPENDENCY CLOSURES, DEP-BEFORE-DEPENDENT (Step 4.2): selection walks techniques in score order; for each it computes the transitive dependency closure (deps first), rejects the whole closure if it would conflict with an already-picked technique or overflow the cap, else adds it. This guarantees every selected technique's dependencies are present, no conflicts, ≤4 — by construction, verified per-case. Consequence (as flagged at 4.1): verify pulls chain-of-thought which conflicts with simplify, so verify+simplify can never co-stack. Order is dependency-first (chain-of-thought before verify, examples before comparative) so it's composition-ready for Step 4.3's "techniques in order".
- SOCRATIC BASELINE = FALLBACK, NOT ALWAYS-PRESENT (Step 4.2): Socratic gets a small baseline score so a bare/ambiguous question yields [socratic] (the CANON default). But when a stronger, conflicting technique (step-by-step/detailed) scores higher, Socratic is correctly dropped from the stack — the baseline only makes it the default *when nothing better fires*, not a permanent member.
- MANUAL TECHNIQUE SELECTION DOES NOT AUTO-ADD DEPENDENCIES (Step 4.2): selectTechniques with a specific technique returns exactly [that technique]; dependencies are an AUTO-DETECT concern (CANON: "Auto-detect ... respects conflicts and dependencies"). If a user manually picks Verify, they get Verify alone, not Verify+Chain-of-Thought — their explicit choice is honored literally. Step 4.3 composition and 4.5's UI rely on this.
- COMPOSITION ORDER IS THE FIXED CONTRACT (Step 4.3): COMPOSITION_ORDER (base-template, role-prime, question, directness, techniques, output-format, confidence-requirement) is PIPELINE.md Stage 4 verbatim and must not be reordered by any later step — a test asserts the order in BOTH the sections[] array and the assembled prompt string. composeFinalPrompt always returns all 7 sections (for the Transparency card) but omits empty ones from the assembled string.
- DIRECTNESS ENCODINGS LIVE IN COMPOSITION (templates.ts), NOT 4.4 (Step 4.3): the per-level tone text (1 supportive / 2 balanced default / 3 blunt) is composition's, since composition owns the assembled prompt. Step 4.4 builds the selector UI + auto-recommend and feeds a DirectnessLevel into composeFinalPrompt — it must NOT redefine these strings. Deliberately settled here so 4.4 doesn't re-decide the wording.
- ROLE-PRIME IS HOISTED, NOT DUPLICATED (Step 4.3): Role-Prime is both a fixed composition SECTION and a technique. When the Role-Prime technique is in the stack, its fragment fills the role section and is removed from the techniques body (an explicit `role` input overrides both; otherwise a neutral DEFAULT_ROLE_PRIME fills the slot). Keeps the 7-slot structure clean; a later step adding role logic should populate the role section, not add a second role instruction among techniques.
- OUTPUT FORMAT DEFAULT ENCODES CANON'S ADHD COGNITIVE-LOAD RULES (Step 4.3): the default output-format section tells the model "no dense walls, ≤3 paragraphs, break into sections, lead with the point, scannable" — CANON ADHD hard rules injected at composition so every answer inherits them. Overridable via input.outputFormat. This is where Feature-wide ADHD output constraints are enforced in the prompt; Step 11.1's audit should check it's present.
- CONFIDENCE REQUIREMENT TIES TO TRANSLATION CONFIDENCE (Step 4.3): the confidence-requirement section always tells the model to flag ambiguity/uncertainty; when the translation confidence is <80 (the Step 2.3 moderate band that still proceeds), it additionally injects the numeric score and a "make sure you're answering what was actually asked" nudge. Uses the SAME 80 threshold as the gate, deliberately.
- DIRECTNESS SELECTOR BINDS TO THE STEP-1.7 SessionState.directness FIELD (Step 4.4): directness/setDirectness (default 2) already existed and are already persisted (SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS) — DirectnessDropdown is a pure binding, no new store state, same pattern as the Model dropdown (3.2). The chosen level feeds composeFinalPrompt's directness param; the production feed is the Step 5.2 orchestrator, the demo proves it now.
- DIRECTNESS METADATA vs ENCODING SPLIT (Step 4.4): src/services/directness.ts owns the level METADATA (labels, default, the recommend function); the per-level prompt TEXT stays in composition/templates.ts DIRECTNESS_ENCODINGS (settled at Step 4.3). Deliberately did NOT duplicate the tone strings — the selector picks a level number, composition turns it into prompt text. One source for the wording.
- DIRECTNESS OPTIONS HAVE NO DESCRIPTOR SUFFIX (Step 4.4): unlike the Model dropdown ("Opus 4.8 — smartest"), the directness options are plain "Directness Level N" because that is exactly what the V3 screenshot's closed dropdown shows — no "— supportive" suffix visible. The tone descriptions live in DIRECTNESS_LEVELS.tone for tooltips/the 4.5 selector, not in the option label. (If the open dropdown in the real design shows descriptors, a future pixel check can add them — same eyeball caveat as the Model dropdown's Haiku/Sonnet descriptors.)
- recommendDirectnessFromEmotion IS ONLY Overwhelmed→1 (Step 4.4): PIPELINE.md maps only the Overwhelmed emotion to a directness level (Level 1); the other emotions map to TECHNIQUES, not directness, so recommend returns null for them (caller keeps the user's/default level). The function exists now as the seam Phase-6 state detection wires into; it is not yet called anywhere (no state detection UI). Auto-recommend must never silently override a manual pick — a later step wiring it should surface it as a suggestion, per CANON "never a black box" (parked note).
- SessionState.technique WIDENED TO techniques: TechniqueId[] (Step 4.5, a real store-schema change, not just "the control"): Step 4.5's own spec text explicitly requires manual selection to support stacking (a conflicting "pair," "the 4-technique stack limit" enforced on manual choice) — impossible with the singular TechniqueId field Step 1.7 built. Before changing it, grepped the whole repo for consumers: only its own declaration + 2 generic-by-design references in persistence.test.ts existed, so the rename/retype had zero other blast radius. `["auto-detect"]` (a one-element array holding the meta id) represents auto mode; any other array is the user's literal manual stack, fed straight into composeFinalPrompt's existing `techniques?: TechniqueId[]` input. Updated together in one step, per STORE-CONTRACT.md's own rule: types.ts, sessionStore.ts (default, setTechniques, SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS), STORE-CONTRACT.md (dedicated explanatory note, not just a silent table edit), and persistence.test.ts.
- dependencyClosure/anyConflict EXPORTED FROM autoDetect.ts, LOGIC UNCHANGED (Step 4.5): these were private helpers inside the Step 4.2 scorer; widened their export surface only (no line of their behavior touched) so the new manual-selection module reuses the EXACT SAME dependency-walk and conflict-check the auto-detect scorer uses, rather than duplicating it. This is "reuse an existing pure export," not "touch the scorer" — the step's scope boundary ("do not touch 4.2... if it looks wrong, stop") is about not rebuilding/fixing the scoring logic, which is untouched; a widened export is the same category of low-risk change as Step 3.2 adding overrideFromSelection to routingService.ts.
