- MANUAL SELECTION AUTO-INCLUDES DEPENDENCIES, VISIBLY — A DELIBERATE DEPARTURE FROM THE SINGLE-TECHNIQUE selectTechniques() BEHAVIOR (Step 4.5): Step 4.2 decided selectTechniques("verify", ...) returns exactly [verify], no auto-added deps, because that function's manual path was "the user's one explicit choice, honored literally." Step 4.5's NEW multi-select path (selectManualTechnique) is a DIFFERENT code path solving a different problem and deliberately auto-includes a technique's dependency closure when manually checked (e.g. checking Verify also checks Chain-of-Thought), always VISIBLE in the resulting array/UI summary — never hidden. Chose this over blocking-with-an-error (forcing the user to hunt down and check the dependency themselves first) because CONVENTIONS rule 6 ("no black boxes... expose what it decided and why") is satisfied by VISIBLE automation, and forcing extra clicks to discover a prerequisite adds cognitive load this very step is supposed to reduce. selectTechniques()'s single-manual-id behavior is UNCHANGED and still used wherever a single manual id needs dispatching; selectManualTechnique() is new, only used by the Step 4.5 popover.
- TECHNIQUE DROPDOWN'S CLOSED STATE IS A <button>, NOT A <select>, VERIFIED AGAINST THE ACTUAL SCREENSHOT (Step 4.5): re-viewed Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png specifically for this step (per its own instruction not to assume from the Directness dropdown or memory) — confirmed the Technique control's closed state is visually IDENTICAL to Model/Directness (label above, single value "Socratic", cyan chevron, glass background) — matched exactly via the same .primitive-dropdown wrapper class + a new trigger button styled to the existing select's CSS declarations (widened selector list, not duplicated rules). The underlying element is a <button> opening a popover (not a real <select>) because the OPEN state must support genuine multi-select, which native <select> cannot do — the screenshot shows no open state for any of the three dropdowns, so nothing here contradicts the reference image; Step 1.4 explicitly anticipated this exact situation ("a feature step needing a richer listbox... builds on top of this rather than this primitive being replaced").
- TECHNIQUE POPOVER IS THE STEP-1.9 KEYBOARD FRAMEWORK'S FIRST REAL CONSUMER (Step 4.5): useDismissableLayer (Escape closes it, topmost-layer semantics) and useFocusTrap (Tab contained inside while open, focus returns to the trigger button on close) are used exactly as designed at Step 1.9, unmodified — resolving that step's own PARKED note ("no component consumes the keyboard framework yet"). Outside-click-to-close is a NEW small addition (a document mousedown listener scoped to when the popover is open) since the Step 1.9 framework covers Escape/focus/typing-isolation/destructive-confirm but not click-outside — logged here as the first outside-click pattern in the repo, available for later overlays (e.g. Step 9.x's revolving-door accordions) to copy.
- CHECKLIST VIEWPORT CAPPED AT ~5 VISIBLE ROWS (Step 4.5): CANON's "never more than 5-7 simultaneous choices in a view" is read literally — the 11-technique checklist has a fixed max-height with internal scroll so at most ~5 rows are visible without scrolling, rather than only relying on "Auto-detect is the default" to satisfy the rule. Both together (default hides the list entirely; the list itself is capped when opened) are the step's own suggested two-part answer ("Auto-detect being the default is most of the answer; the manual list is the part that needs a shape that doesn't dump all 12 at once").
- draftInput ADDED (NOT RENAMED) TO SessionState (Step 5.0): a genuinely new field, `draftInput: string` default `""` — the step's own text is explicit that the textarea's content must be session-store state, not local component state, specifically so the existing Step 1.8 autosave covers it ("a crash mid-thought must not cost the user the thought"). Straightforward per STORE-CONTRACT.md's ADD rule (unlike 4.5's rename, no existing-consumer check was needed since it's brand new). Cleared to "" on every TRANSLATE & ASK submit (not on a timer, not on blur).
- THE THREE STEP 3.2/4.4/4.5 DEMO CARDS ARE REMOVED, NOT LEFT ALONGSIDE THE REAL CONTROL ROW (Step 5.0): ModelRoutingDemo/DirectnessDemo/TechniqueSelectionDemo each mounted the SAME dropdown component (ModelDropdown/DirectnessDropdown/TechniqueDropdown) that ControlRow now also mounts, bound to the identical session-store fields — leaving both would put two live "Model" (etc.) controls on screen simultaneously, redundant and confusing, not helpfully redundant. Each demo's own PARKED note (written at 3.2/4.4/4.5) already flagged this exact supersession as expected. Deleted the three files entirely (verified no test referenced them first) rather than leaving dead code; the underlying SERVICE logic each demo proved (routingService.route, composeFinalPrompt directness/technique feeds) remains fully unit-tested regardless of the UI demo's existence. ConversationArea (Step 2.3) was deliberately left untouched — it's the conversation/history area, Step 5.1's scope, not this one's.
- COMPOSER.TSX IS THE REAL DELIVERABLE; ComposerSection IS THE TEMPORARY WRAPPER (Step 5.0): Composer (input box + control row, emits via onSubmit prop) has zero knowledge of any "last request" demo readout — it is exactly what Step 5.2's orchestrator will mount, unchanged, passing its own real onSubmit. ComposerSection wraps it with local useState + a JSON readout purely so this step's own "emits a complete typed object" claim is provable right now, before an orchestrator exists — same demo-wrapper pattern as every prior Phase 3/4 step, but this time the WRAPPED component (not just the demo) is the lasting deliverable.
- INPUT BOX AUTO-GROWS VIA JS scrollHeight, CAPPED AT 480px THEN INTERNAL-SCROLLS (Step 5.0): satisfies "it grows, it does not clip, it does not force a length" literally for realistic ADHD-rambling input (several paragraphs) while avoiding an unbounded-height box breaking page layout for a truly enormous paste — nothing is hidden/truncated, it's still fully there, just scrollable past the cap. No `maxLength` attribute anywhere; typing is never blocked. The "0 / 2000" counter (screenshot-matched exactly) is informational only — verified by test that huge input (50k chars) still passes through buildTranslateAskRequest completely unclipped.
- TRANSLATE & ASK HAS NO CLIENT-SIDE VALIDATION GATE (Step 5.0): the button is never `disabled` — pressable on a cold load with nothing typed and nothing configured (CANON ADHD Decisions rule: sensible defaults everywhere). Empty/huge input handling is translate()'s job (Step 2.2, already built and tested), not the composer's; the composer's only responsibility is emitting the raw text faithfully.
- HONEST SCREENSHOT DIFFERENCES, LOGGED PLAINLY PER THE STEP'S OWN INSTRUCTION (Step 5.0): (1) the State Detection panel the screenshot shows between the input box and the control row is NOT built — that's Step 6.3's scope, explicitly out of bounds here; the composer currently goes straight from input box to control row. (2) NO icons anywhere (brain icon beside "WHAT'S ON YOUR MIND?" and inside TRANSLATE & ASK, paperclip/context glyphs on Attach/Context) — continues the repo-wide icon debt from Step 1.5, not newly introduced here; Attach/Context use plain-unicode chevron characters ("▾"/"›") as placeholders. (3) On a cold load the three dropdowns read Auto/Level 2/Auto-detect (CANON defaults), not the screenshot's frozen mid-session values (Opus 4.8/Level 2/Socratic) — correct per CANON's defaults, not a bug, but a real visible difference from the static reference image at first paint.
- NO NEW GLOBAL SHORTCUT ADDED (Step 5.0): the step's ADHD-rules section mentions Step 1.9's typing-isolation framework only to say typing must not accidentally fire one; it does not ask for a submit shortcut (e.g. mod+enter), so none was added. The textarea is simply never a target the Step 1.9 shortcutRegistry would act on unless a future step explicitly registers one with allowInTextField.
- ConversationMessage GAINED confidence/downgraded/notes, NOT rating (Step 5.1): STORE-CONTRACT.md named "Steps 5.1-5.2" as the owner of this provisional type's full detail — confidence/downgraded/notes are added now because the FINISHED-answer display needs them persisted (so a reload still shows "N% confident..." and any router honesty note under a HISTORICAL answer, not just a freshly-streamed one — the screenshot shows the confidence line as a permanent part of the message, not a transient toast). `rating` was deliberately NOT added: Step 8.1 ("Feedback rating") owns that field's shape and its save/learning-loop behavior; adding it now would presume a design 8.1 hasn't made yet. The 5-star row (RatingRow) is still fully real/clickable today — it just doesn't persist anywhere until 8.1 wires it.
- streamAnswer() TAKES AN AsyncIterable<string>, MATCHING proxyClient.stream()'S SHAPE EXACTLY (Step 5.1): Step 1.10's createProxyClient().stream() already yields text deltas as an AsyncGenerator<string> — streamAnswer's first parameter accepts that shape directly, so Step 5.2's real orchestrator can pass the live proxy stream straight in with zero adapter code. The four named stages (translating/routing/composing/answering) are yielded by WHOEVER drives the pipeline (the demo, or Step 5.2), not by streamAnswer itself — streamAnswer only owns the token-arrival→done transition, since only the driver knows how long the real translate()/route()/composeFinalPrompt() calls actually take.
- STAGE DELAYS ARE A DEMO-ONLY CONCERN, NOT A RENDER-SPEED VIOLATION (Step 5.1): the offline demo sleeps 350ms per stage / 40ms per token purely so a human watching can perceive the cycle; CANON's "every visual interaction responds under 300ms" governs RENDER responsiveness (state arrives → DOM reflects it, which is instant here, plain synchronous JSX with no CSS transition to await), not how long a real pipeline stage takes to produce that state — that's exactly what the stage indicator is FOR (CANON Time rule: "show an indicator for any wait over 1 second"). Step 5.2's real driver will yield stage states as fast or slow as the actual translate/route/compose calls take; nothing here hardcodes a pipeline duration.
- THE STEP 2.3 GATE/CLARIFY DEMO CARDS WERE REMOVED FROM ConversationArea, THE UNDERLYING CODE WAS NOT (Step 5.1): gate.ts, TranslationCard.tsx, ClarifyPrompt.tsx, ConfidenceBadge.tsx are untouched and still fully unit-tested — only the THREE STATIC SAMPLE CARDS that demoed them inside ConversationArea are gone, replaced by real session.conversation rendering + the streaming demo. They become relevant again the moment Step 5.2's orchestrator decides (via gateTranslation) to show a clarifying question instead of streaming an answer — that wiring, not new UI, is what's still needed.
- ORCHESTRATOR = ASYNC GENERATOR OF TYPED PER-STAGE EVENTS, DEPS INJECTED (Step 5.2): runPipeline(request, {client, plan, signal}) yields one PipelineEvent per stage output (stage/translation/route/techniques/composed/streaming/done/error) — chosen over a callback bag or a single promise so each handoff is observable/loggable the moment it exists and terminal early-exits are structural (the generator returns after a non-proceed/moderate translation event; routing is provably never called on clarify). No store imports and no concrete network client in the service (same seam pattern as translate()); createProxyClient() satisfies the PipelineModelClient slice as-is. PIPELINE-CONTRACT.md documents every handoff; the code is declared authoritative on drift.
- TECHNIQUE SELECTION + COMPOSITION SHARE THE "composing" DISPLAY STAGE (Step 5.2): PIPELINE.md has five build stages but Step 5.1's PIPELINE_STAGES fixes four display names verbatim (translating/routing/composing/answering) — resolved by yielding stage "composing" once, before technique selection, covering both. Neither list was altered.
- STAGE-3 DISPATCH + HINTS (Step 5.2): stored techniques containing "auto-detect" (or empty/corrupted after filtering) → autoDetectTechniques on the TRANSLATED prompt with hints {complexity: route.complexity (base, NOT effectiveComplexity — confidence escalation is about model choice, not technique fit), domain: route.dimensions.domain}; any other array is the user's literal manual stack, order preserved, defensively filtered to known composable ids AND re-truncated to MAX_TECHNIQUE_STACK (the truncation added after a Step 5.2 subagent audit caught that the manual path trusted Step 4.5's UI for the ≤4 cap — CANON's numbers are hard rules, so the orchestrator enforces it too).
- done CARRIES routing.js's notes VERBATIM, THE GATE NOTE IS NOT DUPLICATED INTO THEM (Step 5.2): the finished answer's notes are exactly RouteResult.notes (the 60-79 escalation note among them is the permanent visible moderate-confidence marker); Step 2.3's MODERATE_CONFIDENCE_NOTE renders in the TranslationCard during the run instead. Chosen over prepending the gate note into done.notes, which would say the same thing twice in one AnswerMeta block. Nothing is filtered or dropped — a test asserts done.notes === route.notes.
- COMPOSED PROMPT SENT AS THE SINGLE USER MESSAGE TO THE ROUTED apiString (Step 5.2): execution calls client.stream({model: route.apiString, input: composed.prompt, extendedThinking: route.thinkingApplied || undefined}) with NO separate system prompt — composition's 7-section prompt IS the whole instruction set (a separate answering system prompt would be a second, competing instruction channel no spec asks for). Extended thinking is exactly what routing decided, never re-derived.
- CenterColumn REPLACES ComposerSection + StreamingAnswerDemo, BOTH DELETED (Step 5.2): new components/pipeline/CenterColumn mounts the UNCHANGED Composer and ConversationArea (now accepting children for the transient run UI) — the Step 5.0 JSON-readout wrapper and Step 5.1 offline demo (plus streamingDemo.ts) existed only because no orchestrator did, and both their own log entries predicted this supersession. Step 2.3's TranslationCard/ClarifyPrompt render live at last (every gated kind, including clarify). Refine appends the user's addition as its own user message and re-runs the WHOLE pipeline on original+"\n\n"+addition (translation needs the full thought; the addition alone usually isn't self-contained), with settings re-read fresh from the store.
- CONVERSATION APPENDS ARE THE HOST'S JOB, NOT THE SERVICE'S (Step 5.2): CenterColumn appends the raw input as a user message on non-empty submit only (whitespace-only submits still run — translate() owns empty-handling per Step 5.0 — but add no empty bubble), and appends the assistant message on done with confidence/downgraded/notes. A resubmit aborts the in-flight run via AbortController. The in-flight run itself is deliberately NOT persisted.
- MessageBubble/StreamingAnswer IMPORTED CROSS-FOLDER INTO components/translation/ConversationArea.tsx (Step 5.1): ConversationArea.tsx stayed in its Step 2.3 location (components/translation/) rather than moving to components/streaming/ or a new components/conversation/ — it's becoming a more general "conversation history" host than a translation-specific one, but relocating it wasn't necessary to complete this step and CONVENTIONS already has precedent for cross-feature-folder imports (Step 5.0's ControlRow imports from routing/directness/techniques). Flagged here rather than silently left; a future step is free to rename/relocate if the translation/ location keeps feeling wrong, but it isn't blocking anything now.
- RESILIENCE WRAPS deps.client INSIDE runPipeline, NOT AT THE CALLER (Step 5.3): makeResilientClient is applied once, at the top of runPipeline, over the raw PipelineModelClient every caller passes — rejected wrapping it in CenterColumn (or wherever createProxyClient() is instantiated) because that leaves resilience opt-in per call-site, exactly the kind of thing a future blind session could forget. Baking it into the orchestrator means every consumer (production UI, tests, any future harness) gets retry+timeout by construction. PipelineDeps.client's doc comment now explicitly says "do not pre-wrap."
- runPipeline NEVER THROWS — EVERY STAGE GETS ITS OWN try/catch (Step 5.3): extends translate()'s Step 2.2 never-throws guarantee to the whole generator. Routing/technique-selection/composition are pure and synchronous with no real throw path (confirmed by the Step 5.2 stage audits) — their boundaries are defensive-only (convert an unexpected bug into a neutral terminal event instead of crashing), NOT retried (retrying a deterministic function that just threw on the same input throws again). Only the two network-touching calls (translation's complete, execution's stream) get real retry/timeout via the resilient client.
- STREAM RETRY IS TIME-TO-FIRST-TOKEN ONLY (Step 5.3): makeResilientClient's stream() wrapper retries a failure ONLY if it happens before any chunk has been yielded. Once the model has started producing real output, a failure ends the run as a terminal error instead of retrying — retrying after partial output would replay from the start and show the user duplicated/reset text. This is the one place retry policy differs between complete() (whole-call retry, since translation is a single non-streamed JSON reply) and stream().
- NO NEW UI FOR RETRIES/TIMEOUTS (Step 5.3): CANON ADHD "no countdowns, no forced timeouts" governs what's user-visible, not whether an internal safeguard against a hung request may exist — the timeout IS real (default 30s per attempt, graceful — ends in the existing neutral error copy, never a ticking countdown). No new "retrying…" indicator was added: the existing per-stage indicator (Step 5.1/5.2, visible from the moment a stage starts, before any wait begins) already satisfies "show an indicator for any wait over 1 second" as a strict superset, and a flickering retry sub-state would itself risk CANON's "no competing visual stimuli" rule. Retries happen silently behind the same, already-visible stage indicator.
- RESILIENCE DEFAULTS: 3 ATTEMPTS, 300ms DOUBLING BACKOFF, 30s PER-ATTEMPT TIMEOUT (Step 5.3): picked as reasonable defaults for a proxy call with no documented SLA in CANON/PIPELINE/ROUTING; all three are overridable per-call via PipelineDeps.resilience (tests use tiny values so retry/timeout paths run in milliseconds, not real seconds). Revisit if the real deployed proxy (Step 12.3) shows these need tuning.
- STAGE 2-4 ERROR BOUNDARIES ARE UNTESTED-BY-FORCE, HONESTLY (Step 5.3): routing.js/technique-selection/composition have no real throw path reachable from valid pipeline input (the Step 5.2 stage audits already established this), so their new try/catch boundaries could not be exercised with a genuine forced failure without hacking protected/pure logic purely for test theater — declined to write a fake test that would trivially always pass. Only the two network stages (translation, execution) have real forced-failure tests (resilience.test.ts + 4 orchestrator integration tests). If a future step gives routing/techniques/composition a real fallible path (e.g. Phase 6 state detection touching one of them), that step should add the forcing test then.
- TELEMETRY IS A TEE AROUND runPipeline, NOT A CHANGE TO IT (Step 5.4): built observePipeline (services/telemetry/) as a wrapper that consumes runPipeline's own event stream and re-yields it unchanged, rather than adding logging calls inside orchestrator.ts itself. Chosen so the orchestrator (already fresh-context-audited at Step 5.2, error-boundary-hardened at Step 5.3) stays untouched — a third step editing the same file for a third unrelated concern was exactly the kind of repeated-touch risk CONVENTIONS' "one owning step per file" spirit warns against. Precedent: CenterColumn/usePipelineRun (Step 5.2) already established the "wrap and re-yield" tee pattern for consuming PipelineEvent; observePipeline is the same shape one layer earlier.
- TELEMETRY LOG IS IN-MEMORY, NOT A THIRD STORE, NOT YET PERSISTED (Step 5.4): CANON "STORES AND PERSISTENCE" plans exactly two Zustand stores and neither CANON nor STORE-CONTRACT.md names a telemetry field — adding one now would presume a persistence-duration decision (does it survive a reload? sync across sessions?) that isn't this step's to make. log.ts is a bounded (200-entry) in-memory ring buffer with a subscribe seam; whichever step builds the first real reader (Token Usage panel Step 9.6, or Learning Loop Step 10.1, which explicitly needs 15+ questions of history to survive at least the session) should decide then whether entries need IndexedDB persistence, and wire it FROM this module rather than duplicating the log.
- TOKEN USAGE CAPTURED VIA AN ADDITIVE onUsage CALLBACK ON ProxyCompletionRequest, NOT A RETURN-TYPE CHANGE (Step 5.4): complete()'s Promise<string> and stream()'s AsyncGenerator<string> are both UNCHANGED — changing either to return a richer object would ripple through the TranslationModelClient (Step 2.2) and PipelineModelClient (Step 5.2) seams, both established contracts other steps already build against. An optional onUsage(usage) callback lets any caller that wants token counts ask for them without disturbing callers that don't (translate.ts never sets it — its narrower ModelCompletionRequest type doesn't even have the field — and still works exactly as before). Parses Anthropic's real response shapes: complete() reads response.usage; stream() accumulates message_start's input_tokens and message_delta's output_tokens across the SSE event stream, reporting once at the end.
- translationTokens/executionTokens KEPT SEPARATE, NOT SUMMED (Step 5.4): the step's own wording is "token counts in and out per call" (plural calls per request — translation's completion and execution's stream are two separate model calls); summing them into one pair of numbers would lose which call cost what, information a future Token Usage panel or cost-analysis reader would want back. A consumer that only wants a total can add the two pairs itself; the reverse (splitting a stored sum back into components) isn't possible.
- DETECTION SERVICE MIRRORS THE TRANSLATION SERVICE, DELIBERATELY (Step 6.1): src/services/detection/ copies translation/'s 3-part shape (schema + client-seam + never-throws service) field-for-field rather than inventing a new pattern — because PIPELINE.md's RESOLVED note says detection fires ON THE SAME BUTTON PRESS ALONGSIDE translation on the SAME input, so keeping the two outcome unions/seams parallel makes the Step 6.3/6.5 code that drives both at once uniform. detectState()'s DetectionOutcome {ok|empty|too-large|error} is the exact shape of translate()'s TranslationOutcome; DetectionModelClient is structurally identical to TranslationModelClient (so one proxyClient.complete satisfies both, no adapter).
- DETECTION DIMENSIONS REUSE stores/types.ts UNIONS, NO NEW ENUMS (Step 6.1): StateDetectionResult's four values are EmotionState/RsdLevel/InterestLevel/CognitiveMode straight from stores/types.ts (Step 1.7 fixed them, and StatePills already holds exactly these). Detection defines only the runtime-check ARRAYS (EMOTION_STATES etc.) alongside; toStatePills() projects a result onto StatePills with zero remapping. A future step must not re-declare these unions in detection/ — the store owns them.
- DETECTION IS TOLERANT WITH NO LOAD-BEARING FIELD; UNKNOWN → null, NOT A FABRICATED NEUTRAL (Step 6.1): unlike translation (which THROWS when translatedPrompt is missing, because a wrong request is catastrophic), parseDetectionOutput throws ONLY on a non-object reply — every one of the four dimensions may independently be null. An unknown/garbage dimension value becomes null (pill simply not shown), NOT a defaulted middle value (calm/medium/processing), because a fabricated neutral pill would mislead the user about what was actually detected (CANON: pills are what the app really read, correctable, never a black box). A missing/garbage confidence → 0 (unsure), never fabricated high.
- DimensionReading CARRIES PER-DIMENSION confidence, NOT ONE OVERALL SCORE (Step 6.1): each of the four reads gets its own {value, confidence} because they are independent classifications — the transparency card (Feature 8) will want to show how sure each pill is, and a low-confidence dimension can be filtered/de-emphasized by the UI (6.3) without affecting the others. Rejected a single result-level confidence as lossy. The summary string (user-facing panel line) is separate from all four.
- SYSTEM IMPACTS ARE DERIVED IN CODE, NOT EMITTED BY THE MODEL (Step 6.2): STATE_IMPACTS (impacts.ts) maps each detected state VALUE → its directness/technique/tone impact; the model returns only the four values, and impactsFor()/recommendedDirectness()/suggestedTechniques() derive the impacts deterministically. Chosen over asking the model to also emit the impact so the mapping is auditable and can't be hallucinated into a different recommendation — same deterministic-where-possible philosophy as routing.js/technique auto-detect (FINDINGS.md). STATE_IMPACTS is the single authoritative source the prompt, the spec, and Step 6.5's feed all read.
- "X or Y" IMPACTS RECORD BOTH AS CANDIDATES; 6.5 RECONCILES, DOESN'T BLIND-STACK (Step 6.2): PIPELINE.md's "High interest → Detailed OR Comparative", "Analytical → CoT OR Step-by-step", "Creative → Metaphor OR Comparative" are stored as two-element techniques[] arrays labelled candidates. STATE_IMPACTS only records what each state points at; Step 6.5's state-feed must run these through auto-detect's existing conflict/dependency/≤4-cap logic (not stack all of them, and note e.g. analytical's CoT+Step-by-step don't conflict but socratic⟷step-by-step would if emotion=calm co-fires). Deferring the resolution to 6.5 keeps 6.2 to "what the state means," not "how techniques combine."
- recommendedDirectness() DUPLICATES-BY-DESIGN directness.ts's recommendDirectnessFromEmotion, KEPT CONSISTENT BY TEST (Step 6.2): both encode "Overwhelmed → Level 1, else null." Rather than have one call the other (cross-service coupling between detection/ and the directness service), they're independent one-liners with a test asserting they agree across all five emotions. If a future step changes the emotion→directness mapping it must change both (the test fails otherwise). Acceptable duplication for two trivial pure functions; revisit only if the mapping grows complex.
- DETECTION_SYSTEM_PROMPT REWRITTEN, NOT the SCHEMA (Step 6.2): 6.1's schema.ts was already final (tolerant, no load-bearing field); 6.2 confirmed and documented it rather than changing it. Only prompt.ts changed (the provisional 6.1 classifier → the haiku-tuned, impact-naming final). A test (impacts.test.ts) asserts prompt/table consistency so the two can't silently drift — same protection technique as routing.js↔MODELS and technique-registry↔TECHNIQUE-MATRIX.
- DETECTION FIRES ALONGSIDE THE PIPELINE, NOT INSIDE IT (Step 6.3): PIPELINE.md's RESOLVED note describes State Detection as its own single on-demand classification on the SAME input as translation, not one of the five stages orchestrator.ts runs — so CenterColumn calls detectState() as a parallel side effect inside startRun() (sharing the submission's AbortController), never touching orchestrator.ts (already fresh-context-audited at 5.2, error-boundary-hardened at 5.3 — a 3rd unrelated edit was exactly the repeated-touch risk avoided at telemetry, Step 5.4, for the same file). Consistent with Step 5.4's own "wrap and re-yield" precedent, but detection isn't even a tee on the pipeline's events — it's a fully independent call.
- DETECTION RESULT SPLITS INTO RICH LOCAL STATE + THE EXISTING statePills STORE FIELD (Step 6.3): CenterColumn keeps the full StateDetectionResult (confidence, summary — needed for display/correction) in local React state, NOT persisted, same treatment as the in-flight pipeline run itself (Step 5.2 PARKED: "the in-flight run itself is NOT persisted"); only the four projected VALUES go to session.statePills (Step 1.7's pre-existing field) via toStatePills(), which is the durable "one source" Step 6.5's state bus will read. Rejected inventing a third store or persisting the rich result — CANON/STORE-CONTRACT plan exactly two stores and neither names confidence/summary as persisted fields.
- REFINE RE-FIRES DETECTION FOR FREE, BY SHARING startRun (Step 6.3): detection-firing lives inside startRun() (not handleSubmit), so ClarifyPrompt's "Refine and try again" path — which already calls startRun with the combined original+addition text — automatically re-classifies state on the refined input too, with zero separate wiring. If a future step ever splits startRun's pipeline-start and detection-start apart, it must keep both call sites (submit AND refine) firing detection, not just submit.
- PILL CORRECTION IN 6.3 IS DISPLAY-ONLY; RECORDING IS 6.4's JOB (Step 6.3): handleCorrectState (CenterColumn) updates the panel's displayed value and session.statePills immediately (so a correction visibly takes effect) but writes NOTHING to the account store and touches no correction-count/threshold logic — CANON's "After 15+ corrections... detection adapts" is explicitly Step 6.4's scope ("Correction learning"), not this step's. A future 6.4 implementation should hook into this SAME handleCorrectState call site (or an equivalent onCorrect callback) to also record the correction, rather than building a second, parallel correction-capture path.
- "Adjust" OPENS ALL VISIBLE PILLS' CORRECTORS AT ONCE (Step 6.3, a documented interpretation): CANON/PIPELINE.md name the Adjust control's existence but not its behavior; the screenshot shows it as a single button next to the summary line, distinct from clicking an individual pill (which opens just that one's corrector). Chosen as a bulk-edit toggle (open all / close all) so it has real, distinct behavior rather than duplicating a single pill click. A future step with clearer source material may redefine it.
- PANEL-LEVEL × vs PER-PILL ×: BOTH BUILT, DIFFERENT SCOPES (Step 6.3): the screenshot shows one visible × at the STATE DETECTION header (dismisses the whole panel for this turn); PIPELINE.md Feature 5's own text says "Pills are dismissible (X)" per pill. Read as non-contradictory (the screenshot doesn't show a hover-only per-pill affordance, it just isn't visible in one static frame) rather than the text/image actually disagreeing — same CANON precedence rule as Step 1.5's logout control (only overrides the image when they truly conflict, not when the image is merely silent). Built both: StateDetectionPanel's header × hides the whole panel; each StatePill's own × dismisses just that pill.
- stateCorrections IS A NEW ACCOUNT-STORE FIELD, NOT learnedPreferences (Step 6.4): STORE-CONTRACT.md's learnedPreferences row provisionally named "correction learning 6.4" as a candidate owner, but that field's routing/technique Record<string,unknown> bags are earmarked for the Steps 10.1-10.2 RATINGS-driven rule-refinement loop (PIPELINE.md LEARNING LOOP: "low ratings + 'too verbose' reduce Detailed") — a genuinely different concept (feedback-on-answers vs. pill-value corrections) with no natural shape for structured {dimension,from,to,timestamp} records. Added a proper sibling field instead, per STORE-CONTRACT's own ADD rule; the provisional row's mention was treated as an anticipatory placeholder in an admittedly-provisional shape, not a locked decision — STORE-CONTRACT.md logs this explicitly rather than silently diverging.
- "FOR A GIVEN STATE" READ AS PER (DIMENSION, CORRECTED-TO-VALUE) PAIR (Step 6.4): CANON/PIPELINE.md's "15+ corrections for a given state" doesn't specify whether that's per-dimension or per-value. Chosen: per (dimension, value) pair, because a bare per-dimension count ("emotion gets corrected a lot") gives detection nothing to adapt WITH, whereas "corrected to frustrated 15+ times" is directly actionable (lean toward frustrated when ambiguous). When 2+ values for the same dimension both cross threshold, highest count wins, ties broken by most-recent — reasoned as the best current read of what's actually true for this user.
- ADAPTATION IS A PROMPT ADDENDUM (WEIGHTED HINT), NEVER A SILENT OVERRIDE (Step 6.4): buildAdaptationNote() appends a short note to DETECTION_SYSTEM_PROMPT telling the model "this user has repeatedly corrected X to Y... but still choose differently if THIS message clearly signals otherwise." Rejected post-hoc overriding the model's raw classification with the learned value whenever confidence is low — that would silently substitute a stale historical pattern for what the CURRENT message actually says, risking the exact "black box" CONVENTIONS rule 6 forbids. The model still reasons over every message; the hint only tips genuinely ambiguous reads.
- DetectOptions.adaptationNote IS ADDITIVE, DETECT.TS's SEAM UNCHANGED (Step 6.4): a second touch of a Step 6.1 file, but purely additive (one new optional field, defaults to appending nothing) — every existing Step 6.1/6.2 caller/test is byte-for-byte unaffected (verified: all 34 prior detection tests still pass unmodified). Chosen over a second detectState()-like function or a wrapper, since the adaptation is conceptually part of "what system prompt does this call use," the same seam the function already owns.
- CORRECTION RECORDING HOOKS INTO THE EXISTING handleCorrectState CALL SITE, AS 6.3's OWN PARKED NOTE PREDICTED (Step 6.4): no second, parallel correction-capture path was built — the same CenterColumn function that makes a correction visible (6.3) now also calls recordStateCorrection, guarded on the corrected value actually differing from what was detected (a no-op re-click of the already-correct value isn't a "correction").
- STATE BUS READS session.statePills, NOT THIS TURN'S OWN IN-FLIGHT DETECTION (Step 6.5): PIPELINE.md's RESOLVED note is explicit that detection runs "alongside the translation call," i.e. in PARALLEL with the pipeline, not before it — by the time Stage 3/4 need a technique/tone hint, THIS message's own detectState() call may not have resolved yet. Rather than delay the pipeline to wait for it (reintroducing the sequential wait the RESOLVED note explicitly rejected), deriveStateFeeds() reads whatever session.statePills CURRENTLY holds — usually the prior turn's detected state, occasionally this turn's if detection happens to resolve first. This is a deliberate, documented trade-off (logged here so a future session doesn't "fix" it into a synchronization bug): the state bus is about the user's ONGOING state, not a hard per-message guarantee, and the first message of any session correctly gets no state influence at all (statePills starts all-null).
- STATE BUS OPERATES ON StatePills, A SEPARATE ENTRY POINT FROM impacts.ts's StateDetectionResult FUNCTIONS (Step 6.5): recommendedDirectness/suggestedTechniques/impactsFor (impacts.ts, Step 6.2) take the confidence-aware StateDetectionResult (used by 6.3's local rich `detection` state and any future confidence-aware consumer); stateBus.ts's deriveStateFeeds takes the confidence-blind StatePills (the only source synchronously available at pipeline-submit time — see the point above). Both read the SAME STATE_IMPACTS table, so there's no duplicated mapping data, just two shaped entry points for two different callers' available inputs.
- DIRECTNESS AUTO-RECOMMENDATION IS VISIBLE-AND-APPLIED-ON-CLICK, NEVER SILENT (Step 6.5): resolves the ambiguity Step 4.4's own PARKED note flagged ("must never silently override a manual pick... surface it as a suggestion"). CenterColumn computes the suggestion and compares it to the CURRENT session.directness; StateDetectionPanel shows it only when they differ, with an explicit Apply button wired to the existing setDirectness action — CenterColumn itself never calls setDirectness automatically. Considered auto-applying on first detection each session (tracking a new "manually touched" flag) but rejected the added store-state complexity for a case CANON never explicitly asked for.
- TRANSPARENCY-CARD CONSUMER: DATA SEAM ONLY, NO UI (Step 6.5, a deliberate scope boundary): deriveStateFeeds().transparency is real, tested, directly importable — but Step 8.2 (Transparency Details) owns the actual card. Threading this data through telemetry or ConversationMessage now would mean guessing at 8.2's eventual data-shape needs and touching two more already-built files for a consumer that doesn't exist yet; the same "build the seam now, the owning step consumes it" pattern already used for recommendDirectnessFromEmotion (unwired from Step 4.4 to Step 6.5) and RatingRow's onRate (unwired from 5.1 to 8.1).
- ORCHESTRATOR.TS TOUCHED A 4TH TIME, BUT THIS IS THE STEP THAT SHOULD (Step 6.5): the standing caution against repeated touches (explicit at Step 5.4's telemetry decision) was about NOT rebuilding/extending orchestrator.ts for an UNRELATED concern. Wiring "technique selection" and "answer tone" — two of PIPELINE.md's four NAMED state-bus consumers — is squarely what Step 6.5 exists to do; the additions are purely additive optional PipelineDeps fields, and the full existing orchestrator/composition/technique test suites were re-run and pass unmodified, confirming zero regression to the already-audited flow.
- PDF/IMAGE CONTENT IS AN HONEST PLACEHOLDER, NEVER GARBLED BINARY-AS-TEXT (Step 7.1): no PDF-text-extraction library is installed (adding one is a CONVENTIONS rule-3 DECISIONS-worthy call this step didn't make, since CANON doesn't explicitly ask for PDF extraction the way it explicitly asks for image OCR); calling `file.text()` on a PDF/image would silently produce mojibake that could get fed into a translation/composition prompt as if it were real content — actively worse than admitting the gap. readFileAsContextItem stores a clearly-labeled placeholder string instead, naming the file and the reason, so the gap is visible everywhere the content is displayed (never silent). A test explicitly asserts the raw file bytes never appear in the stored content.
- FILE VALIDATION CHECKS EXTENSION FIRST, MIME TYPE SECOND (Step 7.1): browsers (notably Windows) report unreliable or empty MIME types for some file kinds (`.csv` frequently arrives as `text/plain` or even `""`) — extension is the more consistent signal across OS/browser combinations, so isAcceptedFileType/isImageFile check it first and only fall back to `type.startsWith("image/")` for images not caught by the fixed extension list (covering formats like `.heic` the extension list doesn't enumerate).
- SESSION BYTE TOTAL ACCUMULATES ACROSS A BATCH, COMPUTED BY THE CALLER PER-CALL (Step 7.1): uploadFiles() takes `currentSessionBytes` as a plain number argument (not a store read itself) and accumulates it internally as files in the SAME batch are accepted — keeping services/context/ store-agnostic (same layering as every other service in this build; AttachContextControls.tsx is where the actual `session.context` read happens). This correctly enforces the 50MB cap across a multi-file picker selection even when no individual file exceeds the 10MB per-file cap.
- ~~"Attach ▾" OPENS A FILE PICKER DIRECTLY, NOT A DROPDOWN MENU (Step 7.1, a documented interpretation)...~~ SUPERSEDED at Step 7.3 — Attach is now the real popover menu this note predicted a future step would need; see the Step 7.3 DECISIONS entry below. "Context ›" is still untouched, still reserved for Step 7.5's Context Snapshot panel.
- TESSERACT.JS ADDED AS A NEW DEPENDENCY (CONVENTIONS RULE 3), THE STANDARD BROWSER-OCR CHOICE (Step 7.2): CANON Feature 6 explicitly asks for OCR on images and this step explicitly asks for worker-based OCR — no OCR capability existed anywhere in this build before, so SOME library was unavoidable. tesseract.js is the de facto standard for client-side JS OCR (WASM-based, actively maintained, MIT licensed), and its `createWorker()` API is inherently Web Worker-based — satisfying "run OCR in a web worker" by using the library as designed, not by wrapping it in a second, redundant hand-rolled Worker. `npm view` confirmed registry access works in this sandbox (only live API/model calls are network-blocked here, not package installation) — installed at ^7.0.0.
- NO SECOND HAND-ROLLED WEB WORKER WAS BUILT (Step 7.2): the step's instruction says "run OCR in a web worker so the UI never blocks" — tesseract.js's createWorker() already IS that worker; its recognize() call does the heavy WASM recognition off the main thread by design. Building an additional custom Worker wrapper around an API that's already worker-based would duplicate the library's own correct behavior for no benefit — flagged explicitly here in case a future session assumes worker-wiring is still missing and tries to add it.
- OCR IS BEST-EFFORT, ITS FAILURE NEVER CRASHES OR STALLS AN UPLOAD (Step 7.2): a failed recognize() call (worker init failure, unsupported image, or — the standing sandbox reality — a blocked network fetch of tesseract's WASM/language assets) is CAUGHT inside extractImageContent and turned into the same style of honest placeholder Step 7.1 already used for PDFs, never propagated as a thrown error up through uploadFiles/AttachContextControls. Matches the "never block or crash the answer pipeline running beside it" posture Step 6.1 established for state detection — OCR is the same category of best-effort side enhancement, not a required step in the upload flow.
- ONE SHARED, MODULE-LEVEL OCR CLIENT, NOT ONE PER COMPONENT INSTANCE OR PER IMAGE (Step 7.2): AttachContextControls.tsx creates `sharedOcrClient` lazily at module scope (mirroring CenterColumn.tsx's module-level `const client = createProxyClient()` precedent from Step 5.2) — a fresh tesseract worker per image would repeatedly re-pay the WASM+language-data load cost; a fresh one per component remount would leak workers. ocr.ts's own `getWorker()` caching would actually prevent true duplication either way, but keeping the client itself singleton at the call site is the clearer, more obviously-correct version of the same guarantee.
- A NEW SERVERLESS ENDPOINT (api/fetch-url.ts), NOT A SECOND RESPONSIBILITY BOLTED ONTO api/proxy.ts (Step 7.3): api/proxy.ts (Step 1.10) is specifically shaped for the Anthropic Messages API (API key injection, SSE passthrough, model/message validation) — repurposing it to ALSO fetch arbitrary user-supplied URLs would conflate two genuinely different concerns (an authenticated LLM call vs. an anonymous generic web fetch) into one function with two incompatible response shapes and security postures (one needs a secret key server-side, the other explicitly must never see one). A new, narrowly-scoped endpoint mirrors proxyHandler.ts's exact architecture (platform-agnostic Web Fetch handler, fetchImpl-injectable, thin deploy wrapper outside src/) — same pattern, different job, not a copy-paste of the same job.
- HTML EXTRACTION RUNS CLIENT-SIDE VIA DOMParser, NOT SERVER-SIDE (Step 7.3): the proxy's only job is fetching and returning raw content — no HTML parsing happens there. Edge/serverless runtimes (Vercel Edge, Cloudflare Workers) generally have NO DOM implementation, so server-side extraction would need a new parsing dependency (CONVENTIONS rule 3) for something the browser already does for free via the native DOMParser API. Kept the proxy minimal and generic (fetch a URL, return its body) — the same "thin, generic layer" philosophy api/proxy.ts already established — and put the actual "readable text" logic where the DOM already exists.
- SSRF GUARD IS A FIRST-LAYER LITERAL-ADDRESS CHECK, EXPLICITLY NOT EXHAUSTIVE (Step 7.3): isBlockedUrl() rejects non-http(s) protocols, localhost/loopback, and the private/link-local IPv4 ranges (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16, 169.254/16 — covering cloud metadata endpoints like 169.254.169.254) by inspecting the URL's literal hostname before the server-side fetch dials out. This is a genuine, proportionate security measure for a "server fetches a URL the user controls" feature (a real SSRF vector, worth taking seriously per the standing "don't introduce OWASP-class vulnerabilities" instruction) — but it can't catch DNS rebinding (a public hostname resolving to a private IP only at fetch time, after this check already passed), which needs platform-level network policy to fully close. Logged honestly as a first layer, not a complete SSRF solution.
- A FETCHED PAGE IS BUDGETED LIKE AN UPLOADED FILE, REUSING STEP 7.1's CONSTANTS (Step 7.3): urlFetchHandler.ts and urlContext.ts both import MAX_FILE_BYTES/MAX_SESSION_BYTES from fileValidation.ts rather than defining separate URL-specific limits. CANON's "10MB per file, 50MB per session" doesn't literally mention URLs, but the SESSION cap's whole purpose is bounding TOTAL loaded context regardless of source — treating a fetched page as anything other than "one more item against the same budget" would let URL-pasting bypass the very limit CANON sets. Enforced at TWO layers: the proxy rejects (413) an oversized page before ever returning it to the browser (protecting the serverless function itself), and the client rejects an oversized EXTRACTED text against the remaining session budget (protecting the store).
- "Attach ▾" IS NOW A REAL POPOVER MENU, REUSING THE STEP 1.9 KEYBOARD FRAMEWORK (Step 7.3): extends Step 7.1's single-action button into a two-option menu (Upload a file / Paste a URL) using the exact same useDismissableLayer + manual outside-click pattern TechniqueDropdown (4.5) and StateDetectionPanel (6.3) already established — no new interaction pattern invented. File upload's own logic (uploadFiles, OCR, rejections) is completely untouched, just re-triggered from inside the popover; this is additive UI restructuring, not a rewrite of Step 7.1/7.2's behavior.
- session.variables IS A NEW SIBLING FIELD, REUSING THE ACCOUNT STORE's EXISTING SavedVariables TYPE (Step 7.4): the account store already had `variables: SavedVariables` + setVariable/removeVariable (Step 1.7) since CANON's STORES section names "explicitly-saved variables" as an account-store item. Step 7.4 adds the MATCHING session-store field (same type, mirrored action names setSessionVariable/removeSessionVariable) rather than inventing a different shape — "session store by default, explicitly savable to the account store" reads most naturally as two stores holding the SAME kind of data, one ephemeral one persisted, not two different data shapes for one concept.
- UNMATCHED $name IS LEFT AS-IS, NEVER BLANKED (Step 7.4): substituteVariables() only replaces a $token when the name has a defined value; an undefined variable's $token passes through the text completely unchanged. Rejected silently replacing an unknown variable with an empty string, which would erase text the user deliberately typed and could change the actual meaning of their request — the same "never fabricate, never silently drop content" posture translate()/uploadFiles()/urlContext.ts already established. A future step wanting to WARN the user about an unresolved variable (rather than just leaving it inert) can build that without changing this function's contract.
- SUBSTITUTION RUNS IN Composer.tsx, NOT INSIDE buildTranslateAskRequest OR THE ORCHESTRATOR (Step 7.4): buildTranslateAskRequest (Step 5.0) is documented as "pure... no trimming/validation beyond what's needed" — adding a store-dependent side-concern (variable lookup) there would break that purity and its own established contract. orchestrator.ts (already touched 4 times: 5.2/5.3/5.4/6.5) was avoided again for an UNRELATED concern, consistent with the standing caution from Step 5.4. Composer.tsx already reads session store fields directly for its own concerns (draftInput, model, directness, techniques, context) — reading two more (session + account variables) to substitute BEFORE building the request is the natural, minimal extension of a file already shaped for exactly this kind of "read stores, transform, then call the pure builder" job.
- REFINE SUBSTITUTES ONLY THE NEW ADDITION, NOT THE ALREADY-SUBSTITUTED ORIGINAL (Step 7.4): CenterColumn.tsx's handleRefine runs substituteVariables on refinedInput only, then concatenates it after the (already-substituted, from Composer) lastRawRef.current. Re-substituting the original text would be redundant at best; at worst, if a variable's OWN value happened to contain a literal "$othername" pattern, a second substitution pass could resolve it unexpectedly — an edge case avoided by only ever substituting genuinely fresh, never-yet-substituted user input.
- "CREATE A VARIABLE" IS A THIRD ROW IN THE EXISTING Attach ▾ POPOVER, NOT A NEW UI SURFACE (Step 7.4): CANON Feature 6 lists "create variables" as one of four sibling context-actions (upload/paste-text/paste-URL/variables) — Steps 7.1 and 7.3 already put the first two in this SAME popover, so Step 7.4 extends it a third time rather than inventing a separate entry point. The "Quick Tools" grid (CANON Feature 12) also names a dedicated "Variables" button, but that's explicitly Step 9.6's surface (a different, later step) — this popover form is the minimal REAL creator this step needs, not a preview of 9.6's fuller management UI.
- "PASTE TEXT" (CANON Feature 6's fourth context-action) HAS NO STEP THAT CLAIMS IT (Step 7.4, first flagged here): upload/URL/variables now each have a real entry point in the Attach popover (Steps 7.1/7.3/7.4); "paste a block of text as context" does not appear as any Phase-7 step's explicit job in the plan read so far. Not built here since Step 7.4 is specifically "Variables" — flagged for Step 7.5 or a future gap-filling step to claim explicitly rather than silently skip.
- CONTEXT SNAPSHOT READS TWO STORE SOURCES AT RENDER TIME, NEVER DUPLICATES VARIABLES INTO session.context (Step 7.5): considered making variable creation (Step 7.4) ALSO push a synthetic ContextItem(kind:"variable") into session.context, so the panel would only need to read ONE array. Rejected: that would create two sources of truth for one concept (a variable's value living in BOTH session.variables AND a shadow ContextItem), needing active sync on every create/remove or risking the two drifting apart. buildSnapshotItems() reads session.context and session.variables independently and merges them into one display list at render time instead — session.variables stays the sole store of record for variables, matching Step 7.4's own design.
- REMOVING A VARIABLE FROM THE SNAPSHOT ONLY REMOVES THE SESSION COPY, NEVER THE ACCOUNT-SAVED ONE (Step 7.5): ContextSnapshotPanel's remove × calls removeSessionVariable, never accountStore's removeVariable, even for a variable that was ALSO explicitly saved to the account store (Step 7.4's checkbox). CANON frames the Context Snapshot as SESSION-scoped ("Context persists across questions in the session"); permanently deleting an account-saved variable is a different, more consequential action this panel doesn't expose — likely belongs to the Quick Tools "Variables" management surface (Step 9.6). A user who wants the persisted copy gone too needs that future surface, not this one.
- CONTEXT SNAPSHOT IS ALWAYS EXPANDED, NOT WRAPPED IN REAL ACCORDION MECHANICS (Step 7.5): CANON's LAYOUT names Context Snapshot as one of six "revolving-door" accordion sections (Feature 12), but that collapse/expand behavior is explicitly Step 9.5's own job ("Revolving-door accordions"). Building a one-off expand/collapse interaction here would risk inventing something 9.5 then has to rebuild around, or worse, disagree with. Rendered as a plain always-visible GlassPanel instead — same "real content now, the fancier interactive shell is a later step's job" precedent as the State Detection panel (Step 6.3). The panel also renders unconditionally rather than behind a visibility toggle, which is correct-not-negligent specifically because Context Snapshot's CANON default is ON (Step 9.4 hasn't built the toggle yet, but there's nothing to hide until it does) — the opposite situation from Quick Tools (default OFF, Step 1.5's placeholder correctly stays hidden).
- ONLY THE Context Snapshot PORTION OF THE Step 1.5 PLACEHOLDER WAS REPLACED (Step 7.5): AppShell.tsx's right column had one combined "accordion stack placeholder" GlassPanel naming all six sections. Split it: ContextSnapshotPanel takes over for real, a smaller placeholder GlassPanel keeps naming the other five (Recent Sessions, Recent Activity, Token Usage, Model Status, Active Session) — those remain genuinely unbuilt, still Steps 9.4-9.6's job, not silently dropped from the placeholder text.
- RATING IS DENORMALIZED ONTO ConversationMessage AND ALSO KEPT AS A DURABLE Rating RECORD, WRITTEN TOGETHER (Step 8.1): same split as Step 7.4/7.5's variables — ConversationMessage.ratingStars/ratingComment (sessionStore) exists purely so the UI can render "you already rated this N stars" straight off the message being displayed, with no cross-store lookup; accountStore.ratings is the durable, cross-session, learning-loop-consumable record CANON's STORES section and PIPELINE.md's learning loop actually need. Every save path calls both sessionStore.setMessageRating and accountStore.setRating in the same handler — never one without the other — so the two can't drift.
- accountStore GAINED setRating AS A NEW UPSERT ACTION; THE Step 1.7 addRating WAS LEFT UNTOUCHED, NOT REPURPOSED (Step 8.1): addRating is a pure append with zero consumers anywhere in the app (confirmed by a repo-wide grep before touching anything) — repurposing it to upsert-by-messageId would have been a silent behavior change to an existing public action for no benefit. setRating is new, additive, and is what real UI code calls; addRating remains available/unused, same as it was after Step 1.7.
- setMessageRating's comment PARAMETER IS OPTIONAL AND MEANS "DON'T TOUCH," NOT "CLEAR" (Step 8.1): `setMessageRating(id, stars)` (comment omitted → undefined) preserves any existing ratingComment — a star-only re-rate can't silently erase a comment the user already wrote. An explicit `setMessageRating(id, stars, "")` DOES clear it. This distinction (undefined = don't touch vs. "" = explicit clear) is why the store implementation conditionally spreads the comment field rather than always assigning it.
- THE "WHAT COULD BE BETTER?" COMMENT FIELD IS PROGRESSIVELY DISCLOSED, NOT ALWAYS VISIBLE (Step 8.1): re-checked the screenshot specifically for this step — the only rating UI ever shown is 5 empty stars + a download icon, no comment field in any frame. Read alongside CANON's ADHD "no dense info walls" rule, RatingRow now renders the textarea only once `stars !== undefined` (a star has been picked), matching both the literal screenshot and the general disclosure pattern already used for PillCorrector (6.3) and the URL-paste/variable-creation rows in the Attach popover (7.3/7.4).
- RatingRow WAS REWRITTEN FROM LOCAL useState TO A FULLY CONTROLLED COMPONENT (Step 8.1): Step 5.1's original RatingRow tracked its own `rating` in local state, which meant a reload (or even just re-rendering with a different message) could never show a previously-saved star count. Now `stars`/`comment` are props sourced from `message.ratingStars`/`message.ratingComment`; the component keeps only a local `commentDraft` (for in-progress typing before blur-save) synced to the `comment` prop via an effect. This is the same "store owns truth, component is a thin controlled view" shape every other store-backed control in this app already uses (dropdowns, pills, variables).
- ROUTING/TECHNIQUE/OVERALL CONFIDENCE ARE DERIVED, NOT MEASURED (Step 8.2): CANON Feature 8 names four Confidence sub-card values — translation, routing, technique, overall — but only translation confidence is ever actually computed anywhere in this build (Stage 1's 0-100 score). routing.js and autoDetect.ts are deterministic rule engines with no analogous "how sure am I" number of their own. New `services/telemetry/confidence.ts`'s `deriveConfidenceBreakdown()` (pure, tested) fills the other three from facts telemetry already records, not a second opinion invented independently of what the pipeline decided: routing confidence restates CONFIDENCE_COUPLING.md's own translation-confidence bands as a number (80+ -> 95, 60-79 -> 75, <60 -> 50 — the doc already says routing's trust in its own decision is a direct function of translation confidence, this is that rule as a display value, not a new one). Technique confidence is 100 for an explicit manual stack (nothing to be uncertain about), 90 for auto-detect when a real signal matched a technique, 65 when nothing beat the bare Socratic default baseline (autoDetect.ts's own low-signal fallback case). Overall is the MINIMUM of whichever are present, not an average — CONFIDENCE_COUPLING.md's cost-asymmetry stance is that a shaky layer anywhere should show up, not get smoothed away. A future step reading this differently should treat it as a documented interpretation, not a settled spec value, since CANON itself doesn't define these three numbers.
- TelemetryEntry GAINED scope/thinkingApplied/techniqueReasoning/techniqueMode/techniqueSignalMatched, ALL ADDITIVE (Step 8.2): needed for the Routing sub-card ("scope," "whether extended thinking was applied") and Techniques sub-card ("which applied and why") plus the confidence derivation above. `TechniqueSelection` (autoDetect.ts) gained a `mode: "manual" | "auto-detect"` field the same way — the orchestrator's manual branch and autoDetectTechniques() were the only two places that construct one, both updated; no caller was doing exhaustive-object comparisons on the old shape (checked: tests assert individual fields, never `toEqual` the whole selection). PIPELINE-CONTRACT.md's Stage 3 return-shape line and Telemetry fields table both updated.
- TRANSPARENCY DETAILS IS A REAL, WORKING EXPAND/COLLAPSE TOGGLE, NOT A PLACEHOLDER LIKE Context Snapshot's WAS (Step 8.2): re-checked Step 9.5's own text ("Revolving-door accordions... Recent Sessions, Context Snapshot, Recent Activity, Token Usage, Model Status, Active Session") — it names a specific six-panel right-column list. TRANSPARENCY DETAILS is a different control entirely, in the composer footer, not on that list, so building its own real toggle here doesn't risk contradicting or duplicating Step 9.5's job the way inventing accordion behavior for Context Snapshot would have (Step 7.5's own stated reason for staying always-expanded). Rendered in Composer.tsx below ControlRow inside a new `.composer__footer-row` wrapper div — MULTI-AI ACTIONS (Step 8.3, OPUS, not built this session) is this row's other, still-missing half; the wrapper exists so 8.3 can slot in beside it without restructuring.
- THIS SESSION HAD NO NPM REGISTRY ACCESS (Step 8.2/8.4/8.5): `npm install` failed with a 403 from registry.npmjs.org itself (`x-deny-reason: host_not_allowed`, confirmed via a direct curl, not a proxy misconfiguration — this environment's own network policy doesn't allow it) — `node_modules` was never installed, so `tsc -b`/`vite build`/`oxlint`/`vitest` could not be run at all in this session, for any of the three steps below. This is a strictly worse gap than the standing "no live API network" residual every step since 1.10 has logged: those steps could still typecheck/build/lint/test, just not reach the real proxy. Every change in this session was instead reviewed by hand line-by-line (types traced, imports checked against actual exports, test assertions matched against actual object shapes) with the same care as writing it in the first place, but that is not a substitute for a real compiler/linter/test run. See PARKED — the very first thing a networked session should do is run the full verify suite (`npm install && npm run build && npm run lint && npx vitest run`) against everything this session touched before trusting it further.
- DebateTranscript IS A NEW, PROVIDER-AGNOSTIC CONTRACT INVENTED AHEAD OF ITS OWN PRODUCER (Step 8.4): Step 8.3 (Debate mode) is the step that will actually run a debate and populate one of these; it wasn't built this session (OPUS-tagged). Consensus/Synthesis only need SOME finished transcript to exist, not how it got produced, so this step defines the minimal shape (question/claudeText/partnerLabel/partnerText) rather than waiting. If Step 8.3 lands with a differently-shaped debate result (e.g. it wants to carry the partner's raw api string too, or multiple exchange turns instead of one answer each), that step should adapt to or extend this shape rather than inventing a second, since Consensus/Synthesis are already built against it — flagged in PARKED either way.
- CONSENSUS AND SYNTHESIS SCHEMAS ARE STRICT, NOT TOLERANT LIKE TRANSLATION'S (Step 8.4): translation's schema treats `translatedPrompt` as the one load-bearing field and is lenient about everything else (confidence clamps, unknown gaps drop silently) because the OTHER fields are secondary detail. Here, every field (disagreement/commonGround/unifiedView; refinedAnswer) IS the entire deliverable PIPELINE.md names — a reply silently missing "unified view" isn't a lesser-quality result, it's not a Consensus result at all — so a missing/blank required field throws (MultiAiSchemaError) and the caller surfaces it as outcome "error," same as a transport failure, rather than rendering a hole where a section should be.
- CONSENSUS/SYNTHESIS ARE BUILT STANDALONE, NOT WIRED TO ANY TRIGGER (Step 8.4): there is no Multi-AI Actions entry point in the UI yet (that control, alongside Debate mode itself, is Step 8.3's job) and no real DebateTranscript is ever produced yet either. Wiring a call site now would mean inventing both the trigger AND fabricating a way to get a transcript, well outside this step's own scope ("Build Consensus and Synthesis... OUTPUT: both modes"). Same "service built, then wired" precedent as Step 6.1's Detection architecture (standalone + stub-tested, not wired into the pipeline/UI at that step) — Step 8.3 (or whichever step ends up owning the Multi-AI Actions container) should call runConsensus/runSynthesis with a real transcript and mount ConsensusView/SynthesisView, managing their own loading/error state the way CenterColumn already does for detectState/observePipeline.
- ConversationMessage GAINED telemetryId/statePills SPECIFICALLY TO MAKE "TRANSPARENCY"/"STATE PILLS" EXPORTABLE PER HISTORICAL MESSAGE, NOT JUST THE LATEST ONE (Step 8.5): the Transparency card (Step 8.2) only ever shows the LATEST telemetry entry via useLatestTelemetry — fine for a live "what did the app just decide" control, but Feature 10's download modal is explicitly per-ANSWER ("pick content... for THIS answer"), and neither telemetry nor state pills were ever linked to a specific message before this. telemetryId is a LOOKUP KEY (services/telemetry's log, in-memory/bounded), not a copy — deliberately, since copying the whole breakdown onto every message would duplicate data that's already sitting in the telemetry log, and a lookup miss (old message, log rolled over) is a legitimate, already-established kind of gap in this build (same shape as a stale/rolled-off entry anywhere else telemetry is read). statePills is a snapshot (there's nothing to look up — session.statePills is overwritten every turn, not logged anywhere) — best-effort, since detection runs in parallel and may not have resolved THIS turn's reading by the time the answer completes (an existing, already-logged tension from Step 6.5, not a new one introduced here).
- PDF EXPORT IS A HAND-WRITTEN MINIMAL WRITER, NOT A LIBRARY (Step 8.5): CANON Feature 10 requires PDF as one of exactly four formats; no PDF library exists in this build and this session could not install one to verify it (no npm registry access at all, see Step 8.2 DECISIONS) — trusting an unverified new binary dependency felt worse than a small, fully-understood, hand-verified implementation. The writer is deliberately minimal (no compression, no embedded/custom fonts, character-count text wrapping rather than font-metric-aware) and reuses renderMarkdown()'s output as its page text rather than a second content-formatting path. Non-Latin-1 text becomes "?" (base-14 Helvetica has no encoding for it) — a real, acknowledged limitation of the PDF format specifically; Markdown/HTML/JSON keep full Unicode. If a later networked step wants proper typography, embedded fonts, or full Unicode in PDF, replacing this with a real library (pdf-lib or similar, logged as a DECISIONS line same as tesseract.js was at Step 7.2) is the right move — this hand-written version is meant to satisfy CANON's requirement honestly today, not to be defended forever.
- "COPY TO CLIPBOARD" IS DISABLED FOR THE PDF FORMAT (Step 8.5): CANON says "download or copy to clipboard" without saying every format supports both. Markdown/HTML/JSON are text — clipboard-native. PDF is binary; there is no sensible "copy this PDF as text" action, so the modal disables that button specifically when format is PDF rather than copying garbled bytes-as-text or silently no-op'ing on click.
- VERIFICATION PASS FOUND AND FIXED TWO REAL BUGS FROM THE NPM-BLOCKED Step 8.2/8.4/8.5 SESSION: (1) `Uint8Array`/`Blob` TYPE ERROR (`src/services/export/DownloadModal.tsx:107`, `TS2322`): `pdf.ts`'s `buildPdfBytes` and `renderPdf.ts`'s `renderPdf` both declared their return type as the bare `Uint8Array`, which TypeScript 6's generic-typed-array defaults widen to `Uint8Array<ArrayBufferLike>` (includes `SharedArrayBuffer`) — not assignable to `Blob`'s `BlobPart`, which wants the narrower `ArrayBufferView<ArrayBuffer>`. Confirmed against `node_modules/typescript/lib/lib.dom.d.ts` that `TextEncoder.prototype.encode()` is itself already declared to return `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>` — the runtime value was always correctly, exclusively ArrayBuffer-backed (`TextEncoder` never produces a `SharedArrayBuffer`-backed view); only the two functions' own return-type annotations were too loose. Fixed by tightening both to `Uint8Array<ArrayBuffer>` — a type-precision correction, not a cast/`as any`, and no runtime buffer copy/slice was needed since the actual value already had the right shape. (2) STALE TEST STRING (`buildExportData.test.ts`): asserted the technique label `"Chain of Thought"` (spaced); the real, established registry label (`services/techniques/registry.ts`, Step 4.1, consistently used elsewhere — `detection/prompt.ts`, `manualSelection.test.ts`) is `"Chain-of-Thought"` (hyphenated). `buildExportData.ts` itself correctly calls `getTechnique(id).label`, so this was the test's own wrong expectation, not a production bug — fixed the test, left the registry and `buildExportData.ts` untouched. Both bugs slipped past that session's hand-review specifically because they're the kind a compiler/test-runner catches trivially but a careful human read does not (a type-generics default, and a one-character string mismatch) — reinforcing why the "hand-reviewed, not tooling-verified" caveat on Steps 8.2/8.4/8.5 was worth taking seriously rather than treating as a formality.
- OPEN QUESTION 1 RESOLVED BY DIRECT PRODUCT DECISION, NOT FURTHER RESEARCH: two research passes (logged above, now struck through in PARKED) confirmed Home, Dashboard (left-nav), Messages, Archive (as a nav destination), and Customize were genuinely never specified anywhere in the accessible planning history. The person running this build then decided them directly. CANON.md gained a new `## LEFT NAVIGATION` section, placed right after `## LAYOUT` (where the ten item names are first introduced) rather than as a nested subsection, since CANON.md has no precedent for `###`-level headers anywhere — a flat `##` section matches the file's existing style. Decisions: Home is a landing/overview screen (recent activity feed + quick stats), distinct from Translate (the active composer). Dashboard is session statistics, and is explicitly the SAME destination as the Quick Tools "Dashboard" tile from Feature 12 (Step 9.6 and whichever step wires the left-nav item should link both controls to one screen, not build two). Messages is a live/browsable conversation-thread inbox, distinct from Archive. Archive is the view of closed sessions — the screen Feature 11's "Close Session... save and archive" actually writes to; prior research (this file's PARKED, now struck through) had only found that as a plausible bridge, never a stated fact, and this decision confirms it directly. Customize is a real layout control panel (which panels/widgets exist and where they're positioned — right-sidebar sections, Quick Actions row, possibly left-nav item visibility itself), explicitly NOT a theme/settings panel (theme already lives under the gear-icon Settings). Integrations is explicitly logged as NOT YET DEFINED — a decorative placeholder, deliberately not invented here; whichever of 9.6/9.7 builds it should say so out loud rather than fabricate behavior. Resources/Projects/Tasks/Translate were already found via prior research and are simply confirmed as-is, unchanged. DIVERGENCE-BUILD-PROMPTS.md's own OPEN QUESTIONS section was updated to match: Question 1 removed from the numbered list (Question 2 keeps its original number, not renumbered, matching how Question 3's earlier removal didn't renumber the survivors either), with a one-line pointer added to the intro paragraph — the same terse treatment Question 3 got when it resolved.
- QUICK TOOLS TILE CLICK-BEHAVIOR IS DOCUMENTED IN THIS FILE'S OWN EARLIER RESEARCH ENTRY, NOT YET IN CANON.md: the prior research pass found real "click to..." definitions for all six Quick Tools tiles (Router/Techniques/Prompt Library/Variables/Checkpoints/Dashboard) in an old master-spec draft — see the PARKED entry above (now struck through) and the DECISIONS entry logging that research. This CANON.md update added the LEFT NAVIGATION section only, per explicit instruction; it did NOT add those six click-behaviors into Feature 12's Quick Tools text. Whichever step builds 9.6 (Quick Tools grid) should pull them from BUILD-LOG's research record — they're findings, not yet canonized — or the person running this build may want them formally added to CANON.md first, same treatment as this LEFT NAVIGATION section got.
  RESOLVED as of this commit: Feature 12's Quick Tools sentence in CANON.md now carries all six click-behaviors inline, parenthetical style (matching the visibility checkboxes' existing "(ON)"/"(OFF)" annotation pattern in that same sentence) — Router (view detailed routing decision), Techniques (view applied techniques in detail), Prompt Library (save/load prompt templates), Variables (manage context variables), Checkpoints (save/restore conversation states), Dashboard (view session statistics, cross-referenced to LEFT NAVIGATION as the same destination as the left-nav Dashboard item). Whichever step builds 9.6 should now read this straight from CANON.md, not from this research record.
- ArchivedPair (Step 1.7) STAYS UNUSED; A NEW SessionRecord TYPE OWNS SESSION LIFECYCLE INSTEAD (Step 9.1): ArchivedPair's own doc comment already flagged it "provisional... archive/session lifecycle is Steps 9.1-9.2" — checked at this step, and confirmed it's per-Q/A-PAIR shaped ({question, answer, timestamp}), which fits nothing Feature 11 actually asks for (Duplicate Session copies a whole conversation+context+settings; Close Session archives a whole session; Recent Sessions/Archive list whole sessions). Rather than force-fit ArchivedPair into a shape it wasn't built for (or silently repurpose an unused field, same mistake Step 8.1's DECISIONS already warned against for addRating/setRating), added a new `AccountState.sessions: SessionRecord[]` + `addSessionRecord` action. ArchivedPair/archivedPairs/archivePair remain exactly as they were: declared, unused, untouched — a future step is free to actually use them for real per-pair archiving (e.g. a "save just this Q/A" action distinct from whole-session archiving) if that turns out to be a real, separate need.
- newSession() READS "CLEARS... CONTEXT" AS BOTH session.context AND session.variables, AND ALSO CLEARS statePills/draftInput (Step 9.1): CANON's literal text is "clears history and context," naming only two of SessionState's seven fields. Read `context` as the whole of Feature 6's "Context Management" (which explicitly includes "create variables ($name)" as one of its four load-methods) rather than only the `context: ContextItem[]` field by literal name match — clearing files/URLs but leaving variables behind would leave half of "context" intact. `statePills` and `draftInput` are cleared too even though neither is named at all: both belong to the conversation being cleared (detected state ABOUT that conversation; an in-progress, not-yet-submitted thought FOR that conversation), not to "settings" (model/directness/techniques, which explicitly ARE kept). A future step reading "context" more narrowly should treat this as a documented interpretation to revisit, not a silent inconsistency.
- CLOSE SESSION USES resetSession() (FULL RESET, INCLUDING SETTINGS), NOT newSession() (Step 9.1): New Session and Close Session both end the current conversation, but CANON only grants New Session an explicit "keeps settings" carve-out — Close Session's three outcomes (save and archive / discard / archive tagged) read as a harder, more final stop where starting completely fresh (defaults) is more consistent with "closing" than partially fresh. This finally gives Step 1.7's resetSession() its first real caller, seven-ish phases after it was written.
- ONLY "DISCARD" IS GATED BY useConfirmable, NOT NEW SESSION OR EITHER ARCHIVE OPTION (Step 9.1): CANON's ADHD Decisions rule is "only confirm destructive actions." New Session clears the live conversation but CANON states this plainly with no confirm language, unlike Close Session which explicitly enumerates three distinct, deliberate outcomes — read as intentionally lower-friction (same reasoning Step 8.1 used for NOT requiring a rating before an answer is usable). Save-and-Archive and Archive-Tagged both KEEP the conversation (filed to accountStore.sessions) before clearing the live one, so neither actually loses anything. Discard is the one action across all of Session Management that loses the conversation with nothing saved anywhere — the exact shape `useConfirmable` (Step 1.9, unused until now) exists for.
- CLOSE SESSION LIVES UNDER A NEW "More ▾" OVERFLOW POPOVER, NOT ITS OWN TOP-LEVEL BUTTON (Step 9.1): the screenshot's QUICK ACTIONS row shows exactly four named buttons (New Session, Load Template, Saved Prompts, Duplicate Session) plus an unlabeled fifth "•••More ⌃" control — CANON Feature 11 names six actions total (New/Duplicate/Load Template/Saved Prompts/Import/Close), one more than the row has visible slots for, and Import is explicitly Step 9.3's own separate OPUS step (not a Quick Actions button at all, per its own build-prompt text: "Import with its seven paths," no row placement implied). Close Session is the rarer, more consequential of the remaining five, making it the natural fit for the row's one overflow slot. Same reasoning as Attach's "▾" always having implied a real menu eventually (Step 7.1's own DECISIONS).
- accountStore GAINED templates: PromptTemplate[] SEEDED WITH THREE BUILT-IN PRESETS (Step 9.2): CANON Feature 11 names "Load Template" as a feature but never says what should ship inside it, and nothing else in the build creates templates either — an empty picker with no way to add to it would be a dead-end control. Seeded DEFAULT_TEMPLATES (Quick Question / Deep Analysis / Learning Mode) directly in accountStore.ts, all `model: "auto"` specifically so a seed template can never force a paid-tier model onto a free-plan user (ROUTING.md's free/paid gate). A future step is free to replace/retune these three; nothing depends on their exact content, only that `templates` is a `PromptTemplate[]`.
- LOAD TEMPLATE/SAVED PROMPTS BOTH GAINED A "CREATE FROM CURRENT STATE" AFFORDANCE, NOT JUST LOADING (Step 9.2): CANON's own text for both is read-oriented ("pre-populate," "reuse previous questions") and never names a creation action, but a picker that can only ever show the 3 seed templates (or, for Saved Prompts, literally nothing — accountStore.savedPrompts starts empty per Step 1.7) isn't "Saved Prompts" in any real sense. Added "+ Save current settings as template" (LoadTemplateMenu, reads current model/directness/techniques) and "+ Save current question as prompt" (SavedPromptsMenu, reads current draftInput, disabled while the composer is empty) as small in-popover forms — a documented scope extension of what CANON literally lists, not a re-reading of it; flagged here so a later step doesn't assume these are absent.
- LOADING A TEMPLATE OR SAVED PROMPT NEVER CLEARS THE CONVERSATION (Step 9.2): unlike New Session, CANON's own wording for Load Template is purely additive ("pre-populate settings and a starter question") — model/directness/techniques are set outright (a real, intentional overwrite of the current selection — that IS "pre-populate"), template context items are ADDED to session.context (they carry their own ids, so appending can't collide or clobber), and a starterQuestion (if present) replaces draftInput. A saved prompt's text also replaces draftInput. None of the three touch session.conversation.
- LoadTemplateMenu/SavedPromptsMenu DUPLICATE THE SAME POPOVER-MECHANICS BOILERPLATE AS QuickActionsRow'S More POPOVER, ON PURPOSE (Step 9.2): open/close state, useDismissableLayer, and the outside-mousedown-click useEffect are identical in shape across all three (and, checked, in AttachContextControls and TechniqueDropdown too — neither of those two shares a hook either). Extracting a shared `usePopover()` hook was considered and rejected: this repo's established convention for this specific small mechanical pattern is per-component duplication, not abstraction, and introducing the first shared hook for it now would make this step's two new menus the odd ones out relative to every existing popover instead of consistent with them.
- VisibilityMenu REPLACES TopBar's "Settings" BUTTON RATHER THAN ADDING A SEPARATE GEAR CONTROL (Step 9.4): CANON Feature 12 says "a gear dropdown (top right)" and CANON's own LAYOUT line lists the top bar's icons as "gear, bell, help" — TopBar.tsx's pre-existing "Settings" button (Step 1.5) is that gear slot, just inert until now. Replacing it in place (not adding a second gear-looking control) keeps exactly one gear icon in the top bar, matching both CANON's wording and the screenshot's single gear glyph.
- THE "REMAINING FIVE" SIDEBAR PLACEHOLDER IS GATED IN AGGREGATE ("ANY of the five ON"), NOT PER-ITEM (Step 9.4): Recent Sessions/Recent Activity/Token Usage/Model Status/Active Session are still ONE combined GlassPanel (Step 1.5's placeholder, never split) — Step 9.5 ("Revolving-door accordions") is the step that turns this into five real, independently-rendered panels. Until then, gating the combined block on any-of-five-ON is the most honest interim behavior available: it correctly hides the whole block when a user turns off all five, and correctly shows it when any is on, without fabricating five separately-clickable regions that don't exist yet or silently ignoring 5 of the 7 checkboxes' actual effect. Step 9.5 must replace this aggregate gate with 5 individual ones when it splits the panel.
