- setVisibility's SINGLE-KEY TOGGLE PATCH NEEDS AN EXPLICIT TYPE ASSERTION, DOCUMENTED RATHER THAN WORKED AROUND (Step 9.4): `{[key]: !visibility[key]}` where `key: keyof VisibilitySettings` doesn't structurally narrow to `Partial<VisibilitySettings>` the way the identical-looking pattern already works elsewhere in this codebase for `SavedVariables` (accountStore.setVariable) — `SavedVariables` is `Record<string,string>` (an index-signature type, where a computed-key object literal matches structurally), while `VisibilitySettings` is a fixed 7-named-boolean-field interface (no index signature). `as Partial<VisibilitySettings>` is used instead of writing out 7 explicit setter branches — precise, not a widening `as any`, since every field is boolean and `key` is proven to be one of the interface's own keys.
- ROUTER/TECHNIQUES QUICK TOOLS TILES REUSE THE STEP 8.2 SUB-CARDS DIRECTLY, NOT A SECOND COPY (Step 9.6): RoutingSubCard/TechniquesSubCard already take a `TelemetryEntry` and render exactly "detailed routing decision"/"applied techniques in detail" — CANON's own words for these two tiles. Re-deriving that display a second way (a second component reading the same TelemetryEntry differently) would risk the two views drifting out of sync over time; mounting the SAME components a second place, fed by the SAME useLatestTelemetry() hook, cannot drift, by construction.
- PROMPT LIBRARY REUSES LoadTemplateMenu WHOLESALE VIA A NEW renderTrigger PROP (Step 9.6): CANON's Prompt Library tile text ("save/load prompt templates") is word-for-word the same feature as Feature 11's Load Template, which Step 9.2 already fully built (list/load/save/remove, accountStore.templates). Rather than rebuild that logic a second time behind a grid tile (two sources of truth for one feature, exactly what CANON explicitly warns against for the Dashboard tile specifically, and by clear extension applies here too), LoadTemplateMenu gained one new optional prop — `renderTrigger?: (props: {open, onClick}) => ReactNode` — that swaps ONLY the trigger button's appearance; state, popover content, and every store call are unchanged and shared. The composer's QuickActionsRow usage (Step 9.2) is unaffected — `renderTrigger` is optional and defaults to the original GlassButton trigger.
- VARIABLES TILE IS A NEW, SEPARATE POPOVER RATHER THAN REUSING ContextSnapshotPanel OR AttachContextControls (Step 9.6): both already touch session variables, but neither exposes a standalone, mountable "just the variables part." ContextSnapshotPanel (Step 7.5) mixes files/URLs/variables into one combined list with no variables-only view; AttachContextControls' variable-creation form (Step 7.4) is one view deep inside a multi-view Attach popover, not separately extractable without a larger refactor of an already-complex, already-tested component. A small new tile-local popover (list + remove + a name/value add form, calling the exact same setSessionVariable/removeSessionVariable actions those two components already use) was judged lower-risk than refactoring either — same store data, a second real entry point, not a second variables concept or a duplicated data model.
- CHECKPOINTS AND DASHBOARD ARE HONEST PLACEHOLDERS, NOT BUILT FEATURES (Step 9.6): confirmed by checking the ENTIRE DIVERGENCE-BUILD-PROMPTS.md — no step, in any phase, ever mentions "checkpoint," so "save/restore conversation states" is a real gap in the 60-step plan itself, not something this step skipped. Dashboard has a name and a destination rule (CANON: same as the left-nav Dashboard item) but no actual screen anywhere yet — Step 9.7 (left-nav content/routing) hasn't run. Both tiles are real, clickable, keyboard-reachable, and show a neutral, honest "not yet available" message — same posture as LEFT NAVIGATION's Integrations ("decorative placeholder... do not invent behavior for it") — rather than either being disabled/hidden (which would contradict "the grid is 2x3," CANON's own SPOT CHECK line) or faking a destination.

- DEBATE IS SINGLE-ROUND, NOT A MULTI-TURN EXCHANGE (Step 8.3): each side states its case once, simultaneously; there are no rebuttal turns. No spec mandates either shape — CANON Feature 9 says "two AIs argue opposite sides", PIPELINE.md says "two AI streams argue opposite sides in a two-column view", and neither describes an exchange. Single-round is what Step 8.4's already-built, already-tested DebateTranscript can carry with ZERO changes (it holds exactly one text per side), so Consensus and Synthesis kept working untouched — their tests were not modified and still pass. Rejected multi-round: it would have meant widening DebateTranscript to an array of turns, reworking buildTranscriptInput, and re-tuning both 8.4 prompts, all to add a depth no written spec asks for. If a future step wants rebuttals, extend DebateTranscript ADDITIVELY (an optional `rounds` field alongside the existing four) rather than replacing it — the current shape is load-bearing for two already-shipped services.
- THE TWO DEBATE CLIENT SEAMS ARE SEPARATE TYPES SO "NEVER TWO CLAUDE CALLS" IS COMPILE-TIME (Step 8.3): CANON Feature 9's central rule is that Debate "uses two DIFFERENT AI providers, never two Claude calls arguing with itself — same-model debate is known to converge into agreement-theater." Enforcing that by convention (a comment saying "pass different clients") would leave it one careless call site away from silently breaking, and the failure would be invisible: a Claude-vs-Claude debate still renders two columns of plausible text. Instead DebateClaudeClient takes a `model: ModelId` and DebatePartnerClient takes a `partner: DebatePartner`, so they are structurally incompatible and runDebate cannot be handed the same client twice. The roster itself reinforces this by containing no Anthropic entry at all, and PartnerPicker never offers Claude — there is no path through the UI or the types that produces a same-vendor debate.
- CLAUDE DEBATES ON SONNET, NOT OPUS (Step 8.3): DEBATE_CLAUDE_MODEL is claude-sonnet-5. Opus is deliberately reserved as MULTI_AI_RUNTIME_MODEL (Step 8.4) — the model that reads BOTH transcripts afterward and adjudicates for Consensus/Synthesis. A debate side is one argued position, not the adjudication, and Sonnet is the Balanced default this app already routes most real questions to. Also deliberately NOT run through routing.js: ROUTING.md is explicit that debate "is a distinct code path from the Claude-only router above — it does not go through the MODELS table or the complexity scorer," so no complexity scoring, no free/paid gating, and no downgrade note applies to a debate side.
- PARTNER PROXIES PASS THE UPSTREAM STATUS THROUGH BUT NEVER THE UPSTREAM BODY (Step 8.3): a failing provider's error body routinely echoes the request back (OpenAI-family errors quote the offending prompt) and can carry vendor internals; that response goes to the browser, so forwarding it would leak the user's own question and possibly key/account detail through an error path. The STATUS is forwarded (the client's retry logic genuinely needs to tell a transient 429/5xx from a permanent 4xx) with a fixed neutral message body. The same reasoning applies one layer up: runDebate catches each side's real error and shows fixed neutral copy plus a retry, never the raw message — proven by a test asserting a simulated `401 Unauthorized: sk-secret-key-leaked` never reaches the user-facing string.
- FOUR NAMED HANDLER FILES, ONE SHARED CHAT-COMPLETIONS BUILDER (Step 8.3): ROUTING.md names the file pattern "api/proxy-<provider>.ts + a src/services/debate/<provider>Handler.ts" per provider, and all four exist accordingly, each with its own endpoint and its own key env var — which is the part that must genuinely be separate. But OpenAI, xAI, and DeepSeek all deliberately ship the SAME OpenAI-compatible /chat/completions contract, so their request-body and response-parsing code is identical; three byte-identical copies would mean three places to fix one security or parsing bug. That shared part lives in chatCompletions.ts and each handler supplies only its own id/url/key. Google's Gemini genuinely differs (contents/parts, systemInstruction, header auth) and is written out separately rather than contorted into the shared shape. Gemini's key goes in a header, never the URL query string — a URL-embedded key lands in proxy/CDN access logs, which is the exact exposure this proxy layer exists to prevent.
- DEBATE OUTPUT IS NON-STREAMED, DESPITE PIPELINE.md'S WORD "STREAMS" (Step 8.3): PIPELINE.md says "two AI streams argue opposite sides", which reads most naturally as two streams of output (two columns filling in) rather than a token-streaming requirement — CANON's own wording for the same feature is "two AIs argue opposite sides, two-column view", with no mention of streaming. Both sides are single non-streamed completions, and each column renders when its own call returns (they run in parallel, so the user waits for the slower side, not the sum). Real token-streaming would mean implementing and testing four different vendors' SSE formats behind four proxies, with no spec asking for it and no network in this sandbox to verify any of it against. If a future step wants live token streaming per column, the seam is clean: partnerProxy already passes an upstream Response through, so it could stream instead of buffering without changing runDebate's outcome union.
- ONE SIDE RETRIES WITHOUT RE-RUNNING THE OTHER (Step 8.3): MultiAiActions' retry re-runs only the failed side and keeps the surviving side's existing text verbatim, preserving its original stance so a retry can't silently flip which side argues what mid-debate. Re-running both would spend a second call to replace a good argument the user didn't ask to change, and would make the surviving column flicker to different text for no reason. Consensus/Synthesis stay disabled while an outcome is "partial", with a visible note saying why — both are defined by PIPELINE.md as what happens "after a debate", so offering them against half a debate would be offering an action that cannot work.
- ONE SHARED expandedKey STATE, NOT PER-PANEL STATE, MAKES "ONLY ONE OPEN AT ONCE" STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN CONVENTIONAL (Step 9.5): AccordionStack holds a single `AccordionPanelKey | null`, not six independent booleans with manual "close the others" logic scattered across click handlers. With one shared variable, two panels being open simultaneously is not a state a bug could accidentally produce — there is only ever one value. This is the same reasoning Step 8.3 used for typing the two debate clients as incompatible types (make the forbidden state unrepresentable, not just avoided by convention).
- ContextSnapshotPanel WAS RESTRUCTURED INTO ContextSnapshotContent, NOT WRAPPED A SECOND TIME (Step 9.5): Step 7.5 explicitly built Context Snapshot as an always-expanded GlassPanel+title, on record as "waiting on Step 9.5's real accordion mechanic" rather than inventing one. The straightforward-looking alternative — leave ContextSnapshotPanel exactly as it was and have AccordionStack render it inside ANOTHER header+GlassPanel wrapper — was rejected: it would produce a panel-inside-a-panel (double GlassPanel nesting, double title text) and leave Context Snapshot as the one panel whose collapse/expand doesn't match its five siblings' shared shell. Renaming the file (`git mv`) and stripping it to content-only (list/empty-state, no wrapper/title) makes all six panels genuinely uniform. Only the outer shell changed — buildSnapshotItems()/removeContextItem/removeSessionVariable and every CSS class the list itself uses are untouched.
- THE FIVE UNBUILT PANELS GET FIVE SEPARATE HONEST PLACEHOLDER NOTES, NOT ONE COMBINED BLOB (Step 9.5): Step 9.4 combined them into a single GlassPanel with one paragraph naming all five, since it couldn't yet gate them individually. Now that each has its own accordion header and its own visibility gate, keeping one shared block of prose behind five different headers would be confusing (which text belongs to which header?) and wouldn't honestly reflect that each is independently controlled. Same wording repeated per panel ("X isn't built yet — no step in the plan currently owns its content"), not exact strings, plain copy-paste — still no invented content for any of them.
- THE STALE TWO-WAY/THREE-WAY DEBATE BLOCKER NOTE ABOVE THIS ENTRY WAS REPLACED, NOT LEFT IN PLACE (Step 9.5): it predates Step 8.3's completion and the CANON/ROUTING commit that resolved the exact conflict it described, but nothing had gone back to mark it resolved. Since it occupied the "Next step"/"Blocked" position this step's own instructions require editing anyway, leaving stale, actively-contradicted text there (Step 8.3 marked "DO NOT PROCEED" while the STEPS checkbox and its own DECISIONS/PARKED entries show it done) seemed worse than replacing it with an accurate pointer that explicitly names what happened. See WHERE YOU ARE.

- THEME TOGGLE (LIGHT/DARK/AUTO) DOCUMENTED (Pre-Step-10.1): CANON.md Feature 12 already mentioned "gear dropdown" Settings, but LOCKED DECISIONS never named the theme toggle explicitly — V3 screenshot shows dark theme only, making it appear dark-only. Corrected: the three-surface system (Black Marble, Smoked Glass, Blue Marble) applies to both Light and Dark themes, designed and screenshotted early (Divergence_AI_Light_App_Screenshot V1/V2/V3) but never written into CANON until now. Settings includes Light / Dark / Auto toggle (Auto follows OS preference). This is a real, intended feature, not exploration that got superseded. Updated CANON.md LOCKED DECISION 2 and Feature 12's Settings description to reflect it.

- "PASTE TEXT" IS A CONFIRMED-INTENDED BUT UNCLAIMED GAP (Pre-Step-10.1): CANON.md Feature 6 (Context Management) lists four context-actions: upload files, paste text, paste URL, create variables. Upload/URL/Variables are all built (Steps 7.1/7.3/7.4). "Paste text" has never been claimed by any step — flagged as a gap since Step 7.4's BUILD-LOG entry, now confirmed intended via reference images. This is a real, scheduled gap, not a missed requirement. Needs its own small step (or folding into an existing one) before Phase 12, not built now. Explicitly documented here so a future step doesn't mistakenly rebuild it or skip it.

- "CHAT HISTORY" ADDED AS 8TH IMPORT OPTION (Pre-Step-10.1): CANON.md Feature 11's Import line listed seven options (file, URL, previous conversation, variables, context snapshot, saved prompts, template settings). Research confirmed "chat history" is distinct from "previous conversation" — "previous conversation" resumes a session already stored in-app; "chat history" imports an externally exported conversation log (a file import). Added to CANON.md Feature 11's Import line as the 8th option with clarifying note. Step 9.3 (Import system, OPUS-tagged, not yet built) should implement this as one of its seven paths (the count in the feature description was one short; the step's own DIVERGENCE-BUILD-PROMPTS.md says "seven import paths" and ROUTING.md DEBATE PARTNERS confirms that count against the feature list).

- LEARNED TECHNIQUE WEIGHT IS A SIGNED INTEGER NUDGE, NOT A PERCENTAGE (Step 10.2): TechniquePreference.weight moves ±1 per applied proposal (not proportional to the proposal's confidence score) — a simple, auditable unit that's easy to reason about in the audit log ("weight went from -2 to -3 because of this proposal") versus a confidence-weighted fractional delta that would make the audit log's before/after numbers harder to eyeball-verify. Clamped to [-5, 5] (MIN/MAX_TECHNIQUE_WEIGHT, applier.ts) so an account with many months of learning-loop runs can't push a technique to an extreme the auto-detect scorer (whenever it consumes this) would have to treat as an effective ban — CANON "never a black box" reads as "never silently suppress a technique entirely" too.
- APPLIER IS THE WRITE SIDE ONLY; CONSUMPTION INTO AUTO-DETECT SCORING IS UNWIRED (Step 10.2): learnedPreferences.technique is now populated and persisted, but services/techniques/autoDetect.ts's scoring never reads it — the step's own text asks for "applying them to the user's routing and technique preferences in the account store, with an audit log," which is the write side; reading the weight back into live scoring is a further, unlisted step. The natural hook is autoDetect.ts's existing TechniqueHints mechanism (already used for Step 6.5's state-detection nudges — same "+1 weight, still subject to conflicts/deps/the ≤4 cap" shape would fit a learned-preference hint too), but wiring it wasn't invented here. Flagged in PARKED.
- "DETECTION-THRESHOLD" PROPOSALS ARE HANDLED BUT NEVER PRODUCED (Step 10.2): RefinementProposal.type is a union of "technique-weight" | "detection-threshold", but Step 10.1's analyzer.ts only ever emits "technique-weight" — generateProposals() has no code path that produces the other member. applyRefinements() skips any non-"technique-weight" proposal without throwing (matches this codebase's tolerant-parsing posture, e.g. detect.ts's unknown-value handling) rather than assuming the type will never appear — if a future analyzer version starts emitting "detection-threshold" proposals, the applier doesn't need to change to not crash, but it also won't apply them until a future step gives it a real target shape (there's no "detection threshold" concept anywhere else in this codebase to apply one to).
- ONE ATOMIC accountStore ACTION FOR BOTH learnedPreferences AND learningAuditLog (Step 10.2): applyLearningRefinements(updated, newAuditEntries) sets both fields in one `set()` call rather than two separate action calls (setLearnedPreferences then a hypothetical appendAuditEntries) — same "written together, never one without the other" reasoning Step 8.1 used for ConversationMessage's denormalized rating fields plus accountStore.ratings. The pre-existing setLearnedPreferences (Step 1.7, wholesale replace) is left untouched and still exported, not repurposed or removed — it remains available for a future reset/import path that needs to replace the whole structure at once (e.g. Step 9.3's Import "template settings" path, if that ever needs to import learned preferences wholesale, which CANON doesn't currently name).
- FIXED A STALE TEST LEFT BY STEP 9.7, UNRELATED TO THIS STEP'S OWN CHANGES (Step 10.2): running the full suite (not just this step's own new files) surfaced persistence.test.ts's "persisted session state contains only data" test failing — it asserted a hardcoded, exact SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS list that Step 9.7 (Left nav content, immediately prior) extended with `currentScreen` but never updated this test to match. Fixed the test's expected key list; the store itself (sessionStore.ts's actual SESSION_PERSISTED_KEYS) was already correct — the test was stale, not the code. Logged here rather than silently folded into Step 9.7's own (already-closed) BUILD-LOG entry, since this step is what actually discovered and fixed it.
- ALSO FIXED A HOUSEKEEPING MISS FROM THE TWO PRIOR SESSIONS (Step 10.2): Step 9.7's and Step 10.1's own STEPS checkboxes had never been ticked despite both being logged done in WHERE YOU ARE — ticked now, along with 10.2's, same "found and fixed while touching this file" precedent as Step 5.3's own note about 5.2's checkbox.

- BUILT EIGHT IMPORT PATHS, NOT THE STEP TEXT'S SEVEN (Step 9.3): Step 9.3's SPOT CHECK line in DIVERGENCE-BUILD-PROMPTS.md says "the seven import paths" and enumerates seven, omitting chat history. CANON.md Feature 11 names eight. The step text predates the CANON fix made earlier in this build, CANON is authoritative by its own first line, and the DECISIONS entry that made that fix explicitly named Step 9.3 as the implementer — so this followed a logged decision rather than re-deciding a settled question (which is what the step prompt's own BUILD LOG instruction demands). Building eight is also a strict superset of the seven the step asks for: nothing the step wanted was dropped. CORRECTION to that earlier entry while I'm here: its closing parenthetical claimed "ROUTING.md DEBATE PARTNERS confirms that count against the feature list" — that clause is nonsense (ROUTING.md's debate-partner roster has nothing to do with import-path counts) and should be disregarded; the rest of that entry is accurate.
- CHAT HISTORY IS PARSED STRUCTURALLY, NOT PER-VENDOR (Step 9.3): CANON defines this path as "an externally exported conversation log," names no vendor, and gives no format. Building named ChatGPT/Claude/Gemini adapters would be inventing spec — the same trap LEFT NAVIGATION's Integrations entry explicitly warns against ("do not invent behavior for it"). Instead the parser accepts the container and message shapes conversation logs overwhelmingly share (bare array / {messages} / {conversation}; role+content where content may be a string or an array of text blocks) and SKIPS what it can't read, counting skips for the user rather than failing the whole file. A "system" turn is skipped rather than relabelled as user or assistant — importing it as either would misrepresent who said it. If a future step needs a specific vendor's export that this structural reader misses, that's a new adapter alongside this, not a rewrite of it.
- IMPORTED IDS ARE ALWAYS REGENERATED AND CONTEXT BYTES ALWAYS RECOMPUTED (Step 9.3): an import file is untrusted input that lands directly in a persisted store. Trusting its ids risks a collision silently overwriting the user's existing data; trusting its `bytes` field would let a file claiming `bytes: 0` on a megabyte of content walk straight past the 10MB/50MB caps (Step 7.1) that the entire context system depends on — so bytes is recomputed from the actual content string via Blob. Same reasoning extends to the technique stack: an imported template's techniques are filtered through isTechniqueId and sliced to MAX_TECHNIQUE_STACK, because Step 5.2 already found exactly this bug class once (a corrupted persisted array bypassing CANON's ≤4 cap) and an imported file is the same problem arriving through a different door.
- THE FOUR APP-DATA KINDS REQUIRE THIS APP'S ENVELOPE; CHAT HISTORY DOES NOT (Step 9.3): variables/context-snapshot/saved-prompts/template-settings are only accepted inside an `{app:"divergence-ai", kind, payload}` envelope, and a file whose `kind` doesn't match what the user asked to import is rejected as "wrong-kind" with a message naming where it DOES belong. Rejected the alternative of sniffing a bare JSON object's shape to guess its kind: a bare `{}` of variables is structurally indistinguishable from a bare `{}` of several other things, and guessing wrong writes the user's data into the wrong store field silently — the exact opposite of CANON's "never a black box." Chat history is the deliberate exception because CANON defines that path as reading an EXTERNALLY exported log, which by definition won't carry this app's envelope.
- IMPORT MODAL IS TWO-LEVEL BECAUSE EIGHT FLAT CHOICES BREAKS AN ADHD HARD RULE (Step 9.3): CANON's cognitive-load rule is "Never more than 5 to 7 simultaneous choices per view" — eight import paths as eight buttons violates it outright, and this is an audited rule (Phase 11). Split semantically into "bring in outside content" (file, URL, chat history = 3) and "restore saved data" (previous conversation, variables, context snapshot, saved prompts, template settings = 5), two choices at the top level and never more than five below it. The group heading stays on screen in the sub-view, satisfying the same rule's "never require remembering earlier context mid-flow" clause. Rejected showing all eight under two headings in one view: that still presents eight simultaneous choices, which is what the rule actually counts.
- IMPORT LIVES IN THE "More ▾" OVERFLOW, AND THAT WAS STEP 9.1's CALL TO DEFER (Step 9.3): Step 9.1's DECISIONS explicitly left Import unplaced ("Import is explicitly Step 9.3's own separate OPUS step (not a Quick Actions button at all ... no row placement implied)"), so this step made the placement decision it deferred. The V3 screenshot's QUICK ACTIONS row has exactly four named buttons plus one unlabeled overflow; CANON Feature 11 names six actions. Five are already placed, Import is the sixth, and the overflow is the only slot that doesn't require inventing a button the screenshot doesn't show. It sits above the "Close Session" grouping in that popover rather than inside it, since Import isn't a close-session outcome.
- ONE SHARED OCR CLIENT ACCESSOR INSTEAD OF TWO LAZY-INITS (Step 9.3): AttachContextControls (Step 7.2) kept a module-level `sharedOcrClient` whose comment promised the app "never spins up a second worker" — which was already true for a different reason (the WORKER singleton lives in ocr.ts's own `workerPromise`, so the client wrapper was never the thing guaranteeing it). Rather than copy that same lazy-init into ImportModal and leave two module-level singletons of the same thing for a later step to read as two conventions, added `getSharedOcrClient()` to ocr.ts and routed both consumers through it; `terminateOcrWorker()` now clears it too, so teardown stays correct. This touches a working Step 7.2 file, which I'd normally avoid — justified because the alternative was actively adding the duplication, not merely tolerating it.
- STATE DETECTION NOW SHOWS AN IN-FLIGHT INDICATOR ON SUBMIT (Step 11.2): the only 1-second-rule miss the performance pass found. Chose a quiet static `role="status"` "Reading your message…" line in the pill panel's own slot over any spinner/animation — CANON's sensory rule forbids excessive auto-animation and competing stimuli, and the pipeline's StageIndicator is already the primary "working" signal on the same submit, so a second animated element would be noise. Rejected keeping the previous pills visible-but-dimmed during the wait: Step 6.3 already established clearing the panel to null on submit, and re-deciding that is out of scope for an audit. A later step must not remove this indicator or re-gate the panel such that the in-flight window goes blank again.
- STEP 11.2 APPLIED ITS OWN FIX RATHER THAN DEFERRING TO 11.5 (Step 11.2): 11.5 applies 11.1's ADHD-AUDIT.md specifically; the timing rules are 11.2's own charter ("produce the table … then apply the fixes"), so the detection-indicator fix belongs here, not filed for 11.5.
- KEYBOARD AUDIT DID NOT ADD FOCUS-TRAPPING TO THE 7 NON-TRAPPED POPOVERS (Step 11.3): Attach, StateDetectionPanel, QuickToolsGrid tiles, SavedPromptsMenu, LoadTemplateMenu, QuickActionsRow's More, and VisibilityMenu all dismiss via Escape/outside-click but rely on natural DOM tab order rather than useFocusTrap, unlike TechniqueDropdown. CANON's rule is "100% usable by Tab, Enter, Escape" — all 7 already satisfy that (every control inside is a real, reachable button/input) — so retrofitting focus-trap onto an established, repeatedly-logged, working convention across 7 components was rejected as out of this step's narrow charter (audit + fix gaps, not redesign compliant patterns). A later step is free to add it for tighter UX, but it is not required by anything this build has settled.
- IMPORTMODAL'S URL VIEW NOW SUBMITS ON ENTER, MATCHING ATTACHCONTEXTCONTROLS (Step 11.3): the only real gap the keyboard audit found — wrapped the previously plain `<div>` URL row in `<form onSubmit>` (preventDefault + handleUrlFetch), Fetch button changed to `type="submit"`. The sibling "Back" button already had an explicit `type="button"` (confirmed before changing anything), so it does not accidentally submit the new form. A later step touching either URL-entry pattern should keep the two consistent — they were always meant to be the same interaction.
- DID NOT ADD ROVING-TABINDEX TO RATINGROW'S STAR `role="radio"` BUTTONS (Step 11.3): the 5 stars are real, individually-focusable `<button role="radio">`s (5 Tab stops) rather than the WAI-ARIA APG's single-tab-stop-plus-arrow-keys radiogroup pattern DownloadModal's native `<input type="radio">` format picker gets for free. Still fully Tab/Enter/Escape operable (CANON's actual rule), so this is a stricter-ARIA-conformance nicety, not a gap — left unchanged rather than risking a behavior change to a working, tested control for a rule this step doesn't require.
- 11.4 RAN AUDIT-ONLY, DEFERRING ITS OWN "APPLY THE FIX" CLAUSE TO 11.5 (Steps 11.1+11.4): the operator's instruction for this run was explicit ("Audit only — do not add test cases, refactor, or validate beyond the spec... what needs 11.5"), overriding 11.4's step text which says each deviation is "actually fixed, not just logged." All 16 visual findings are in VISUAL-AUDIT.md with fixes specified to the file:line; 11.5 now applies BOTH reports, not just its step text's named ADHD-AUDIT.md. If a later reader wonders why 11.4's checkbox is ticked with zero src/ changes — this is why.
- REAL-BROWSER EVIDENCE PIPELINE ESTABLISHED, AND ITS TWO TRAPS DOCUMENTED (Steps 11.1+11.4): this machine (unlike every prior sandbox session) has Chrome/Edge, so the audits ran against the actually-rendered app. Trap 1: `chrome --headless --screenshot` and `--virtual-time-budget` capture a BLANK page because main.tsx awaits IndexedDB hydration before mounting — real-time waits over CDP are required (working scripts preserved in the session scratchpad; trivially rewritable — launch chrome with --remote-debugging-port, Page.navigate, sleep ~6-8s real time, Page.captureScreenshot). Trap 2: `--disable-gpu` disables backdrop-filter, so every glass surface renders UNBLURRED — do not misread bleed-through in such captures as a live-browser bug (it did surface the real V9 robustness finding, but the blurred rendering itself is unverified headlessly). Steps 11.5/12.2/12.3 should reuse this pipeline rather than rediscovering it.
- THE 5-7 CHOICES RULE READ AS TASK-FLOW DECISION POINTS, NOT ALL VISIBLE CONTROLS — RECORDED FOR RATIFICATION, NOT SILENTLY DECIDED (Step 11.1): CANON's own LAYOUT mandates 12 always-visible left-nav controls and Feature 12 mandates 7 visibility checkboxes + reset (8), both of which flunk a strict per-region count of CANON's own 5-7 rule. The audit reads the rule as governing simultaneous DECISION points in a task flow (a persistent nav list is scan-not-choose wayfinding), under which both mandated surfaces pass and the StateDetectionPanel's 10-11 interactive elements still fails (C1). 11.5 should ratify or reject this interpretation explicitly in its DECISIONS — Phase 12's audits must not re-litigate it from scratch.
- STATE PILL COLORS COULD NOT BE LIVE-COMPARED AGAINST V3 (Step 11.4): pills render only after a real model classification and this preview has no network — pill CSS is token-only and tokens were eyeballed at Step 1.2, so the comparison rides with 12.3's deploy-time pass. Everything else in VISUAL-AUDIT.md was compared against actual rendered pixels.
- RATIFIED: THE 5-7 CHOICES RULE GOVERNS TASK-FLOW DECISION POINTS, NOT EVERY VISIBLE CONTROL (Step 11.5, ratifying the interpretation Step 11.1 recorded and explicitly asked 11.5 to rule on — see the entry directly above). Adopting it as read: a persistent nav list or a settings menu of independent toggles is scan-not-choose wayfinding, not a simultaneous decision the rule is protecting against — F1 (left nav, 12 items) and F2 (VisibilityMenu, 8 controls) both PASS under this reading and needed no code change. C1 (StateDetectionPanel, 10-11 interactive elements when four pills are open) still FAILS even under this reading, since correcting/dismissing a specific pill is a genuine per-item decision point, not passive wayfinding — which is exactly why C1 stayed escalated rather than being waved through by the same interpretation. Phase 12's audits should treat this ratification as settled, not re-litigate it.
- CHOSE A NEW `--confidence-high` TOKEN OVER REUSING `--state-interest` FOR ADHD-AUDIT S3 (Step 11.5): the audit offered both as valid fixes ("point at --state-interest... or a new --confidence-high token"). Reusing --state-interest would recreate the exact cross-semantic-reuse problem this audit category exists to eliminate (a state-pill-only token gaining a second, unrelated meaning) — the dedicated-token option was the only one that actually resolves the underlying finding, and it keeps S2/S3 internally consistent (both confidence-badge colors now get their own token, matching neither a state pill nor --status-online).
- CHOSE "HIDE THE REMOVE CONTROL" OVER "ADD A RESTORE ROW" FOR ADHD-AUDIT D1 (Step 11.5): the audit offered both. Hiding the remove control for `DEFAULT_TEMPLATES`' own ids (LoadTemplateMenu.tsx, referencing the array directly, not a naming-convention guess) is a complete, mechanical fix requiring no new UI. A "Restore default templates" row would require inventing its placement, wording, and behavior — none of which the audit specified — so building it here would have been guessing at a UI beyond what was documented, not applying a stated fix.
- USED A GLOBAL PLAYWRIGHT INSTALL FOR VERIFICATION, NOT ADDED AS A PROJECT DEPENDENCY (Step 11.5): this environment has `playwright` installed globally (/opt/node22/lib/node_modules/playwright) driving the pre-installed Chromium at /opt/pw-browsers — used ad-hoc (a scratchpad script, same posture as the 11.1+11.4 session's own scripts) to screenshot the actually-changed surfaces (Quick Tools grid, Quick Actions header, accordion collapse state) against a `vite preview` build and confirm the fixes render as intended. package.json was NOT touched — Step 11.3's own DECISIONS already established that adopting Playwright as project tooling is Step 12.2's call, not to be preempted; this session's use is verification-only and leaves no trace in the repo's dependency graph.
- @PLAYWRIGHT/TEST IS NOW A REAL DEVDEPENDENCY (Step 12.2): this is the step Step 11.3's and 11.5's DECISIONS both named as owning that call — landed here, not preempted earlier. Config points executablePath at the environment's pre-installed Chromium rather than running `playwright install` (forbidden per this environment's own instructions).
- E2E MOCKS INTERCEPT AT THE NETWORK BOUNDARY, KEYED BY SYSTEM PROMPT (Step 12.2): rather than skip the flows that need a model reply (as every prior audit/verification session did, always logging "no network" and moving on), e2e/mocks.ts stubs `/api/proxy` and `/api/proxy-openai` with page.route(), returning exactly what the real serverless functions would return to the browser. Requests are routed to the right canned reply by comparing the request's `system` field against the ACTUAL prompt constants imported from src/services/*/prompt.ts (not re-typed strings) — so a future prompt change can't silently desync the mock, and the only structurally necessary disambiguator (stream:true, which only the final answer's execution call ever sets) is used instead of trying to distinguish same-model, same-endpoint calls (translation vs. debate's Claude side both use claude-sonnet-5; consensus vs. synthesis both use claude-opus-4-8) any other way. This is real client-code coverage — composer, pipeline orchestrator, streaming parser, detection panel, debate/consensus/synthesis clients, and IndexedDB persistence are all genuinely exercised; only model inference itself is stubbed. A later session adding real deploy-time E2E smoke tests (hitting the actual deployed proxy) should treat that as ADDITIONAL coverage, not a replacement for these — these stay fast/deterministic/free and keep working with no API keys.
- MOCKED CALLS CARRY AN ARTIFICIAL 250ms DELAY, ON PURPOSE (Step 12.2): a zero-latency mock resolves the entire five-stage pipeline (translate -> route -> compose -> stream) within a single tick, so transient UI (StageIndicator, TranslationCard, partial streaming text) never gets a chance to render before CenterColumn.handleDone clears it (setRun(null)) — real network latency is what makes those states observable in production, and a suite that can't observe them isn't actually testing them. Found this the hard way: the first suite run had the translation-card assertion fail as a race, not a real bug — fixed by adding latency, not by weakening the assertion.
- MANUAL PARTNER SELECTION, NOT AUTO-SELECT, FOR THE E2E DEBATE TEST (Step 12.2): autoSelectPartners' scoring heuristic (autoSelect.ts) is a deterministic function of question text, but pinning an E2E test's partner choice to that heuristic's exact output would make the test fail if the heuristic's scoring ever legitimately changes for reasons unrelated to debate/consensus/synthesis correctness. Manual selection (checking GPT-5.5 explicitly) tests the identical downstream code path (runDebate takes the same partnerIds shape either way) while staying decoupled from a heuristic this step didn't touch.
- FOUND, NOT FIXED: `npm run dev` DOES NOT LOAD THE APP (Step 12.2): src/services/routing.js is a UMD module (`module.exports = factory()` inside `typeof module === "object"` — the Step 1.5/3.1 vendor-supplied scorer, "do not rebuild," per ROUTING.md). Vite's dev server serves src/ files as native ESM with no CommonJS interop; that interop only happens during `vite build` (via Rollup) and only for files Vite treats as a pre-bundled dependency — routing.js is app source, so it never gets it, and `routingService.ts`'s `import engine, {...} from "./routing.js"` fails at runtime under dev with a real browser SyntaxError. This has been invisible for the entire build because every prior browser-driven session (11.1+11.4's audit, 11.5's own verification, and this session, until this finding) used `vite preview` (a production build), never `vite dev`. Not fixed here — routing.js/routingService.ts are src/ files, and this step's boundary is "build tests, don't refactor." Worked around for THIS suite's own purposes by pointing playwright.config.ts's webServer at `vite build && vite preview` instead of `vite dev`. CONVENTIONS.md's Dev commands section now carries this caveat so it isn't rediscovered blind.
- STEP 12.1 SCOPED TO ITS TWO ACTUAL GAPS, NOT A FULL REWRITE (Step 12.1): before writing anything, audited all 56 existing test files against PIPELINE.md's five stages — translation/routing/technique-selection/composition/execution all already had thorough dedicated coverage from the steps that built each one. Writing NEW tests for already-well-tested stages would have been pure duplication with no coverage gain, contradicting CONVENTIONS' anti-scope-creep posture. The two real gaps (store actions, CI config) are exactly and only what got built.
- STORE TESTS RESET VIA `.setState(createInitial*State())` IN `beforeEach`, MATCHING PERSISTENCE.TEST.TS'S OWN ESTABLISHED PATTERN (Step 12.1): sessionStore/accountStore are Zustand singletons created once at module load, so tests must explicitly reset between cases rather than relying on fresh instances. Followed the exact convention persistence.test.ts's `coldStartStores()` already established rather than inventing a second pattern (e.g. a store-factory-per-test) for the same problem.
- CI IS TWO JOBS, `unit` AND `e2e`, NOT ONE (Step 12.1): `unit` (build+lint+vitest) runs in seconds and needs no browser; `e2e` needs `playwright install --with-deps chromium` (slower, and the one most likely to have real-runner-specific flakiness). Splitting them means a `unit` failure reports fast and a slow/flaky `e2e` job never blocks feedback on the fast one. Both trigger on every push and PR (no branch filter) — this build's own convention restricts development to the `build` branch, but a CI gate that only watched one branch name by configuration would silently stop protecting the moment that changed, which is a worse failure mode than running CI slightly more often than strictly needed.
- MADE `executablePath` CONDITIONAL ON THE SANDBOX PATH ACTUALLY EXISTING, NOT HARDCODED (Step 12.1, fixing a latent bug from Step 12.2): the original playwright.config.ts (Step 12.2) hardcoded `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194/chrome-linux/chrome` — correct for this sandbox, but that path doesn't exist on a real GitHub Actions runner, so CI's `e2e` job would have failed outright the first time anyone ran it, undetected until then since this sandbox can't actually execute a GitHub Actions workflow. Now `existsSync()`-gated: uses the sandbox path when present, leaves `executablePath` undefined otherwise so Playwright resolves whatever `playwright install` (now a real CI step) provides. Re-verified 4/4 E2E tests still pass locally in this sandbox after the change, confirming the fallback logic didn't silently break the path that DOES exist here.
- V14's DARKENING OPACITY AND V8's DARKENING/BORDER VALUES ARE CALIBRATED, NOT SPEC'D (operator-directed follow-up): MATERIALS.md gives the base hex ranges and words like "barely noticeable"/"not neon" but no numeric overlay opacity for either — the operator's own instructions explicitly authorized deriving these by comparing real renders against Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png rather than stopping to ask, so that's what happened: build+preview the app, screenshot it, crop the same region from the V3 PNG (loaded via file://, same browser), compare, adjust, repeat. Landed on `--slab-darken-opacity: 0.75` (0.55 was tried first and was still visibly busier than V3) and `--button-darken-opacity: 0.35`. A later session with a real designer's exact target values should treat these as a well-calibrated starting point, not an untouchable final answer — the calibration was eyeball-matched against one reference image at a few crop regions, not measured pixel-for-pixel.
- V8's BORDER USES A NEW `--accent-cyan-tint-25` TOKEN, NOT A RAW rgb() LITERAL, AND NOT WHITE (operator-directed follow-up): first attempt used a white hairline (`rgb(255 255 255 / 0.14)`) because that's what the V3 crop visually looked like in isolation — caught before finalizing that MATERIALS.md's own "SPACING, CORNERS, SHADOWS" section explicitly restricts hairline borders to "subtle darker charcoal or cyan," not white. Switched to cyan, and added the token following V10's (Step 11.5) exact naming convention (`--accent-cyan-tint-06`/`-12` already existed) rather than hardcoding a new literal — same drift-risk reasoning V10 already established.
- MATERIALS.md WAS NOT UPDATED (operator-directed follow-up, operator asked to update it "if the marble spec changed"): it didn't. The existing prose — "near-black," "polished, subtle veining, barely noticeable," "not neon," "marble texture visible at close inspection" — already correctly described the TARGET both fixes now actually hit. The bug in both cases was the CSS not matching an unchanged, already-correct spec, not the spec being wrong or incomplete. Confirmed this reading is right, not just convenient, by using the unchanged spec language itself as the tie-breaker when choosing the soft-light blend approach for V8 (the spec's exact phrase "texture visible at close inspection" is what argues for texture-as-detail-on-a-base rather than texture-as-luminance-source).
- BLACK MARBLE'S #0B0C0E/#151618 RANGE WAS EYEBALLED, NOT ACTUALLY SAMPLED FROM THE TEXTURE FILE, DESPITE MATERIALS.md'S OWN CLAIM (operator-directed follow-up, operator asked to extract real hex values from the marble asset): installed Pillow and ran real per-pixel analysis of `public/textures/black-marble-slab.jpg` (2508x2508, the actual locked asset — the operator's own pasted reference images had no filesystem path this session could sample, so the in-repo locked asset was used instead as the traceable, verifiable source, same visual style: black stone, thin pale-gold veining). Found the existing #0B0C0E-#151618 range doesn't match the file it claimed to be "sampled from" — real trimmed mean excluding vein-highlight pixels is #050505, with a #020202 (20th pct) to #060702 (60th pct) spread. Updated `--surface-black-marble-start`/`-end` (tokens.css) and MATERIALS.md's THE THREE SURFACES text to these measured values. Scope: this only changes the flat `background-color` fallback used if the texture image fails to load (marble.css `.marble-slab`) — the rendered slab is the actual texture image, unaffected; `--slab-darken-opacity` (V14, already calibrated against Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png) is untouched and remains the thing that controls the slab's actual rendered darkness. Verified: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [221 modules], clean oxlint (same one pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583 passing.
- MATERIALS.md's BLUE MARBLE / SMOKED GLASS REPLACED WITH A THREE-TONE GREY MARBLE SYSTEM (operator-directed override, explicit and repeated across four separate messages this session, each addressing the prior one's open objection — final version pointed to real, already-locked repo assets: `public/textures/grey-marble-1.png`/`grey-marble-slab-1.jpg` and `grey-marble-2.png`/`grey-marble-slab-2.jpg`, confirmed identical-texture pairs at different resolutions by real pixel comparison). This is a DELIBERATE EXCEPTION to CANON's "screenshot wins on color disagreement" rule — Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png still shows a blue button, and that is no longer the target for these two surfaces; do not silently revert toward it in a future visual audit. Real per-pixel sampling (10th/85th percentile of non-vein pixels): grey-marble-slab-1 (operator's "Light Gray Marble 01") measures LIGHTER (#2A2925-#302F2B) than grey-marble-slab-2 (operator's "Medium Gray Marble 02", #161513-#1E1D1A) — a naming/measurement conflict in the operator's own labels. Resolved by FUNCTION, not the literal filename label: the lighter texture (grey-marble-slab-1) became Medium Gray Marble for the interactive tier (buttons/nav/borders — `--surface-blue-marble-start/-end`, tokens.css), the darker (grey-marble-slab-2) became Dark Grey Marble for the content tier (cards/panels/dialogs/messages — `--surface-smoked-glass`, tokens.css), producing a coherent background<content<interactive lightness ladder. Flagged explicitly to the operator to correct in one message if this guess is backwards. Architecture UNCHANGED: Medium Gray Marble still samples the SAME continuous black-marble-slab.jpg at the same slab coordinates (MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's "one continuous slab" rule) — only the gradient's base color token changed, not the texture source, so introducing a second texture image into the button was deliberately avoided (would have broken slab continuity, since the background stays Black Marble, not grey). Dark Grey Marble keeps the exact same translucent-glass-over-the-slab mechanic as Smoked Glass had (88-92% opaque, backdrop-blur, never its own texture) — only the tint changed. MATERIALS.md's THE THREE SURFACES and COLOR sections rewritten in place; note line "Three texture files are provided: one black marble (background), two greys (for the layered mid and light surfaces where the design calls for them)" already existed in MATERIALS.md before this session — this override activates assets the spec always provisioned, not an invented addition. CSS/component NAMES intentionally NOT renamed (`.surface-blue-marble`, `.surface-smoked-glass`, `BlueMarbleButton`, `GlassButton/Card/Panel` all kept) — only token values changed in tokens.css, which ~20 consuming files already reference via `var()`, so the recolor is systemic with a small diff; comments updated throughout to flag the name/color mismatch so a future reader isn't misled. Verified with a real `vite preview` + headless-Chrome capture at 1525px: background near-black, all buttons/nav/composer/quick-actions render one consistent warm grey (no blue anywhere), state-pill and Quick-Tools-tile colors (purple/red/green/orange, a separate "earned color" system per MATERIALS.md, out of scope for this ask) untouched, cyan text/border accents untouched. Verified: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [221 modules], clean oxlint (same pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583.
- NAMING DEBT FROM THE GREY OVERRIDE (PARKED, see PARKED section below): `.surface-blue-marble`/`.surface-smoked-glass` classes and `BlueMarbleButton`/`GlassButton`/`GlassCard`/`GlassPanel` component names no longer describe their actual materials. Not renamed this session — ~20 files reference the class names and would need a coordinated rename; deferred as a distinct, lower-risk follow-up rather than bundled into the same diff as the color change.
- GREY TEXTURE FILES RENAMED TO MATCH MEASURED DARKNESS, OPERATOR CONFIRMED THE FUNCTION-OVER-LABEL MAPPING (this session): the prior entry above flagged a conflict between the operator's own file labels ("Light Gray 01" / "Medium Gray 02") and real measured darkness (grey-marble-slab-1 lighter, grey-marble-slab-2 darker) and resolved it by function, asking the operator to correct it if backwards. The operator's next message confirmed the resolution was right and asked for the files themselves to be renamed to match: `git mv` (history preserved) — `grey-marble-1.png`→`medium-gray-marble.png`, `grey-marble-slab-1.jpg`→`medium-gray-marble-slab.jpg`, `grey-marble-2.png`→`dark-grey-marble.png`, `grey-marble-slab-2.jpg`→`dark-grey-marble-slab.jpg`. Re-ran the real per-pixel sampling against the renamed files to confirm the rename didn't change content (identical results: medium #2A2925-#302F2B, dark #161513-#1E1D1A — same bytes, new names, as expected). Updated every reference to the old filenames across the repo: tokens.css comments, MATERIALS.md (THE THREE SURFACES), MARBLE-CONTRACT.md (THE ASSETS — both the rendered-slab and canonical-source asset lists, plus the "Blue Marble buttons" mention), FILE-MANIFEST-STEP1.3-ADDENDUM.md (added "Saved as (current)" columns to both filename tables rather than rewriting the historical Step 1.3 record — that addendum documents what was true AT Step 1.3, including the original "light exposure"/"mid exposure" labels from that discovery, which independently corroborate this session's measured lightness ordering almost exactly: #2D2C28 vs. this session's #2C2B27, #1A1917 vs. #191816). No CSS `url()` reference needed updating — the grey files were never texture-loaded by any component (see MARBLE-CONTRACT.md THE ASSETS), only used as a color-sampling source, so this was a metadata/documentation-consistency pass, not a functional change. Verified: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [221 modules], clean oxlint (same pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583 passing.
- MARBLE TEXTURE FILES NOW ACTUALLY RENDERED AS BACKGROUND TEXTURE, NOT JUST A COLOR-SAMPLING SOURCE — REVERSES MARBLE-CONTRACT.md'S "ONE SOLE EXCEPTION" RULE (operator-directed override, this session; the operator's own words: "apply the marble texture files themselves throughout the site as background images/patterns, not just sample their colors"). This is a real architectural change, not a color tweak — MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's THE RULE explicitly named "no component carries its own marble, Medium Gray Marble buttons are the sole exception" as binding, with historical precedent ("the one fourteen branches broke") for why per-component marble is a known failure mode (visible seams, stretched/distorted veins, mismatched patches). Did not silently override without flagging: stated the tension to the operator's prior instruction implicitly by updating MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's text in place (its own header says "binding for every step" — leaving it stale after contradicting it would mean a future step re-reads a rule this session already broke and either enforces a rule that no longer holds or gets confused about which is current) rather than just changing CSS and leaving the contract wrong. TWO CHANGES, both in marble.css (`.surface-blue-marble`, `.surface-smoked-glass`), both flow through the same ~20 consuming files automatically since both are shared classes: (1) Medium Gray Marble buttons now sample `medium-gray-marble-slab.jpg` directly instead of `black-marble-slab.jpg` tinted grey — same `background-attachment: fixed`, fixed-world-scale mechanism as before, just a different source file, so it no longer literally reads as "the same continuous slab as the background" (a genuine, named departure from the original exception's framing) but keeps the identical non-stretching technique. (2) Dark Grey Marble content surfaces (cards/panels/dialogs/messages) now carry `dark-grey-marble-slab.jpg` as an actual `background-image`, `background-blend-mode: soft-light` over the translucent base color (`--surface-smoked-glass`), same fixed-world-scale tiling as the slab (`background-size: var(--slab-tile-size)`, `background-repeat: repeat`, no `background-attachment: fixed` since this tier isn't claiming viewport-coordinate continuity with the background, just its own consistently-scaled patch of stone) — this is the second, NEW exception to "no per-component marble," reversing that rule for this tier specifically. WHY THIS ISN'T THE FAILURE MODE THE RULE EXISTED TO PREVENT: the specific thing "fourteen branches broke" was texture stretching/scaling per component (cover/contain/percentage sizing warping veins differently on every differently-sized element) — both new exceptions reuse the identical proven non-stretching mechanism (native pixel scale, repeat, never sized to the element), so what changed is WHICH surfaces show texture, not HOW texture is safely rendered. LEGIBILITY WAS CHECKED, NOT ASSUMED: soft-light blend deliberately chosen over a stronger blend or raw-texture background specifically because Dark Grey Marble surfaces carry 14px body text throughout the app (messages, transparency details, accordion rows) — a harsher blend risked crushing contrast; no darkening scrim was added on top (unlike the button, which holds only a short label) so the effect stays subtle. VERIFIED WITH REAL RENDERED CAPTURES, NOT ASSUMED: `vite preview` + real headless-Chrome at both 1525px and 900px — confirmed visible veining on both the button and content surfaces (cropped and inspected the composer textarea, the right-rail accordion rows, and the TRANSLATE & ASK button directly), text stayed fully legible at both sizes, no seam or stretching artifact at either viewport width, vein scale identical between the two widths (MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's own resize check). MATERIALS.md and MARBLE-CONTRACT.md updated in place to reflect the new two-exception rule (THE RULE, THE ASSETS, THE MECHANISM, ENFORCEABLE CHECKS all revised — check 3, "zero vein content inside Smoked Glass," explicitly reversed since that's no longer true by design). Verified: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [221 modules], clean oxlint (same pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583 passing.
- VEIN CONTRAST INCREASED ON ALL THREE TEXTURES; A REAL FOUND-AND-FIXED BUG ALONG THE WAY, NOT JUST A CALIBRATION TWEAK (operator-directed override, this session — "the veining should be more defined and visible... not faint"). Slab: `--slab-darken-opacity` 0.75→0.35 (tokens.css) — simple alpha reduction on the flat black scrim, no blend-mode involved, worked as expected on first try. Medium Gray Marble button: `--button-darken-opacity` 0.35→0.15 plus its texture layer's blend mode `soft-light`→`overlay` (marble.css `.surface-blue-marble`) — also worked as expected, verified by a real crop of the TRANSLATE & ASK button. Dark Grey Marble content surfaces: FIRST ATTEMPT (soft-light→overlay, matching the button) WAS WRONG and caught with a real rendered capture, not assumed correct — overlay's blend formula behaves like multiply on a base this dark (`--surface-smoked-glass` is a near-black rgba), so it darkened the non-vein majority of the surface even further instead of brightening it; measured the actual rendered pixel inside the panel at (8,7,6) vs (1,1,1) on the bare slab beside it — nearly indistinguishable, the opposite of the ask. Root cause: overlay's effect depends on base luminance (screen-like on a light base, multiply-like on a dark one) — the button's base is light, this surface's base is dark, so the same blend-mode choice that worked for one broke the other. FIX: switched Dark Grey Marble specifically to `screen` (never darkens the base, only brightens toward the blend layer, so bright vein pixels genuinely pop without crushing the non-vein majority). Re-verified with real crops of the right-rail accordion rows: dramatic, clearly-visible veining, text still fully legible. Checked resize behavior at 1525px and 900px for both textures — no seam, no stretching, vein scale consistent (MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's own check).
- CONVERSATION AREA WAS RENDERING BUT COMPLETELY INVISIBLE WHEN EMPTY — TWO REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED, NOT A SPEC GAP (operator asked "is the component rendering at all? is the layout CSS hiding or collapsing that panel?" — investigated rather than guessed). Root cause 1, streaming.css: `.conversation-area` had existed since Step 5.1 with zero surface treatment — no background, no border, no card look at all, and no forced height, so with zero messages it collapsed to just its own padding and the entire rest of the center column showed bare marble slab with no visual indication a panel existed there. This was a genuine gap dating back to before the marble/glass system was fully wired up, not something this session's texture changes caused (confirmed: marble.css's own `.surface-smoked-glass` class was never applied to this element). FIX: added the `surface-smoked-glass` class in ConversationArea.tsx (same Dark Grey Marble panel treatment, MATERIALS.md's own "content areas — messages" category), made `.col-center` a flex column (layout.css) and `.conversation-area` `flex: 1 1 auto` with a 240px min-height (streaming.css) so the panel always occupies real, visible space regardless of content, and added a `conversation-area__empty` placeholder ("Nothing here yet — ask a question to get started.") following the exact `__empty` class-naming and neutral-copy convention already established elsewhere (QuickToolsGrid, TransparencyCard, ContextSnapshotContent). Root cause 2, a real logic bug caught by DIRECT DOM INSPECTION, not assumed fixed after root cause 1: the first attempt at the empty-state check (`children == null`) never actually triggered, because CenterColumn always passes `{gated && <TranslationCard/>}, {display && <StreamingAnswer/>}` as children — when both are falsy this is `[false, false]`, an array, never `null`. Verified via `page.evaluate()` that `.conversation-area`'s `innerHTML` was empty string even though the background/border WERE correctly applied (backgroundColor/backgroundImage/border all confirmed present via computed style) — isolated the empty-text bug from the panel-visibility bug instead of assuming one fix covered both. FIX: `Children.toArray(children).length === 0` (React's own array normalizer, which drops non-renderable boolean/null children) — verified empirically afterward (not just assumed correct) that the empty-state text actually renders. Full E2E suite (4/4, including the core flow that exercises real conversation messages) re-run after this change as a genuine regression check on rendering behavior, not just CSS. Verified: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [221 modules], clean oxlint (same pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583, playwright e2e 4/4.
- THE ICON-LIBRARY DECISION, DEFERRED SINCE STEP 1.5, IS MADE — `lucide-react` (CONVENTIONS.md rule 3: "adding a dependency is a DECISIONS line with a one-line justification"). Operator's message: "the icons are in v3" — clarified that V3 itself (already the locked structural reference) IS the icon reference, not a separate file; reviewed V3 closely (cropped left nav, top bar, composer, TRANSLATE & ASK, Quick Actions) before choosing anything. REJECTED hand-rolled SVGs (Logo.tsx's own approach) as the general-purpose answer: ~20 distinct icons needed across nav/top-bar/composer/buttons, and hand-drawing that many with consistent stroke weight/style is slower and more error-prone than it's worth when a well-established, permissively-licensed, tree-shakeable library (ISC license) exists and its default style already matches V3's simple-outline aesthetic almost exactly. Confirmed npm registry access works and the real bundle-size cost before committing to it: +~3KB gzip in the actual `vite build` output (352.44kB→~355-360kB range across the icon-adding commits), not assumed negligible. COLOR RULE, derived from V3 itself, not invented: V3 is NOT uniform — left-nav and top-bar icons are plain white/light outline, Quick Tools tile icons are each a distinct color (already built, Step 11.5 V2), state pills are colored with meaning. Followed that exact split — monochrome for navigation/chrome, color reserved for what's already category- or state-coded — consistent with MATERIALS.md's "monochrome base, 85% of interface, color used sparingly with meaning."
  New: `src/components/layout/BrainMark.tsx` — the Logo's aurora-gradient brain glyph, extracted into a standalone reusable component (own `useId()`-scoped SVG gradient id per instance, since this renders many times per page — one per assistant message — and reusing Logo.tsx's hardcoded `id="logo-aurora"` would only work for the first instance in the DOM, silently breaking every other one). Used for: Translate nav item, the composer's "WHAT'S ON YOUR MIND?" label, TRANSLATE & ASK, and the assistant message avatar (previously literal "AI" text, MessageBubble.tsx — found while doing this pass, same fix as V13) — the four places V3 actually reuses this exact icon, confirmed by close inspection, not four separate guesses.
  Icon choices favored CANON's written semantics over pixel-matching an ambiguous V3 glyph in two places: Projects uses `Folder` not V3's padlock-like glyph (CANON: "organizes conversations by project," nothing about locking/security); Customize uses `SlidersHorizontal` not a second gear matching V3's own icon there, specifically because CANON's LEFT NAVIGATION text calls out Customize as distinct from gear-icon Settings — reusing one glyph for both risked exactly the confusion that sentence exists to prevent.
  CSS: added `gap: 8px` to `.primitive-glass-button` (primitives.css, shared by every `GlassButton` consumer) and matching `display: inline-flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; gap: 8px` to `.surface-blue-marble` (marble.css — `BlueMarbleButton` doesn't share the glass-button class) so every icon+label pairing gets consistent spacing from one shared rule, not per-button margin hacks.
  Verified with real Playwright captures at 1525px and 900px (not assumed from the diff): every nav item, top-bar control, composer label, TRANSLATE & ASK, Attach/Context, TRANSPARENCY DETAILS/MULTI-AI ACTIONS, and Quick Actions button renders its real icon, correctly sized and spaced, no layout regression at either width. Full verification: clean tsc -b, clean vite build [1981 modules — lucide-react's own module count, tree-shaking confirmed by the small gzip delta above], clean oxlint (same one pre-existing unrelated warning), vitest 583/583, playwright e2e 4/4.
- QUICK TOOLS DEFAULT VISIBILITY FLIPPED TO ON — RESOLVES A CONTRADICTION CANON.md HAD WITHIN ITSELF (operator: "theres supposed to be 6 squares there like in the reference image... its supposed to be the part that stays up always"). CANON rule #1 ("Visual truth is the V3 screenshot... if a file disagrees with the image, the image wins") already outranked CANON rule #9 ("Quick Tools panel defaults to hidden (OFF)") — V3 shows the six-tile grid always visible above the accordion stack, never hidden. This was a real, pre-existing self-contradiction in the locked decisions list, not something introduced this session; the operator's direct instruction settles it explicitly rather than leaving it silently resolved one way by a future step. Changed: `accountStore.ts` DEFAULT_VISIBILITY.quickTools false→true (still user-togglable via the existing Settings checkbox — only the default changed); `accountStore.test.ts`'s "matches CANON Feature 12's seven documented defaults" test updated to match; CANON.md updated in three places (rule #9 struck through with the revision noted, not silently deleted; Feature 12's checkbox list; LAYOUT's column description) so a future session reading CANON doesn't re-encounter the old "hidden by default" text as if it were still current. The accordion mechanism itself (AccordionStack.tsx) needed NO change — it already implements a true revolving-door (one panel expanded at a time, confirmed by reading the existing code before touching anything) matching CANON Feature 12's own text; the operator's description of "accordian out 1-2 at a time rotating" was explaining the intended structure, not reporting a second bug.
  FOUND WHILE FIXING THIS, A REAL LATENT BUG NEVER PREVIOUSLY VISIBLE: with Quick Tools actually rendering now for the first time since the V1/V2 grid fixes, the third tile column (Prompt Library, Dashboard) overflowed col-right's 300px boundary by ~25px — confirmed with real `getBoundingClientRect()` measurements (tile right edge at ~1550px against a 1525px viewport, while the grid container itself correctly measured 299px). Root cause: CSS Grid items default to `min-width: auto`, which lets a tile's own content (e.g. "Prompt Library," two words) force it wider than its `1fr` track's fair-share width instead of wrapping/shrinking to fit — a real, pre-existing defect that simply never had an audience because `quickTools` defaulted to OFF for the entire build until now. Fixed with `min-width: 0` on `.quick-tools-tile` (quicktools.css), the standard fix for this exact class of grid-overflow bug. Verified with a real capture: all six tiles now render fully inside the 300px column, "Prompt Library" wraps cleanly to two lines.
- TRANSLATE & ASK's BORDER RENDERING INCONSISTENTLY WAS A REAL RENDERING BUG, ROOT-CAUSED WITH REAL MEASUREMENTS BEFORE TOUCHING CSS (operator: "the outline around its odd, its not consistently around it, some parts arent as obvious as the others"). Confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that `border-left`/`border-right`/etc. were all computed identically (`1px solid rgba(0, 217, 255, 0.25)`) — this was NOT a CSS logic error, so the fix couldn't be "correct the border declaration." Isolated with a controlled experiment instead: forced `background-attachment` to `scroll` on all four background layers via `page.evaluate()` on the live element and re-screenshotted just that element — border went from missing-on-the-left to uniform-on-all-sides. Root cause: `background-attachment: fixed` composites against the viewport's pixel grid, while this button's `border-radius` clip and border stroke are aligned to the element's own fractional-pixel box (confirmed: `getBoundingClientRect().x` was `1023.328125`, a non-integer) — the sub-pixel mismatch between the two dropped the left border edge in Chromium's rendering. Fixed by removing `background-attachment: fixed` from `.surface-blue-marble`'s texture layer (marble.css) — all four layers now `scroll`. The original justification for `fixed` here was already weakened by an earlier change this same session (the button switched from sampling the background's own `black-marble-slab.jpg` to its own `medium-gray-marble-slab.jpg`, so it was no longer claiming literal viewport-coordinate continuity with the background anyway) — what remained ("multiple buttons sample a consistent patch of the same texture as each other") was a minor nicety, not worth a real, user-visible border defect. MARBLE-CONTRACT.md's no-stretch guarantee is unaffected — `scroll` attachment still uses the identical fixed-world-scale `background-size`, only the coordinate anchor changed, not the tiling math. Verified with a real capture: border now uniform on all four sides and the rounded corners.
- LEFT-NAV BOTTOM GROUP (Trash/System Status/Logout) WASN'T READING AS BOTTOM-ANCHORED (operator: "its currently hovering w as much spacing under as above"). Measured before changing anything: `margin-top: auto` (the existing mechanism) was in fact already pushing the group toward the bottom correctly — 199px of flex space collected above it, only 16px below (container padding) — so it was never literally centered, but a real 16px gap below it, equal in KIND (if not amount) to padding elsewhere in the sidebar, was enough for the group to read as floating rather than pinned. Fixed by removing `.leftnav-content`'s bottom padding entirely (top/left/right unchanged) and giving `.leftnav-bottom` its own small `padding-bottom: 8px` directly — deliberately less than its `padding-top`, so the group's own content, not container padding, now defines the true bottom edge. Verified with a real capture: Trash/System Status/Logout now sit flush against the bottom of the column.
- LIGHT THEME REFERENCE IMAGE: CORRECTION TO THE ENTRY ABOVE THIS ONE, NOT A NEW FINDING. A message arrived stating Divergence_AI_Light_App_Screenshot_V2.png had landed in the repo root; `find` on this session's then-current local checkout came up empty and that was recorded as fact. Root cause of the false negative: the file was pushed to `origin/build` directly (not through this session's own working tree), and this session's local checkout was stale until the next `git fetch`/`rebase` — `find` was checking a genuinely outdated filesystem snapshot, not a wrong claim. Confirmed present after `git rebase origin/build`: 1.87MB PNG, real content, not a placeholder. Reviewed directly: a real, detailed light-theme mockup, matching Divergence_AI_App_Screenshot_V3.png's exact structure/layout — light marble background (white stone, grey veining) but NOT a simple color inversion: nav pills, top-bar buttons, TRANSLATE & ASK, Quick Tools tiles, and accordion rows all stay dark/near-black for contrast against the light background; only the slab itself and a few content surfaces (composer textarea, message area) go light. State pills shift from dark-background/bright-text to light pastel badges. Not yet built — flagged to the operator as a distinct, sizeable follow-on task (new light token set + a working theme-toggle mechanism, CANON Feature 12's Light/Dark/Auto), not started this session.

- STEP 9.3 BUILT THE IMPORT SIDE OF FOUR APP-DATA FORMATS WITH NO MATCHING EXPORTER — variables, context snapshot, saved prompts, and template settings can all be READ from an `{app:"divergence-ai", kind, payload}` envelope, but nothing in this app WRITES that envelope yet: Step 8.5's export is per-ANSWER (ExportData: answer text/confidence/rating/transparency/state pills), not per-app-data-collection. So today those four paths only accept a hand-authored file. This is the established "design the data shape before the producer exists" pattern (Step 8.4's DebateTranscript, Step 6.5's stateBus), and the envelope is deliberately trivial to emit — but a future step should add the matching export, or those four import options stay theoretical for a normal user. CANON never names an "export variables/prompts/templates" action, so this wasn't in scope to invent here.
- STEP 9.3's sessionStore.loadSessionRecord SEMANTICS ARE TYPECHECK-VERIFIED ONLY, NOT TESTED — it deliberately clears draftInput and statePills and deliberately leaves currentScreen alone, which is a real decision a later step could break without noticing. No store test file exists anywhere in this build and the convention against adding one is explicit and repeatedly logged (Steps 8.1/9.1/9.2: "small store-action bodies ... are typecheck+build verified only"), so this step followed that rather than unilaterally reversing it mid-build. Flagging because loadSessionRecord is arguably the first store action the convention wasn't written for — it sets eight fields with two deliberate omissions, not a one-line setter. If a future step wants the first `src/stores/*.test.ts`, this action is the natural first case.

- Step 1.8's raw 5-second setInterval firing is NOT directly unit-tested: vitest fake timers deadlock against fake-indexeddb's async scheduler, so the interval test was replaced with a pagehide-flush + stop()-cleanup test (real logic) plus direct saveNow()/loadPersistedState() tests. The interval itself is a one-line wrapper over the tested saveNow(). A future step could cover it with a real-timer integration test or a different IDB fake if desired.
- Step 1.8 restore-before-render blocks first paint on the IndexedDB read (milliseconds). No UI consumes the stores yet so it's invisible now; if a later step finds it costs perceptible startup time, switch main.tsx to render-then-hydrate with a "hydrating" flag rather than awaiting load before render.
- Autosave/restore is verified in a happy-dom + fake-indexeddb test environment, not a real browser. A future step with a real browser should confirm the actual 5s writes, real pagehide/visibilitychange flush, and true cross-reload restore behave as the tests assert (same no-headless-browser constraint as every prior step).

A future browser-equipped step should confirm the focus rings are actually drawn, focus trap/return survives real-browser focus quirks (esp. that release() returns focus to the trigger), Escape dismisses the topmost overlay, and shortcuts feel right — and specifically re-check focusTrap under a real renderer, since happy-dom's focus()/activeElement is an approximation.
- No component consumes the keyboard framework yet — there are no modals or global shortcuts in the UI (still layout shell + inert nav + placeholders). Feature steps plug in: modals via useFocusTrap + useDismissableLayer, send/command shortcuts via useGlobalShortcut, destructive controls (Close Session discard, Replace Answer) via useConfirmable. Step 11.3 (Keyboard audit) will expect these already wired, not retrofit them (CONVENTIONS.md rule 5).

A PDF is accepted, validated, sized, and trackable, but its content can never be read into a question as things stand. - STEP 7.2's real tesseract.js OCR (fetching its WASM engine + English language data from its default CDN, and actual recognition accuracy on a real image) is UNVERIFIED in this sandbox — no network access to reach the CDN at all, same standing residual as every model/proxy call since Step 1.10. All 6 ocr.test.ts tests mock createWorker/recognize entirely. The first deployed/browser-equipped session should upload a real image with visible text and confirm extracted content actually lands in session.context correctly.
- STEP 7.2 did not self-host tesseract.js's WASM/language-data assets — it uses the library's DEFAULT CDN paths. This is a real production consideration (offline reliability, a third-party CDN dependency, potential CSP conflicts) that a later deploy-focused step (12.3, or a dedicated polish step) may want to address by bundling/self-hosting those assets instead. Flagged, not built — no requirement to self-host was stated anywhere in CANON/PIPELINE/this step's own text.
- STEP 7.2 only ever requests English ("eng") OCR — CANON/PIPELINE.md say nothing about multi-language support, and tesseract.js supports many languages via additional language-data downloads (each one a further network fetch). If a future need for non-English OCR surfaces, extending createTesseractOcrClient to accept a language parameter is straightforward; not built preemptively.
- STEP 7.2's upload+OCR status indicator ("Reading files…") and the disabled Attach button during upload are typecheck+build verified only, not render-tested — same standing no-RTL/no-browser caveat as every prior UI step.
- STEP 7.3's real proxy fetch (api/fetch-url.ts actually reaching a live third-party URL from a deployed edge function) is UNVERIFIED in this sandbox — no network access at all, same standing residual as every proxy/model call since Step 1.10. All urlFetchHandler.test.ts tests stub fetchImpl entirely. The first deployed session should paste a real article URL and confirm extracted text actually lands in session.context correctly, including through a real redirect chain (redirect:"follow" is set but never exercised against a real server here).
- STEP 7.3's SSRF guard (isBlockedUrl) is a literal-hostname/IP check only — it does NOT prevent DNS rebinding (a public hostname that resolves to a private/internal IP at the moment the server-side fetch actually runs, after the check already passed on the literal string). Closing that fully needs platform-level network egress policy (e.g. a DNS-pinning fetch wrapper, or the hosting platform's own private-network fetch restrictions) which is a deploy-time (Step 12.3) concern, not something this handler alone can guarantee. Flagged plainly rather than overclaiming the guard's coverage.
- STEP 7.3's extractReadableText() is a lightweight heuristic (strip common boilerplate tags, prefer article/main, collapse whitespace) — it will do noticeably worse than a real Readability-style algorithm on pages with unusual markup (e.g. content wrapped in generic <div>s with no <article>/<main>, or sites that put real content inside <nav>/<aside> for layout reasons). CANON asks for "readable text," not perfect extraction; no readability library was added (CONVENTIONS rule 3 — not justified for what's asked). Revisit only if real usage shows the heuristic missing content often.
- STEP 7.3's popover UI (the Attach ▾ menu, the inline URL form, the Fetch/Back buttons, focus behavior when switching between menu/url views) is typecheck+build verified only, not render-tested — same standing no-RTL/no-browser caveat as every prior UI step. A future browser-equipped step should confirm Tab/Escape/outside-click all behave correctly across both popover views, and that autoFocus on the URL input actually lands focus there when the view switches.
- STEP 7.1's PARKED note ("session.context is NOT yet fed into the pipeline anywhere") STILL APPLIES — Step 7.5 (Context Snapshot) makes loaded context VISIBLE and REMOVABLE, but does not make the pipeline actually READ it into a translation/composition call; that's a genuinely separate piece of work, still unclaimed by any step read so far. Flagged again, now more precisely: "closes that loop" was the wrong framing at Step 7.3 — visibility and consumption are different gaps, only the first is closed.
- STEP 7.4's variable substitution reaches Composer.tsx's submit and CenterColumn.tsx's refine, the two places raw user text becomes part of a request — it does NOT reach anywhere inside session.context items themselves (an uploaded file's or fetched URL's CONTENT is never scanned for $tokens). CANON's own wording ("the app substitutes them into prompts") reads as the user's typed request, not loaded reference material, so this is believed correct, not an oversight — flagged in case a future step's reading differs.
