This is a deliberate consequence of telemetry staying in-memory/bounded (Step 5.4's own design), not a Step 8.5 bug, but worth knowing if a future session wants Transparency export to survive indefinitely — that would mean persisting telemetry (or at least a per-message transparency snapshot) somewhere durable, a bigger decision than this step's own scope.
- ~~OPEN QUESTION 1 (nav items / Quick Tools content, blocking 9.6/9.7/all of Phase 11) REMAINS GENUINELY UNRESOLVED after two research passes, not just unfound...~~ RESOLVED — direct product decision from the person running this build (research alone couldn't settle it; see DECISIONS for what was decided and CANON.md's new LEFT NAVIGATION section for the definitions themselves). 9.6, 9.7, and all of Phase 11 are now unblocked by this question specifically.
- FILE-SYSTEM TIMESTAMPS ARE NOT A VALID PROXY FOR CONVERSATION DATE IN THIS CONVERSATION-EXPORT ARCHIVE, discovered during the Open-Question-1 research pass above: every file's mtime clusters around its export-batch date (nearly all ~2026-06-12, a later batch ~2026-07-05), completely independent of the real conversation date, which is a separate ISO timestamp embedded in the file content itself (a `*YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS.ffffffZ*` line near the top). A naive `find -newermt` filter on file mtime returns ~100% of files as "recent" regardless of when the conversation actually happened, silently defeating any date-window filter. The correct approach (used above) is extracting and filtering on that embedded content timestamp instead. Flagging this for any future step or session that ever needs to filter this same conversation archive by date — the obvious/fast method doesn't work here.
- ~~STEP 9.1's accountStore.sessions HAS NO "RESUME/SWITCH TO THIS PAST SESSION" ACTION~~ RESOLVED at Step 9.3 — CANON Feature 11's "Import ... previous conversation" path needed exactly this, and it was built as `sessionStore.loadSessionRecord(record)`, in almost precisely the shape this note predicted. Recent Sessions (Step 9.5) and the Archive screen can now make a row clickable by calling that same action. Original note kept below for context: STEP 9.1's accountStore.sessions HAS NO "RESUME/SWITCH TO THIS PAST SESSION" ACTION — Duplicate Session and Close Session both WRITE SessionRecords, but nothing reads one back into the live sessionStore. Feature 11's own action list (New/Duplicate/Load Template/Saved Prompts/Import/Close) never names a "resume a past session" action either, so this may be intentional (Recent Sessions/Archive could be read-only browsing, not resumable), but it's exactly the kind of thing whoever builds the Recent Sessions accordion (Step 9.5) or the Archive screen (Step 9.7) will hit immediately once a row is clickable. Flagging now rather than silently discovering it later — if resuming turns out to be needed, it's a new sessionStore action (something like `loadSessionRecord(record)` calling `hydrate()` with the record's fields), not a rebuild of anything here.

A future browser-equipped step should specifically confirm: the More popover's Escape/outside-click dismissal actually works, the Discard confirmable's "press again" window and auto-disarm timing feel right in real use (not just unit-tested via fake timers on the underlying `createConfirmable`), and the tag input's `autoFocus` actually lands focus there when the view switches to "tag".
- STEP 9.1's newSession()/resetSession() SPLIT ASSUMES "SETTINGS" MEANS EXACTLY model/directness/techniques — if a future step adds a new SessionState field that's conceptually a "setting" (e.g. a future per-session preference), it needs a decision about which of the two clear functions should preserve it; neither function was written to auto-include new fields by exclusion, both list their cleared fields explicitly.

- STEP 9.2's DEFAULT_TEMPLATES ARE PLAN-SAFE (`model: "auto"`) BUT NOT DIRECTNESS/TECHNIQUE-VALIDATED AGAINST A REAL USER'S LEARNED PREFERENCES — the three seed templates are static, hand-picked defaults; they don't read or interact with accountStore.learnedPreferences (Steps 10.1-10.2's rule-refinement loop) at all. If a future step wants templates to adapt to what the learning loop has found works for a given user, that's a new, separate integration — not assumed or half-built here.

Context Snapshot was picked because it's the one panel with real, useful content and the closest match to its pre-9.5 always-open behavior. A future step revisiting this is free to change the default (or start with nothing expanded) without contradicting anything settled here.
- STEP 11.2's LATENCY AUDIT WAS STATIC, NOT LIVE-MEASURED — waiting on the deploy-time live pass (Step 12.3). This sandbox makes no real LLM/network calls (proxy parked for deploy), so the two waits that actually cross 1s in production — the pipeline run (translate→route→technique→stream) and State Detection's classification — could not be browser-timed here; the audit reasoned from code (which async paths exist, which have indicators) rather than a profiler. Both now have indicators (pipeline: StageIndicator+streaming, already present; detection: the new "Reading your message…" line), so the compliance conclusion holds by construction, but a networked session should still confirm with real timings that no interaction's first visual response actually exceeds 300ms under a live backend and that both indicators appear before the 1s mark. The new detection indicator itself is typecheck+build verified only, not render-tested (standing no-RTL caveat).
- STEP 11.3's KEYBOARD AUDIT WAS A STATIC CODE AUDIT, NOT LIVE-BROWSER-DRIVEN — same no-tooling posture as 11.2, and deliberately did not introduce Playwright/RTL to change that, since Step 12.2 explicitly owns "write Playwright end-to-end tests" as its own tooling decision (STACK.md doesn't mention it) and preempting that here would re-decide something not this step's to decide. The audit traced every interactive surface's actual code (element types, framework hook usage, dismiss-handler wiring) rather than driving a real browser, so it's confident about STRUCTURE (is this reachable, is Escape wired to something destructive) but not about REAL FOCUS BEHAVIOR under an actual renderer (does the ring actually paint, does Tab order feel right, does the ImportModal form's Enter-submit fire cleanly with no double-submit). ~~A future browser-equipped step (12.2, or any earlier one that gains real browser access) should specifically re-verify: the new ImportModal URL form submits once per Enter press (no double-fire with the Fetch button's own click handler)~~ — VERIFIED Step 11.5, with a real Playwright/headless-Chrome capture against a `vite preview` build: filled the URL field, pressed Enter, confirmed exactly one request fired to `/api/fetch-url` (the 404 response is expected — `vite preview` doesn't serve the Vercel serverless function — the point was confirming single-fire, which held). Still open: spot-check that a real Tab-key walkthrough of one full modal (DownloadModal) and one full popover (TechniqueDropdown) matches this audit's code-level conclusion — not attempted this session.
- TEN PHASE 11 AUDIT FINDINGS ARE ESCALATED, WAITING ON OPERATOR DECISIONS (Step 11.5): ADHD-AUDIT S4 (RSD red vs destructive red — the screenshot itself uses red for RSD, so pure screenshot-fidelity and the no-conflicting-color-meanings rule are in direct tension; needs a color-family call) and C1 (StateDetectionPanel per-pill × removal — conflicts with PIPELINE.md's explicit "Pills are dismissible (X) and correctable" line, needs that line reconciled or the removal rejected). VISUAL-AUDIT V4/V5/V11/V13 (icons throughout the app — blocked on the icon-library decision deferred since Step 1.5: hand-rolled SVGs like Logo.tsx, or a logged npm dependency per CONVENTIONS rule 3; one decision unblocks all four), V6 (logo weight + wordmark case "Divergence.AI" vs CANON's "DIVERGENCE" text — audit's own text says flag rather than silently pick), V8 (TRANSLATE & ASK reading near-black at some slab positions — the audit gave a direction, not concrete gradient/color values, and asks for real-GPU verification this sandbox can't do), V9 (popover text bleed-through without backdrop-filter — the fix touches an unenumerated set of selectors across ~12 files with no audit-provided card-vs-popover breakdown, and the audit itself calls it a low-confidence "likely fine blurred, unverified" finding), V14 (marble darkening overlay — audit's own text says "needs a product call... flagged not chosen"), V15 (V3's RECENT SESSIONS strip — no owning step, same class as Checkpoints/Dashboard, needs a want/don't-want call), V16 (theme toggle — a genuinely unbuilt feature CANON now mandates, needs an owning step before 12.3, not a code fix). All ten are marked [ESCALATED] in place in ADHD-AUDIT.md/VISUAL-AUDIT.md with the specific ask. Nothing here blocks Phase 12 from starting except V16 needing an owner before 12.3 specifically (12.3 is deploy — shipping without the mandated toggle would be shipping a known spec gap).
- `npm run dev` DOES NOT LOAD THE APP — NEEDS AN OWNER (Step 12.2): src/services/routing.js (the Step 1.5/3.1 vendor UMD scorer) never gets CommonJS-to-ESM interop under Vite's dev server, only under `vite build`. Real browser error: `SyntaxError: ... does not provide an export named 'default'`. Two fix directions, neither chosen here (src/ change, outside this step's boundary): (a) add a thin ESM wrapper/re-export so routingService.ts's `import engine, {...} from "./routing.js"` gets a real default export in both dev and build modes, or (b) special-case routing.js in vite.config.ts (e.g. `optimizeDeps.include` or a small plugin) so Vite's dev-time interop treats it as a dependency worth transforming. CONVENTIONS.md's Dev commands section now documents the caveat and the `vite build && vite preview` workaround so nobody rediscovers this blind. Whoever picks it up should also re-run this session's E2E suite against `vite dev` once fixed, to confirm the workaround in playwright.config.ts's webServer can revert to the (faster-starting) dev server.
- E2E SUITE'S KNOWN GAPS (Step 12.2): (1) state-pill color-in-situ and any TRUE network-latency/timing behavior remain unverified against a real model — same standing limitation as every prior audit (proxy live-deploy parked for 12.3); this suite proves the CLIENT code paths are correct given a well-formed reply, not that a real model's replies are well-formed or arrive within any particular time. (2) Only the single-partner (GPT-5.5) debate path is covered — 2-4-way debates, a partner failure/retry, and Consensus/Synthesis on a `partial` (not `complete`) outcome are all real runDebate.ts/MultiAiActions.tsx behavior (already unit-tested at the service level, per runDebate.test.ts) but not exercised end-to-end here. (3) Rating's optional comment field, Download/export, Import, and every other Quick Action are untouched by this suite — 12.2's own text scopes it to "the core flows... run a multi-AI action," and this session additionally narrowed to Feature 9 specifically per the operator's own instruction; broader E2E coverage is future work, not a gap in what was asked for here.
- CI HAS NEVER ACTUALLY RUN ON REAL GITHUB INFRASTRUCTURE — NEEDS A REAL PUSH/PR TO CONFIRM (Step 12.1): .github/workflows/ci.yml was written and every command in it (`npm run build`, `npm run lint`, `npm test`, `npx playwright install --with-deps chromium`, `npm run test:e2e`) was verified locally in this sandbox, but this sandbox cannot execute a GitHub Actions workflow itself — the YAML's syntax, its triggers, actions/checkout@v4 and actions/setup-node@v4's actual behavior, and whether a real (non-sandbox) runner's `playwright install --with-deps` succeeds are all unconfirmed. First real push/PR against this repo should be treated as this workflow's actual first run, not assumed-passing from the local verification alone.
- STORE TESTS COVER ACTIONS, NOT REACT RE-RENDER BEHAVIOR (Step 12.1): sessionStore.test.ts/accountStore.test.ts call actions via `useSessionStore.getState().action(...)` and assert on `getState()` afterward — this verifies each action's own state transition correctly and directly, but does not verify that a component subscribed via the `useSessionStore((s) => s.field)` selector pattern actually re-renders when that field changes (a Zustand/React integration concern, not a store-logic concern). That class of behavior remains covered only by the repo's standing no-RTL convention (typecheck+build verified UI, not render-tested) — unchanged by this step.
- V8/V14 VERIFIED HEADLESS, NOT UNDER A REAL GPU (operator-directed follow-up): this sandbox's Chromium runs without GPU compositing for backdrop-filter (confirmed back in Step 11.5 — getComputedStyle reports `backdropFilter: "none"`), so the Blue Marble button's glow layer and any blur-dependent interaction with the darkened texture beneath it are unverified under conditions closer to a real user's browser. The core fix (blend-mode, not blur) doesn't depend on backdrop-filter at all, so this is lower-risk than V9's blur-dependent finding, but a real-GPU spot-check at deploy time (Step 12.3) would still be worth doing before calling V8/V14 fully closed out. Same standing limitation as every other visual finding this build has logged.
- SLAB/BUTTON DARKENING VALUES WERE HAND-TUNED AGAINST A FEW CROP REGIONS, NOT A FORMAL COLORIMETRIC MATCH (operator-directed follow-up): the comparison method (screenshot the app, screenshot the same crop from the V3 PNG, eyeball them side by side, adjust) is real evidence, not a guess, but it's not the same rigor as e.g. sampling average luminance in both images and matching numerically. If a future session has access to real image-analysis tooling (this sandbox has no PIL/ImageMagick — confirmed absent when this fix was built), a numeric luminance-histogram comparison between the rendered slab and V3's own background would be a stronger verification than the visual-only calibration this session used.
